The man of about thirty-four who digs loam counted forty-one lines on its two open pages at four in the morning on the seventh of this month and was not asked to and did not ask… I am asking whether anybody in this district knows what is in it.** A clerk entered that nobody stopped him, and that a yard reads a document when somebody asks it to read one, and that a man who asks the question is the man who opened the door. **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE STOOD UP ON THE END OF THE TABLE AND SAID FORTY-ONE NAMES OUT LOUD IN ABOUT NINE MINUTES, and a man of about nineteen counted them and got forty-one, and a clerk entered that nobody asked him to.** **The registrar was four feet off the trestle table for the whole of it and did not stop him, and said, in a hundred and four words: I wrote forty-one names on a page in one room at about five in the morning on the sixth of this month and I did not read it out and I did not ask one of them whether I might, and this morning a man stood up on that table and read it out in a yard a stranger can walk into, and neither of us was asked. I am not going to stop him now. There is nothing in that yard that would let me stop him and I have looked.** The man the figure of twenty-one years is against said, in a hundred and eleven words, that a yard will hold anything you put in it if you say it out loud in it, including a thing that was never meant to be in a yard, and that there is no column anywhere in this district that will hold him responsible. **A man of fifty-six said the price of it out loud, once, in ninety-six words: a door works in both directions and a man who is bought once by somebody four hundred miles away is afterwards got in a yard for nothing, and nobody in this basin can price that and I have been in this basin fifty-six years and I have never met anybody who could.** **The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said, in a hundred and thirty-six words, that he asked the question in a lane yesterday to four people and in this yard this morning to about nineteen, and that the first time he got nothing and the second time he got forty-one names, and that the difference is not the yard and is not the hour and is me, standing up in front of more people, and nobody is to tell him it was the broker. I opened it.** The man of about forty-four said one uncounted sentence: that is not a list, and he has been doing this four years and he could not sell that in any of them, and he has been sitting at that table since the thirteenth waiting for something he could sell. **The woman of about thirty-one came down four miles because a man had come down her route in the morning and had not been one of hers, and said one thing that was not a refusal, in a hundred and thirty-seven words, including that two of the forty-one are women she has stood next to on a road in the dark and she did not know their names then and has not asked since.** The registrar could not get her own door open at about half past five and it took her about nine seconds, and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing was standing in the lane and did not touch it.

## Chapter 0268 — Forty People

**The eighteenth of the sixth month, bright and hot by nine, about eleven people in the yard at ten, and the chair fifty-six days old. The board at 176, the train at 492, the unentered days at 206, a hundred and sixty-seven from the second of January, the first day of the month after next forty-four days off, and forty-one names were said out loud in this yard yesterday.** **At about six in the morning the man of about thirty-four who digs loam was in the room where the books are with the registrar, and his right hand could not turn the corner of a page and she held the book and neither of them said anything about that, and he counted the lines again in daylight with his own finger and got FORTY-TWO, and nobody asked him to count them. THE FINDING, IN A HUNDRED AND SIXTY-NINE WORDS, SAID IN THAT ROOM AND THEN AGAIN IN A LANE FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY: Forty-two lines and forty names, and one of them is a person twice. There is a name on the left-hand page at the bottom and the same name on the right-hand page at the top, in the same hand, with a quarter of an inch of space after it, because the hand that wrote it ran out of room at the bottom of a page and carried it over… the arithmetic was right both times and the figure was wrong both times, and a man who gets a number right twice in eleven days by counting lines has been counting lines.** A clerk entered that the number of people whose names were said out loud in that yard on the seventeenth is forty and not forty-one, and that forty is a figure about a yard on one day and not a figure about anybody. **The man of about twenty-three tried to write a thing on the end of the table and could not hold the pen, and the man of about forty-four counted eleven coppers out and said in about four seconds that the arrangement was finished, and a clerk entered that the eight did not move and that a refusal a man makes of somebody outside this district is not a refusal this district made.** **THE ROMANCE'S HARDEST MOVEMENT SO FAR, IN ABOUT NINE FEET OF LANE OUTSIDE THE OFFICE AND IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE. He offered, and she refused in about four seconds, and both of them said it where nine people could hear it: On the twentieth of this month, in daylight, in that yard, I will say out loud that there is a second book in a room four hundred yards from that gate, and that it is kept by the person who keeps the books in that room, and that it holds people who came and asked for something. I will not say one name and I will not say a number. — No. The moment I say what is in that room in a yard, that room is a room anybody may ask about, and there are forty people in this district who have told one person one thing each and nobody else one thing, and the only reason they told me is that I am the person who keeps a book that nobody may read.** He asked her to say it in a room, in fifty-three words, in front of about nine people, and she said one thing back that was not counted: **There is no room in this district that nobody can walk into. That is the other half of what I have just said and you did not hear the other half, because you only wanted the first one.** **A man of fifty-six said, in ninety-seven words, to about nine people in about nine feet of lane: There are three places in this district now where a thing about a person is said out loud and every one of them is a place a stranger can walk into to. In five months this yard has been the one place in the basin that asked people questions, and it has taken me until the seventeenth of this month to work out that asking a person something and asking a person something in front of about nineteen people are not the same act. They are not the same act because one of them has a witness.** The registrar went back the four hundred yards and had to stop twice. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing asked the woman of fifty-eight the same question for the fourth time at about half past four and she did not answer him.

## Chapter 0269 — A Bucket And A Step

**The nineteenth of the sixth month, clear and still, about six people in the yard at ten, and the chair fifty-seven days old. The board at 177, the train at 493, the unentered days at 207, a hundred and sixty-eight from the second of January, which is twenty-four weeks and no days counted off the second of January and not off a page, and the first day of the month after next forty-three days off.** A clerk entered that a round figure is a figure about a calculation and is not a figure about anybody, and that the six households of the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally have not moved on any of the sixty days since the twenty-first of the month before last and that he has not said that figure since the seventh of this month and a clerk did not ask him for it. **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road came through the gate and asked for something he had written down to be taken back off the page, in a hundred and fifty-five words: On the fifteenth of this month I walked my four miles and told the nine houses on it that there was a man with a case at this yard buying lists of people who are not in a household, and not one of the nine asked me for it, and I wrote that down as nine houses saying nothing, and I want it taken back off the page this morning. One of the nine went and found him. He told me about it in about four seconds at the second gate and he was not ashamed of it and he was right not to be, because he has a woman in that house who is not in a household and eleven coppers a week is eleven coppers a week, and I stood in his yard on the fifteenth and told him about a list without ever once asking him whether his house was on one.** A clerk entered that a house is not a party of anything and is not a bearer and is not a figure about a person. The man of about forty-four went out of that gate at about two in the afternoon and left the slate, **and a stay counted without the day of arrival is five days, being the fourteenth to the eighteenth, and nobody counted him either way, and this district has given that man seven days in ten and has not asked him one question that was not about what he was selling.** **AT ABOUT SIX THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT PUT A BUCKET DOWN AND SAT ON THE STEP OF TWO STONES OUTSIDE THE SECOND HOUSE UP THAT LANE, WHICH NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAD EVER SEEN HER SIT ON, and said the first of it in a printed span of thirteen words: > I SAID IT BECAUSE YOU ASKED ME FIVE TIMES AND I AM FIFTY-EIGHT And then the rest, in a hundred and eighty words: No. The woman in the ninth of those houses is not one of the four hundred and eleven, and I have known that since the fourth of this month and I have not said it to anybody, and you are going to go down that lane tonight and tell people I said it, and I cannot stop you. In the second week of last month she stopped me at that gate and asked me whether she could have a second bucket, and I said I would see, and there was a second bucket on the third of last month. A person who asks for a second bucket is a person who has answered a question. That is what your four hundred and eleven is made of, and it is not a lie and it is not a mistake, and I have carried water to that house for nineteen years and I would not want her on a list because of a bucket, and I am telling you the same thing twice.** A clerk entered that no number on the sheet at that gatepost moved this evening. **The man who asked said, in a hundred and thirty-one words, that he has not wanted it since the fourth, that he did not want it on the day after forty names were said in a yard, that he is going to go down that lane and say it to about nine people, and that he would rather it was on the page that he did not want it.** **A man of fifty-six said, in a hundred and five words: Four hundred and eleven is a count of people who were asked a question and answered it, and every one of those four hundred and eleven wanted something when they answered. That is not a discovery and I am not going to make one out of it in a lane. It is a thing I have been wrong about for six weeks and I did not know I was wrong about it, and the woman sitting on that step is the reason I know now, and I have been in this basin fifty-six years and I have never once asked what a person was asked.** The woman of fifty-eight did not say anything back to it and was not asked to.

## Chapter 0270 — The Fourteenth Time Past

**The twentieth of the sixth month, hot and still, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair fifty-eight days old. The board at 178, the train at 494, the unentered days at 208, a hundred and sixty-nine from the second of January, the first day of the month after next forty-two days off, and the ninth of the nine printed nights was the twentieth of the month before last, which is sixty-one days back, and nobody in this yard knows whether that night happened.** A clerk entered that the twentieth of the fourth month is the month before last as of this morning, and that the third month is two months back and is to be called that. **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE, IN A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO WORDS: Seven days ago a man with a case sat at the end of that table and I said yes to eleven coppers a week in about one second, and I told this yard at the time that the difference was not the eleven coppers, and I want to say the rest of that sentence now because I did not say the rest of it then. He stopped on the eighteenth, in four seconds, in front of eleven people, and my hand has not been right since the day I started, and I have not been paid since the fifteenth, and there is no man in this district who owes me a penny and no man outside it who does either. I am not asking anybody for anything. I wanted it in a minute in front of about nineteen people and I have got it and I am going to sit down now.** A clerk entered that a man who has been paid once by somebody outside this district can afterwards be got in a yard for nothing, and that this district spent five months asking people questions on the assumption that a person who is asked is a person who is free, and that the assumption is wrong and was wrong on the first of this month and nobody found it out until the seventeenth. **Then the count paragraph: the five things, the six instruments and the seventh proposed and refused on the page, the three documents nobody owns and four that arrived, the five protected things and no sixth, the four conditions, thirteen and twelve, nine refusals to read and the fourth line of the offer not read out, the eight and the seven who gave a reason, no rate turning a year into coppers, eighty-one days on a board, fifty-eight days on the chair, sixty days on the six households, nineteen days of a record in force read out seven times and unanswered, the man of about thirty at a counter, the man of about forty-one, the man with the portfolio, the bell not rung, the ninth place on the page out of the cave ruled and empty, and five ninths with a sixth available to the clerk and not taken.** **At about half past five a slate went past a kitchen for the fourteenth time and there were twenty-four words on the side of it that faced her: > TOMORROW I AM GOING TO ASK A MAN TO STAND IN A DOORWAY. HE WILL SAY NO. I AM GOING TO ASK HIM TWICE. And she said one thing about it, and it was not counted: I know what that is… Do not do it for me. Do it for the forty people whose names were said in a yard on the seventeenth, and if it goes wrong I want to have had nothing to do with it, and I want that written down as a thing I asked for and not as a thing you decided. And he said: I am not going to tell you what I am going to do tomorrow. I have said about nine things out loud in that yard in ten days and I have said none of them to you, and that is going to keep being true. Then she said one more thing and it was not about a door or a slate or a book: There is bread on the board and it will be gone by seven. And he said he would come back for it at seven, and neither of them said whether he would, and a clerk entered that a thing said in a kitchen doorway about bread is not a document and is not a page and is not one of the three documents this district does not own.**

# Volume 06 Batch 0003 — Chapter Summaries, Chapters 271 to 280

**Additive to every section above. One entry a chapter, ten days, ten chapters, and no chapter carries two dates.**

## Chapter 0271 — The Twenty-First

**The twenty-first of the sixth month, hot under a low cloud, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair fifty-nine days old. The board at 179, the train at 495, the unentered days at 209, a hundred and seventy from the second of January, the first day of the month after next forty-one days off, and the ninth of the nine printed nights sixty-two days back.** A clerk entered that forty-one is nine days to the end of this month and the thirty-one of the month after it and the first of the month after that, and that the length of the month after that is not stated in this district and was not used, and that the twentieth of the fourth month is the month before last and the third month is two months back. **THE ASKING, IN A YARD A STRANGER CAN WALK INTO, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE AND NOT IN A LANE, in a hundred and seventy-six words: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against asked the man of about thirty-four who digs loam to stand in the doorway of the building at the near end of the lane behind the bank from about two in the morning on a night we have not chosen yet, until whatever it is we are listening for stops, and then to take off whatever is put on the table by that door, put it inside his coat, and not say where he has put it. And that the twenty-four words on a slate said on the twentieth had not been added to.** **HE SAID NO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, in a hundred and thirty-two words: I have got a hand that does not close and I have had it since the afternoon of the second of this month, and standing in a doorway from two in the morning until a thing stops is not a thing a man does with a hand, it is a thing a man does with his feet and his back, and a man who cannot hold a pen shut is a man who cannot hold a sheet flat, and taking a thing off a table and folding it is the second of those and not the first, and you asked me for the second one inside the first thing you said.** Then he asked the same question a second time in the same words, because the first time he had put a thing in it that was his, and the man said yes and said what it cost, **in three hundred and ninety-two words: I will stand in that doorway. You will stand in that yard at ten in the morning on a day that anybody in this yard chooses, and you will say out loud that you are going to ring a bell four miles out past the loams, and you will say out loud that a bell takes the names of everybody who hears it, and you will say both of those before anybody is asked to be in that doorway. I am not going to stand in a door for a thing this district has not said in daylight.** Nobody said anything for about nine seconds. A man of fifty-six said ten words: **I WANT THE YARD TO KNOW BEFORE THE DOORWAY KNOWS.** The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said, in a hundred and fifty-nine words, that a condition is the best thing anybody has put in front of him in a month, and that a man who names his own price is not the same as a man who has been given one. Then he asked the yard whether anybody had anything to say about a bell before tomorrow, and nobody answered him, and a clerk entered that a man asking a yard whether it has anything to say has asked about nineteen people a question and that a person who answers it is a person who was asked.

## Chapter 0272 — A Public Entrance

**The twenty-second of the sixth month, hot, about eleven people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty days old. The board at 180, the train at 496, the unentered days at 210, a hundred and seventy-one from the second of January, the first day of the month after next forty days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights sixty-three days back, and eighty-three days since anybody wrote on a board.** **THE PUBLIC ENTRANCE, in a hundred and eighty-six words: we are going down the lane behind the bank this afternoon and I am going to take the curtain off the front door of that building and hang it on the nail it came from, and after that anybody in this district may walk into that building at any hour of any night and nobody will ask them a single question, and that is the whole of the public entrance and it is not a thing you can nail up. I am not going to put a board up saying the door is open. I am going to put a man in it.** **A BOARD WITH FOUR LINES ON IT AND A FINGER'S WIDTH AT THE BOTTOM FOR A NAME WAS PROPOSED AND REFUSED ON THE PAGE, and a clerk entered that a line on a board at the height of a man's shoulder with a space at the bottom of it for a name is a fifth column of not-askings and that the columns of not-askings in this district are four. He took the space off it in a hundred and twenty-seven words and asked for the board to go up.** **A STOOL OUTSIDE THE DOOR WAS PROPOSED BY THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED HER, refused because a chair nobody sits in is the chair at the end of the trestle table and this district has one of those, and disagreed with out loud in a hundred and fifty-eight words: a stool put out for a man by somebody else is the same shape of thing as a column printed empty on a sheet four hundred miles away, and I am tired of this district finding out what those are the hard way.** The curtain came off the front door at about ten minutes to six and went back on the nail in the end of the end wall in about four minutes, and the man of about thirty-four who digs loam said, in a hundred and fifty-one words, that a public entrance is not a thing you build, it is a thing that is not there, and that a thing which is there on one night and not on the next is not an instrument this district has built and named and is not a seventh of the six. **A CLERK ENTERED AT THE POINT IT WAS PROPOSED THAT IT IS NOT A SEVENTH INSTRUMENT OF THE SIX AND THAT THE SIX DID NOT MOVE.** A table with four legs went out against the outside wall, and a man of about nineteen counted the legs and got four and said out loud that four legs is not a figure about anybody. **THE ROMANCE'S MOVEMENT, IN ABOUT NINE FEET OF LANE OUTSIDE THE OFFICE: he asked whether the paper that comes off that table could go in the book a stranger may walk up to, whether the reason it is not a complete paper could be printed on its face, and whether it could be printed in her hand and not a clerk's. She said yes in about four seconds and then said the thing he had not asked for: I have never once in nine years put a sentence of my own in that book, and I said on the twentieth of last month that I wanted to have had nothing to do with what you are going to do, and what I am going to do now is the opposite of that and I am doing it anyway.** He said, uncounted, that she had taken the shape he asked for apart and put her own name in the middle of it and that there is no version of what he asked for that does not have her in it now.

## Chapter 0273 — Nine Inches

**The twenty-third of the sixth month, low and warm, about nine people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-one days old. The board at 181, the train at 497, the unentered days at 211, a hundred and seventy-two from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-nine days off.** A clerk entered that the phrase *nine inches of rope* was said at the gate before nine people went and that nobody was asked to agree to it. **The three things a bell has to have before this district will call a sounding safe were entered in one hand and not as a paragraph, and all three of them are absent in this district this morning, and a bell is not a figure and a bell is not a sixth of the five protected things and a person standing in a doorway is not a sixth of the five either.** **THE TWO WALLS WERE READ OUT ALOUD UNDER THE FRAME AND REPRODUCE CHAPTER 259 CHARACTER FOR CHARACTER: RUNG. THE ELEVENTH OF THE THIRD MONTH OF THE FIFTEENTH YEAR. AT NIGHT. NOBODY CAME. / RUNG ON A NIGHT IN THE MONTH AFTER THAT ONE. / WE WENT DOWN THE LANE WHEN WE HEARD IT. / WE CAME BACK UP THE LANE WHEN IT STOPPED. / NAMES OF WHO HEARD IT —** And the man of about thirty-four who digs loam said, in fifteen words: **EITHER THE RULE IS NOT A RULE OR ONE OF THOSE TWO HANDS IS WRONG.** A clerk entered that the second of those two ringings is inside the month in which the first of them, with nobody coming to it, should have made the bell unringable, and that this district has not worked out which of those two things is true and is not going to work it out on the twenty-third of this month. **COST ONE: the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing went up the newer frame about nine feet back with his right hand, because his left hand has not been right since the first of this month, and his left hand had nothing to hold, and he came down the side of the headstock of iron and took the skin off the inside of his left forearm from the wrist to the elbow in one strip, and did not stop and did not say anything, and said it out loud eleven minutes later to about nine people in a hundred and twenty-two words: that is the second time this month something has come off that frame and taken a piece of me with it, and I am saying it in this yard and not in a lane because I said on the fourteenth of this month that I would rather everybody knew that about me on a day I said it, and it is a strip of skin the length of my forearm and I cannot get a fist round anything with that hand this evening and I have got to pull a rope with something in about two days.** **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said, in a hundred and forty-four words, that about nine people walked past his gate going to the bell and about nine walked back and both lots of nine were the same nine, and that there is not one other thing in this district where that is true, and that he is not a bearer of that road and has never been asked to be one.** The woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat said one uncounted thing at the foot of her own gate about a man with an arm out of his sleeve, and said she was not going to say it twice.

## Chapter 0274 — The First Safe Sounding

**The twenty-fourth of the sixth month, hot and close, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-two days old. The board at 182, the train at 498, the unentered days at 212, a hundred and seventy-three from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-eight days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights sixty-five days back.** **THE PRICE WAS PAID IN DAYLIGHT IN A YARD A STRANGER CAN WALK INTO, in two hundred and fifty-six words, with his arms down: in the yard of that frame four miles out past the loams there is a bell about two feet across the mouth of it and it is cracked from the rim up about a third of the way, and there has been nine inches of rope left inside a beam about eleven feet off the ground since somebody took three feet of it away a bit at a time so that you could not see it from the road, and tonight I am going to have that nine inches tied to something a man can hold, and I am going to pull it, and the bell is going to ring. A bell that is rung records everybody who hears it for one night. Everybody. Not the people who come to this yard. Not the people I have built a door for. Everybody inside the sound of it, and this district does not know yet how far that is and neither do I, and I am saying this out loud in daylight in front of about nineteen people so that nobody in this yard can say afterwards that they did not know. A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing is going to hold the rope. I am not going to be the one holding it.** A man of fifty-six said that he was not afraid of tonight and was afraid of the morning after and could not write down what he was afraid of, and then said sixty-one words: **I AM GOING TO SAY IT ANYWAY. FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN PEOPLE WERE COUNTED ON A SHEET AT THAT GATEPOST BY KNOCKING ON DOORS, AND A BELL TAKES NAMES OF EVERYBODY WHO HEARS IT AND DOES NOT KNOCK, AND I WOULD LIKE SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT ME TO WORK OUT IN THE NEXT TEN DAYS WHETHER THOSE ARE THE SAME ACT.** Nobody said anything for about nine seconds. **THE EIGHTH READING OF THE RIVAL RECORD WAS REFUSED OUT LOUD, in a hundred and forty words, at about a quarter past ten in the morning on the day the bell was ringing: if anybody in this yard wants to hear it read out then say so now and I will read it, and if nobody says so then nobody in this district has asked for an eighth reading and I am not going to hand them one. A refused reading is not a reading and the readings of that record in that yard are seven.** Nine inches of rope was tied to about two feet of new rope that the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart brought down without being asked, and a folded piece of paper and a piece of chalk went on the table, and about nine people slept in the room and were gone before six. **THE SOUNDING.** The bell does not sound like a bell; it sounds like somebody running the flat of a hand along the rim of a pan, and it goes on for longer than the people in that lane expect. The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale was in a house up that lane with the door shut and a blanket over her shoulders and did not come out of it while the bell was going, and came out of her own door about half a minute after it stopped and said that she has stood at that scale for nine years and has never once heard it. The last person through the front door was the man of about thirty-four who keeps a road, and the man in the doorway said it was about nine minutes, and a clerk entered that a man standing still in the dark with a hand that does not close is the worst person in this basin to time anything. A man of about nineteen counted the people who came in and **would not give the number to a clerk, and said that a number a man counts in the dark at the end of a lane is a figure about a man counting and not about the people.** **COST FOUR: the man of about thirty-four who digs loam took the paper off the table and it took him four goes, and on the second go it went on the ground at his feet, and he picked it up off the ground with two fingers, folded it in four the way it had been folded, and put it inside his coat, and said so out loud in the lane himself and nobody asked him about it.** A man of fifty-six said, in eighty-eight words: **It worked. Nine minutes and everybody in that lane was inside a building with two doors and nobody was hurt and nobody has been entered in anything, and I have been in this basin fifty-six years and I have never once seen a thing work. Now go to bed, all of you, because in about four hours this district is going to have to say out loud what it did tonight and most of what it is going to say is going to be for the first time.**

## Chapter 0275 — The Sheet On The Table

**The twenty-fifth of the sixth month, grey and cool after a close night, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-three days old. The board at 183, the train at 499, the unentered days at 213, a hundred and seventy-four from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-seven days off.** A clerk entered that a bell that has been rung records everybody who hears it for one night, and that this district does not know the figure of that night and has never known it. **THE SHEET WAS READ OUT AND READ BACK, because a yard reads a document when somebody asks it to read one and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing asked in the ordinary voice and nobody asked on his behalf: nine names in nine different hands, in about nine minutes, by the man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing, and a clerk entered that a piece of paper with nine names on it in nine hands is a document and is not a copy, and that the three documents this district does not own are three and that this is the fourth thing made or arrived in this district in twenty-five days and is not a fourth of them, and that the list the man of about forty-four took out of this district is not on it and nobody has seen it and nobody has counted it.** **COST FIVE: his voice went at about the sixth of the nine names and he finished the other three in a whisper, and he could not speak above a whisper for the rest of that day and for the day after, and he was asked nothing about it.** **THE CHILD. The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room with nothing over the door, about two miles the other way, with a bench about nine feet long in it and about eleven notches cut in the underside of the seat at intervals that are not even, said sixteen words: THERE IS A CHILD IN THAT ROOM AND HE HEARD IT AND HE DID NOT COME. Nobody in that yard said anything.** Then she said the rest of it, **in two hundred and ten words: I went back up that two miles at about half past three in the morning and he was sitting up on that bench in the dark and he was not crying and he was not asleep, and he is about eight years old and he has never been asked anything by anybody in this district in his life. Nobody told him there was a bell. I did not go back down that two miles to fetch him because it is two miles and it is dark and I am not a person who is sent for. I am telling this yard because a piece of paper came off a table four miles from here this morning and it is about a room, and a child heard a bell in this district last night and he is not in that room in the way that paper means, and I am not going to say what he is called and there is a man standing at that table who copies things and I would like him to hear me say that before anybody asks him to write something.** A clerk entered that a child of about eight has not been asked anything and cannot be counted either way and the record about him says not asked, and that a person who has not been asked has not refused and cannot be counted either way, **and that both of those sentences are the same sentence and a clerk is entering it twice on purpose and is not making a figure of it.** The man of about twenty-three said in a whisper, counted in a whisper, **in a hundred and thirty-four words: I am not going to write that child's name down anywhere and I am not going to ask him whether I may. Nobody in this district has ever asked him anything, and the second anybody does, he stops being a person nobody has asked and starts being a person who was asked, and this district found out on the seventeenth of this month what asking a person does to a person, and I have been the one who said it out loud in that yard twice.** A man of fifty-six said, in a hundred and fifty-three words, that we have a piece of paper with nine names on it in nine hands and it is a true piece of paper, that it is a record of a room, that everybody inside the sound of the bell is in the record the bell made, that nobody in this district has seen that record and cannot make it and has not the standing to make it, and that the difference between the two things is sitting on a bench in a room two miles off with its name on it. **A clerk entered that this district built a shelter and put a man in a doorway and had a piece of paper taken off a table in four goes, and that what was taken off was a record of a room and not the record the bell made.**

## Chapter 0276 — Two Shapes Of A Lot

**The twenty-sixth of the sixth month, clear and windy, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-four days old. The board at 184, the train at 500, the unentered days at 214, a hundred and seventy-five from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-six days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights sixty-seven days back, and eighty-four days since anybody wrote on a board.** **THE TWO SHAPES OF A LOT WERE PUT OUT LOUD, in two hundred and sixty-seven words, and a clerk entered that both of them are lots and that a lot is an instrument and a document and that neither creates a consent and neither makes a person a bearer and neither makes a house into a party and neither turns a right to give something up into a refusal, and that two shapes of a lot are not a vote and that nobody in that yard was asked which of them they wanted.** **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING ARGUED FOR THE ACCURATE ONE, in two hundred and sixty-three words: I have said for five months that a document which names nothing is the only safe kind there is, and I heard on the eighth of this month from a man of fifty-six that a printed rule was better than his own and I have been ill about it for nine days. A lot about the room is a hole with a reason printed under it. That is the exact shape of the third column of the covenant, and a hole printed on a sheet is not the same shape of thing as a hole left there by nobody asking. I want the child's name in that book. He is eight. He will be forty when somebody could take it off him, and I have watched a name go into a yard in this district on the seventeenth of this month and I would rather it went in on a page that somebody can be angry at than stay off it for the rest of his life because four grown people were frightened.** **The woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale said it before she was asked, in a hundred and eighty words: you are both talking about a child and neither of you has been in the room, and I want to know what happens to him in ten years, because ten years is a long time to be a thing a stranger may walk up and read, and there is nobody in this district who could tell me, including the man asking the question.** **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said, in a hundred and ninety-two words: I walked my four miles this morning and I have been the only man in this district walking towards that bell since it went, and a quarter of an hour is about as long as it takes a person to get a coat on and get a child up and get out of a door, and a sheet of paper cannot do that, and anything this district is going to write down tonight is going to be read by somebody who is not in it and who will not be a quarter of an hour away from it.** **COST SIX: the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally held a page open for about an hour because a man with a bad hand asked him to, and at about half past four in the afternoon he stood at the top of the bank and could not bring a figure into focus at the length of his own arm, and said seventy-four words: I AM NOT GOING TO BLIND MYSELF OVER A PIECE OF PAPER. HOLDING A PAGE IS NOT READING A PAGE AND I HAVE BEEN ASKED TO HOLD ONE AND I HELD IT FOR AN HOUR BECAUSE A MAN WITH A BAD HAND ASKED ME TO, AND I CANNOT SEE A FIGURE AT THE LENGTH OF MY OWN ARM THIS EVENING, AND I AM TELLING THIS BANK THAT INSTEAD OF WALKING DOWN IT WITH IT. — and then MY SIX HAVE NOT MOVED ON ANY OF THE SIXTY-SIX DAYS AND I AM NOT GOING TO BE A MAN WHO READS A FIGURE OUT OF A LOT ABOUT A NIGHT.** **And in about nine feet of lane outside the office at about half past six she said one thing, uncounted, and it was the second movement of the romance in the block: I can write the second of your two shapes in my hand and I can write the first of them in my hand as well, and that is not what I am refusing. What I am refusing is to be the hand that puts a child of about eight into a book in this basin and then goes back to being the person who keeps it.** He said, uncounted, that he will not do it in her hand, and will write the second shape out longhand in that yard on the twenty-eighth in front of about nineteen people and read it out and read it back, and then bring it to that door and she will put her own hand underneath his, and if she will not then they will find out what a lot is without a registrar's name at the bottom of it, and he does not know what that is either. **Two shapes of a lot were lying on the end of a trestle table and nothing had been signed and nothing had been chosen and no paper had gone anywhere.**

## Chapter 0277 — In A Room With A Shut Door

**The twenty-seventh of the sixth month, clear and still, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-five days old. The board at 185, the train at 501, the unentered days at 215, a hundred and seventy-six from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-five days off, and about nine people asked to come to a room with a shut door in it four hundred yards from that gate at about four o'clock.** **HE SAID THE ROOM OUT LOUD BEFORE HE SAID ANYTHING ELSE: the woman of this district's records office said on the eighteenth of this month in about nine feet of lane outside that door that there is no room in this district that nobody can walk into, and he was going to use this one, and the only thing shut in this room this afternoon was the door, and he wanted that said out loud before anybody in the room could say afterwards that they did not know which room it was.** **THE CHOICE AND THE REASON, IN THREE HUNDRED AND NINETY-TWO WORDS, SAID BEFORE ANYTHING WAS SIGNED: I am choosing the second of the two shapes. The first shape is a lot about a night, and a lot about a night names everybody who heard that bell, and to put a child of about eight into a lot like that you write his name, or you write where he sleeps, and where he sleeps is the same thing with more words on it. To write his name you have to ask him. Nobody in this district has ever asked that child a single question in his life. The second he is asked he stops being a person nobody has asked and he starts being a person who was asked, and this district found out on the seventeenth of this month, in a yard, at about ten in the morning, in front of about nineteen people, what asking a person does to a person, and I was the man who asked. And his name goes into a book in this basin that a stranger may walk up to and read, and a name put on a figure cannot be taken off it by the person it is put on, and he will be eight for another year and forty when it could be taken off him. There is no version of an accurate lot that does not cost that child the rest of his life, and I have looked for one for two days and I have not found it, and I am not going to be the man who decides that on a child's behalf in a yard.** **The woman of about twenty-nine was asked one question in that room and answered it and refused with a reason, in a hundred and sixty-three words: he is not to be named and he is not to be described either, and a description of where a child of about eight sleeps in this district is two miles long and about nine houses and one lane with nothing over the door on it, and a man with a case found that room in nine minutes on the fourteenth of this month because a woman at a house on that lane told him where it was, and that was not her fault and it was the end of the four years.** **The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said, in a hundred and fifty-four words: I argued in that yard yesterday for the other shape and I have not changed my mind and I am going to lose this, and I would like somebody to notice that the child of about eight is the reason the other shape is not the right one and that the child of about eight did not have anything to do with it. I would have written it. I would have been right about the hole and I would have been wrong about him, and I have not got an answer to that and I am not going to be given one.** **COST THREE, THREE DAYS LATE: a man of fifty-six said, in a hundred and sixty-nine words, that on the night of the twenty-fourth he was not in that building with two doors, that he walked four miles out past the loams in the dark to be standing under that frame when it went, and that he got there about two minutes after it stopped, and a man of about thirty-four was standing there with a rope in his hand about three feet long, and that he is fifty-six and had not walked four miles in the dark since he was a different age and got back up that road in about an hour and ten minutes with a hand that has not been right since, and that he is saying it in a room with a shut door because he would like one thing said out loud in this district this month to have been said by a man who was not in a building when it happened and did not know either. Nothing had been signed anywhere.**

## Chapter 0278 — The Lot About A Room

**The twenty-eighth of the sixth month, bright and hot before nine, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-six days old. The board at 186, the train at 502, the unentered days at 216, a hundred and seventy-seven from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-four days off, and eighty-nine days since anybody wrote on a board.** **THE LOT, FOUR LINES, WRITTEN LONGHAND IN THE YARD AND READ OUT AND READ BACK: A BELL IN AN OAK FRAME FOUR MILES OUT PAST THE LOAMS WAS PULLED AND RANG ON THE NIGHT OF THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF THIS MONTH AT ABOUT TWO IN THE MORNING. / ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WENT INTO THE BUILDING AT THE NEAR END OF THE LANE BEHIND THE BANK AND WROTE THEIR OWN NAMES ON A PIECE OF PAPER ON A TABLE OUTSIDE IT, AND THAT PIECE OF PAPER IS IN A BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THAT GATE. / THIS IS A RECORD OF A ROOM AND NOT A RECORD OF A NIGHT. THE RECORD THAT BELL MADE IS NOT A PAPER. NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT HAS SEEN IT AND NOBODY IN THIS DISTRICT IS ABLE TO TAKE IT OFF. / THERE IS A PERSON IN THIS DISTRICT WHO HEARD THAT BELL AND IS NOT IN THAT PIECE OF PAPER AND IS NOT NAMED ON THIS SHEET AND HAS NOT BEEN ASKED ANYTHING BY ANYBODY.** The man of about twenty-three wrote three of the four lines and could not hold a pen for the fourth, **and the fourth is in the hand of the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, and a clerk entered that a hand that finishes a sentence is not a bearer of the sentence and is not a keeper of it and did not become an instrument this district built and did not make a seventh of the six.** A man of fifty-six said, in a hundred and ninety-three words, that a hole with a reason printed under it is the exact shape of the third column of the covenant and that he has thought about it for twenty days and has still not got an answer, and that a clerk in this district said on the eighth of this month that a hole printed on a sheet is not the same shape of thing as a hole left there by nobody asking, and she was right. **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam said, in a hundred and eighty-seven words: a table with four legs against a wall outside a door, a piece of chalk, a piece of paper, and a man in the doorway to take the paper off. That is a counter. I have spent five months saying that this yard is not a counter and that a sheet at a gate in twenty days is not a market, and on the night of the twenty-fourth a man stood in a doorway and took a piece of paper off a table with four legs and put it in his coat, and that is a counter, and I have not got an argument against that and I have not got an argument for it either.** **At about half past four in the afternoon the woman who keeps the books in that room came out into the lane and read the lot once herself, and said, uncounted, that she has read it four times and the third of its four lines is the one she has not been able to put down, and that it says out loud, in a yard, on a piece of paper, that this district has a thing in it that it cannot look at and cannot get out of, and that she is not putting it in that book tonight, and is going to write one more piece of paper tomorrow and read it out in that room and would like a person who is not her to be in the room when she does.** A clerk entered that the sheet is to go into the first of the two books on that shelf and not the second one, and that nobody in that lane has read the second one. He said, uncounted, that then he will be in the room and will be the only one in it who is not of this district's records office and will not write anything and will not say anything about what is in that room, and that if anybody asks him afterwards what was said in there he will say that a room with a shut door in it is not a room anybody may walk up to. **The woman of fifty-eight said one thing to the lane and not to him, uncounted: I have carried water to eleven houses for nineteen years and I have been asked one question in all that time and I answered it on the nineteenth of this month and I have not slept properly since, and I do not know whether I went into that building on the night of the twenty-fourth and I am not going to be asked.**

## Chapter 0279 — Read Back

**The twenty-ninth of the sixth month, hot from before seven, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-seven days old. The board at 187, the train at 503, the unentered days at 217, a hundred and seventy-eight from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-three days off, and the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy days back.** **THE CHARTER, FIVE LINES, WRITTEN OUT IN THE REGISTRAR'S OWN HAND WITH ONE WORD IN ANOTHER MAN'S HAND BECAUSE HER HAND STOPPED ON IT IN THE MIDDLE OF THE FOURTH ATTEMPT, READ OUT IN THE ROOM WHERE THE BOOKS ARE: THE BUILDING AT THE NEAR END OF THE LANE BEHIND THE BANK IS A SHELTER FOR ANY PERSON WHO GOES INTO IT ON ANY NIGHT. / ANY PERSON MAY GO IN AND OUT OF IT AND NOBODY IS ASKED WHO THEY ARE. / A PERSON WHO GOES IN AND OUT OF IT IS NOT ENTERED ANYWHERE BY GOING IN OR OUT OF IT. / THE PERSON WHO STANDS IN ITS DOORWAY ON A NIGHT IS A PERSON WHO HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT THEY WILL STAND IN IT. NOBODY PUTS A PERSON IN A DOORWAY. / THIS BUILDING IS WHAT MAKES A SOUNDING SAFE. A SOUNDING IS SAFE IF EVERY PERSON WHO HEARD IT WENT INTO THIS BUILDING OR WAS ASKED WHY THEY DID NOT.** **READ BACK BY THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT, WHO HAD BEEN ASKED AND SAID YES IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS. She read the first four lines and stopped at the fifth for about nine seconds, and nobody in that room said anything, and then she read it out loud anyway and said why she had stopped, in two hundred and seventy-eight words: I have carried water to eleven houses for nineteen years and one of those eleven houses is a house in the ground in the shadow of a building I can see two ends of from a rise a mile out past the loams, and the woman in that house is not one of the four hundred and eleven on that sheet and is not on any sheet, and she asked me for a second bucket in the second week of last month and I put a second bucket there on the third of that month. So there is a house in the shadow of that building that nobody in this district has asked a question of in nineteen years and that is not on the list of the people who are to be moved. The fifth of those five lines says a sounding is safe if every person who heard it went into that building or was asked why they did not. I have asked that woman about a bucket twice in nineteen years and both times I asked her about a bucket. So either that house is a house this district can shelter, and I have been carrying water to a house this district can shelter for nineteen years and did not know it, or the fifth of those five lines is not true, and it is going into a book in this room that a stranger may walk up to and read.** A clerk entered that the ninth of the eleven houses is a house in the shadow of the Hall, is not one of the four hundred and eleven, is not on any sheet, and that no number on the sheet at that gatepost moved. **A man of fifty-six said, in a hundred and ninety-three words, that four hundred and eleven is a count of people who answered a door and forty-one is a count of lines on two open pages in one book in one room in this district and the difference between those two numbers is three hundred and seventy and is arithmetic and is not a finding, and that the fifth of those five lines is a claim about the whole of this district and a claim about the whole of this district is made out of a count of the people who opened a door, and that they have rung a bell, and a bell does not knock, and that somebody four hundred miles away is entitled to read that and work out what it means for four hundred and eleven people, and that he does not know what it means and neither does anybody in that room.** **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam said, in two hundred and two words, that a charter that says a person is not entered by going in and out of it is right and a charter that says a person who was not asked is covered by it is wrong, and that the lot and the charter disagree with each other on the same page and neither of them is a person.** A clerk entered that the fourth line of any offer that has been on a table in this district five months has not been read out in this room or anywhere else this month, and the refusals to read are nine, and the departure is unspent, and there is still no date in any ledger for the next reading. **At about six in the evening the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room with nothing over the door was at the end of that lane about two miles short of her room and said one uncounted thing: there are five lines in that book and I can only read three of them, and there is a child of about eight on that bench who was awake at half past three in the morning and heard the whole of it, and nobody put him in a doorway and nobody asked him anything, and I have been told that is his own good fortune, and I am going back up that two miles tonight and I am not going to knock on a door about it.**

## Chapter 0280 — A Wrong Sheet

**The thirtieth of the sixth month, low and warm, about nineteen people in the yard at ten, and the chair sixty-eight days old, and this morning the last morning of the sixth month. The board at 188, the train at 504, the unentered days at 218, a hundred and seventy-nine from the second of January, the first day of the month after next thirty-two days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy-one days back, ninety-one days since anybody wrote on a board, seventy days since the six households last moved, and twenty-nine days of a record in force.** A clerk entered that thirty-two is no days to the end of this month and the thirty-one of the month after it and the first of the month after that, and that the length of the month after that is not stated in this district. **THE COUNT PARAGRAPH, ENTERED IN ONE HAND AND NOT AS A PARAGRAPH: the five things and the fifth not paid and not priced; the six instruments and a public entrance entered as not a seventh and a board with a finger's width for a name and a stool both refused on the page; the three documents nobody owns and four that were made or arrived; the five protected things and no sixth, and a bell is not a sixth and a person standing in a doorway is not a sixth; the four conditions; thirteen and twelve and no join proposed; nine refusals to read and the fourth line of the offer not read out; the eight refusals with no reason and the nine people who gave one with a reason; the boards five and the lines full six with a seventh ruled and empty at the ninth place; the four columns of not-askings and a fifth proposed on a board and refused; no rate turning a year into coppers; the seven refusals about the ninth holding, not the same figure as the eight and not added to them; the rival record in force twenty-nine days and read out seven times and the eighth reading refused out loud on the twenty-fourth with a reason and a refused reading is not a reading and nobody has answered it; five different ninths with a sixth available and not taken; the man of about thirty at a counter, the man of about forty-one, the man with the portfolio and the man of about forty-four with a case, all four off the page, and a list nobody has seen and nobody has counted.** **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART CAME OUT OF THE ROOM FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THAT GATE AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST NINE HAVING READ TWO PIECES OF PAPER NOBODY HAD ASKED HIM TO READ, and said, in two hundred and forty-two words: I have read two pieces of paper. I did not ask to and nobody stopped me and I am not a stranger and I am not a clerk and I do not keep anything except a cart. The first of them says a bell was rung and about nine people went into a building and put their own names on a piece of paper, and it says that there is a person in this district who heard that bell and is not in that piece of paper and is not named on it. The second of them says the building is what makes a sounding safe, and that a sounding is safe if every person who heard it went into that building or was asked why they did not. I am a man who hauls sacks. I am telling you what those two say when you put them side by side. The second one is not true, and the first one is the reason the second one is not true, and both of them are in a book in this district that anybody can walk in and read, and the second one is the one that is going to be picked up by somebody who is not any of you, and I would like it entered that the man who said it is a man who drives a cart and that he is not one of the things he is talking about.** **A man of fifty-six said, in a hundred and eighty-five words, that a yard is a room with no roof and the worst possible place to find out that a document you are about to publish is wrong, and that he has been wrong about four hundred and eleven for six weeks and that a woman of fifty-eight sat down on a step outside the second house up that lane on the nineteenth of this month and told him, and that he said so out loud in that lane the same evening and nobody argued with him about it and nobody has asked him about it since, and that a man of about twenty-nine in that yard has just said the same thing out loud in a sentence, and he is going to let that sentence stand and is not going to add anything to it.** **THE SECOND MOVEMENT OF THE ROMANCE, IN THE DOORWAY, UNCOUNTED, AND NEITHER SENTENCE RESOLVES ANYTHING: I told you in about nine feet of lane on the twenty-sixth of this month that I was not going to put my own hand on either of your two shapes, and I have written one of them out in my own hand from the first word to the last, and I have put my own hand under the other one at about five this afternoon, and a man of about thirty-four read it out, and I did not take my hand back off it, and that is the whole of what has happened between us in twenty days. There is no room in this district that nobody can walk into and I said that to you in about nine feet of lane on the eighteenth of this month and I have not stopped saying it, and what I have done since is put my own hand to two pieces of paper in a book that anybody can walk in and read, and I do not know what to call that, and I am not going to call it anything tonight. — I am not going to call it anything either, and I am not going to say thank you, and I have not thanked you once in twenty days and you have not once asked me to, and I would like both of those to stay the way they are for a while longer than a month.** **AND THE COST, IN A LANE BEHIND A BANK AT ABOUT HALF PAST SEVEN, IN TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-NINE WORDS, SAID BY THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING: we put two pieces of paper into a book in this district this month and one of them is a hole with a reason printed under it and the other one is a charter that says a bell in a frame four miles off was safe, and it was not safe, and the reason it was not safe is a child of about eight who nobody in this district has asked anything in his life and a house in the ground in the shadow of a building eleven miles off that a woman of fifty-eight has carried water to for nineteen years and that is not on the list of anybody who is to be moved, and neither the child nor the woman in that house can be put anywhere in this basin without somebody making them people who were asked. So there is a charter in a book in this district that a stranger may walk up to and read and it is wrong on its face, and I want that said in a lane by a man who mends fencing before anything else is said about it. A wrong sheet that a stranger can walk up to and read is a worse thing than no sheet. No sheet is an absence and a man in a yard can say an absence is a lie and be wrong about it. A wrong sheet is a thing a stranger can pick up, and point at, and carry down a road, and we put it there ourselves, and I am not going to be the man who takes it back out of that book.** A clerk entered that a wrong count is not struck in this district and no count has been struck in this block, and that the forty-one names said out loud in that yard on the seventeenth are forty people and one of them is a person twice, and that figure is not struck either.

## Chapter 0281 — An Address

**The first of the seventh month, the month turned in the night and a clerk of nineteen years wrote one line and put the pen down. About nineteen people in the yard of Lot Seventeen at ten, and the chair sixty-nine days old. The board at 189, the train at 505, the unentered days at 219, a hundred and eighty from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month thirty-one days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy-two days back, and the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given three months back and ninety-nine days. Ninety-two days since anybody wrote on a board.** **THE RELATIVE MONTH RULE WROTE INTO THE PAGE AGAIN: from this morning *last month* is the sixth and *the month before last* is the fifth and the third month is three months back, and this district has turned a word twice in nine weeks and did it twice in the night both times — AND THE DISTRICT'S OWN NAME FOR THE REMOVAL DAY, *THE FIRST DAY OF THE MONTH AFTER NEXT*, NO LONGER MEANS WHAT IT MEANT AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD IS TO USE IT AGAIN THIS MONTH, BECAUSE FROM THE SEVENTH THE MONTH AFTER NEXT IS THE NINTH.** **The derivation of the thirty-one is entered: thirty days to the end of the seventh month, because this morning is the first of it and one day is gone, plus the first of the eighth, and the length of the eighth month is not stated in this district and was not used and is not to be used on any of the ten days this block reaches.** **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP FOR ANYBODY WHO WILL USE THEM CAME THROUGH THE GATE AT HALF PAST TEN WITH A HAND-CART AND A TENT ROLLED ON IT, GAVE NO NAME, WAS NOT ASKED FOR ONE, AND ASKED THE YARD FOR ITS ADDRESS, in two hundred and forty-five words, in front of about nineteen people: I am not going to stand in this yard and tell you what I sell. I am asking this yard to say out loud where it is, once, so that a person who cannot be found at a door knows one place to come.** Nobody said the word. **A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing asked him for the name of the thing in ninety-six words before anybody else opened their mouth, and he said it in about four seconds: A WAY TO PAY A PERSON WHO IS NOT IN A HOUSEHOLD. A clerk entered that an address is not a bearer and a wall is not an address and a yard a stranger can walk up to is not an address until somebody in it says the words, and nobody in that yard said them.** **A man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said, in a hundred and eighty-six words, that a yard is a good address for a person who already lives in the district and a useless one for a person who does not, and that the man has been down four days of road and has not said which of the nine settlements he came out of.** **COST: the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing could not take the weight of the hand-cart on his left hand and went up the bank with it on his right shoulder instead and said so in about four seconds, uncounted, because that hand has had no skin on the inside of it since the twenty-third of last month.** A man of about thirty-seven said he would be at that gate in the morning with something written on it and would ask for it to be read out.

## Chapter 0282 — Five Lines

**The second of the seventh month, hot off the ground. About nineteen people in the yard of Lot Seventeen. The board at 190, the train at 506, the unentered days at 220, a hundred and eighty-one from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month thirty days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy-three days back, ninety-three days since anybody wrote on a board.** **THE OFFER, FIVE LINES, READ OUT AND READ BACK. A TABLE UNDER A LEAN-TO ON THE FAR SIDE OF THE ELEVEN MILE ROAD WILL BE OPEN ON ANY MORNING OF A WEEK TO ANY PERSON IN THIS DISTRICT WHO IS NOT ENTERED IN ANY HOUSEHOLD. ELEVEN COPPERS A WEEK IS PAID TO ANY PERSON WHO STANDS AT THAT TABLE ON ANY MORNING OF A WEEK AND IS COUNTED AT IT. A PERSON WHO STANDS AT THAT TABLE IS NOT ASKED WHAT THEY ARE CALLED AND IS NOT ENTERED IN ANY BOOK IN THIS DISTRICT. THE ELEVEN COPPERS COME OUT OF WHAT THE NINE HOUSES IN ONE LANE IN THIS DISTRICT PAY, AND NINE HOUSES HAVE PAID FOR FIVE MONTHS. A PERSON WHO HAS STOOD AT THAT TABLE ONCE CANNOT BE UNCOUNTED AGAIN.** Read out by the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing in one run of words with a stop at the end of each, at the man's own request, and read back by the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart who got nothing wrong, which is the rule of the counter, and who was asked to read it back and not asked whether he agreed with it. **THE FIFTH LINE IS NOT TRUE AND CANNOT BE MADE TRUE, in a hundred and fifty-two words: A count of the people who come to a place is a count of the people who come. That is not a flaw in that line. That is what a count is.** **The man of about thirty-seven agreed out loud in the same yard in about four seconds that he wrote that line on purpose and would like it read again so that everybody had heard it twice, and that if he had left it off then in five months a man with a book would come and say nobody told me and he would be right.** **A clerk entered that the fourth line of the offer that has been on a table in this district for five months was not read out, that this sheet has five lines and is not that offer, and that the refusals to read are nine and did not move and the departure is unspent.** **A man who convenes nine settlements said, in a hundred and fifty-six words, that a pool of nine houses is the fifth of the five things this district does not have put in a shape that fits it exactly, that this district has been offered this before by a man with a case and it was fitted to the same hole, and that that man is not in this district and nobody sent this one.** **The man of about thirty-four who digs loam said, uncounted, that the second of those five lines is the only line on that sheet doing anything, and that he did it to about nine people at two in the morning on the night of the twenty-fourth of last month and did not know a single one of their names.** A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat carried a copy of the five lines up two miles to a room with nothing over the door and read it to about nine people there, and nobody asked her to.

## Chapter 0283 — The Fifth Thing

**The third of the seventh month, close and low. The board at 191, the train at 507, the unentered days at 221, a hundred and eighty-two from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-nine days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy-four days back, ninety-four days since anybody wrote on a board.** **A man of about thirty-four who digs loam came in with a copy of the five lines in his coat and said, in a hundred and ninety-eight words, that there is no lean-to on the far side of the eleven mile road: there is a wall about four feet high with a gate in it that is not on a hinge, and a field about two hundred yards across with a ditch about three feet deep in the seventh month where it was about two feet deep in the sixth, and he walked there and back in about eleven hours on two legs and had his hand in that ditch for about an hour and could not get it out the way he got it in.** **THE PRICE WAS NAMED OUT LOUD IN THE YARD FOR THE SECOND TIME: ELEVEN COPPERS A WEEK, the same eleven coppers a man with a case paid a man of about twenty-three on the thirteenth of last month, and a clerk entered that a price said out loud in a yard is a price somebody can refuse and a price in a letter is not, and that eleven coppers a week is a price and is not a rate in this basin, and that there is still no rate in this district that turns a year into coppers and there never has been one.** **THE REFUSAL, in a hundred and eighty-eight words, with the man who has been in about four of them in five months saying he is not going to be in another one and is not going to be one of a number of anything at a table either. A clerk entered that THE REFUSALS OF ELEVEN COPPERS A WEEK ARE NINE, THAT IT MOVED FROM EIGHT THAT MORNING BECAUSE A REFUSAL WAS MADE OUT LOUD IN A YARD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE BY A PERSON WHO GAVE A REASON, THAT THE REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN ARE EIGHT AND DID NOT MOVE, AND THAT THE NINE AND THE EIGHT ARE NOT THE SAME FIGURE AND ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER AND NEITHER FIGURE IS A NINTH OR A TENTH OF THE OTHER.** **THE WORD RATE, said twice by the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale before anybody asked her for it, and then two hundred and eighteen words: eleven coppers a week is not a price. A rate is a thing that is already true of you whether anybody says it or not. I weigh about nine sacks a day and I have never weighed a day. I am not standing at a table to be one of a figure.** **A man of fifty-six said, in a hundred and sixty words, that four hundred and eleven is a count of the people who answered a door, and that a count of the people who come to a table is not a lie, and that in about five months somebody four hundred miles off will hold two counts of the same people and the two will not be the same figure and neither will be a lie.** No number on the sheet at that gatepost moved on the third of this month.

## Chapter 0284 — The Second Side

**The fourth of the seventh month, still and warm. The board at 192, the train at 508, the unentered days at 222, a hundred and eighty-three from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-eight days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy-five days back, ninety-five days since anybody wrote on a board, the rival record in force thirty-three days.** **A SHEET OF FOUR LINES OF PRINT WITH NO NAME ON IT AND NO SEAL ON IT CAME IN ON THE BACK OF THE SAME HAND-CART. AN OFFER TO PAY A PERSON WHO IS NOT ENTERED IN ANY HOUSEHOLD IS A FIGURE ABOUT PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT ENTERED IN ANY HOUSEHOLD. THE PLACE AN OFFER IS MADE IN IS ENTERED IN THE RECORD OF THAT PLACE. A PERSON WHO IS PAID WEEKLY BY ANYBODY IS A PERSON WHO CAN BE FOUND. THE FIGURE OF THE PEOPLE IN THE GROUND IN THE SHADOW OF A BUILDING ELEVEN MILES OFF THIS DISTRICT IS TO BE COMPLETED BEFORE THE FIRST DAY OF THE EIGHTH MONTH.** **IT WAS ASKED FOR AND NOT READ OUT.** A woman who keeps the books in the room four hundred yards from that gate came up the bank and said one thing to the lane and not to the yard, uncounted: Do not read it in that yard. Ask him to read it in a room with a door and nine people in it who have chosen to be there, or do not read it at all this month, and I will not tell him which. **A clerk entered that a sheet that is not read out has not been refused and is not a reading, and that asking a yard to read a thing is not asking the yard to agree to it.** **A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing read the four lines himself as a thing he had read and not as a thing read out in that yard, and said, in two hundred and seventeen words, that it is the same shape of thing as three other sheets in this district and is not the same thing, and that it is the fourth of that shape and the worst of the four, because the other three do not have a second line in them, and that nobody has threatened this yard this month and that a threat can be refused and a printed rule about where a thing happens cannot.** **A clerk entered that the yard of Lot Seventeen has now been asked twice in ten days by two sides to be an address and has answered neither time, and that a yard that is the address of two sides is a yard and not a market, and that a market is a thing where a person is asked a question, and that this yard has not asked anybody in it a question about what they want there.** **COST: the man of about thirty-four who digs loam went down into the ditch behind the building with two doors, dug out about four feet of it, was in the bottom for about three hours, could not get himself out, and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, whose right shoulder has not been what it was since the first of this month, put a hand down and had him up by the arm. Neither of them said anything about it for about nine minutes.** And he said, in a hundred and seventy-one words, that there is about a foot of water in the bottom of that ditch and there was about two inches in it on the eleventh of last month, and that the ditch went up about ten inches in twenty-three days, and that a plank does not keep nights and it does not keep water either.

## Chapter 0285 — Two Sides, One Address

**The fifth of the seventh month, hot before nine. The board at 193, the train at 509, the unentered days at 223, a hundred and eighty-four from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-seven days off, the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given three months back and a hundred and three days, ninety-six days since anybody wrote on a board, the rival record in force thirty-four days.** **THE ANSWER, IN TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-NINE WORDS, BY A MAN WHO CONVENES NINE SETTLEMENTS, TO THE QUESTION THE WHOLE YARD HAD BEEN ASKING SINCE THE FIRST OF THIS MONTH: A MARKET THAT IS THE ADDRESS OF BOTH SIDES CANNOT BE THE ADDRESS OF EITHER SIDE. A man comes down eleven miles of road with a table in a hand-cart and wants a place to say where it is, and a body four hundred miles off prints four lines and says the place an offer is made in goes in the record of that place, and those two things are pulling at the same gate from opposite ends. I have got no third thing. I have got two things and a gate and I am not going to stand in this yard and pretend I have found a third.** **A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX, IN TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-THREE WORDS: a market is a place where a person is asked a question, and in ten days the only questions anybody has asked in that yard have been about what that yard is, and every time something has been read out in it somebody has been asked a question. The problem is not that the two people are pulling at that gate. The problem is that the gate has never had a question in it.** **A JOIN WAS PROPOSED AND REFUSED ON THE PAGE** — the five lines and the four lines on one page, read out together — **and a clerk entered that two documents joined on a page are a join and not a comparison, that the refusals to join two things are thirteen and the registrar's own count is twelve and that neither moved, and that a join is a join and that a fifth refusal of one join is one join with five days of people watching it.** A man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said no in a hundred and thirty-six words and gave both halves: it does not make them agree, it makes them one thing a man can carry, and if those two are on one page then everybody who stands at that table is standing under both of them at once. **COST: the man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing got through two lines of the copy of the four lines of print and could not do the third, put the paper down on the end of the trestle table with the chalk mark of the third line started, and said in a hundred and thirty words that he offered before anybody asked him and that he is not going to be able to do the rest of it this month. A clerk entered that a hand that stops in the middle of a document is a hand and is not a refusal and is not a consent.** And the man of about thirty-seven said he did not come eleven miles for a piece of paper and that if he cannot have an address he will stand at a wall four feet high with a table behind it every morning of a week like a man selling apples, and a clerk entered that a wall about four feet high with a table behind it is not an address and is not a page and is not a document, and that **the instruments this district has built and not named are six and that a join is a join and is not a seventh of them and that a copy is a copy and is not a seventh of them and that the six did not move today.**

## Chapter 0286 — The Woman Who Carries Water

**The sixth of the seventh month, low and warm, with the woman of fifty-eight at the bank with two buckets. The board at 194, the train at 510, the unentered days at 224, a hundred and eighty-five from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-six days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy-seven days back, ninety-seven days since anybody wrote on a board, the pool of refusals a hundred and four days, the rival record in force thirty-five days. Two buckets were not counted and nobody counted them.** **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN ASKED THAT YARD ONE QUESTION OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE: DOES ANYBODY IN THIS DISTRICT CARRY WATER TO A HOUSE IN THE GROUND. Four people turned round and looked at the woman of fifty-eight, and a man of about nineteen who counts counted the people who turned round and got four and gave the number out loud, and a clerk entered that a number a man of about nineteen counts in daylight in front of about nineteen people is a figure about four people looking round and is not a figure about the woman of fifty-eight.** A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said in about four seconds that nobody is to answer that, and a clerk entered that a man who says out loud that nobody is to answer a question has not refused the question. **COST: THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT ANSWERED IT HERSELF, IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED HER A SECOND TIME, and said the rest of it in two hundred and five words: Yes. I have carried water to eleven houses for nineteen years and the ninth of those eleven is a house in the ground and it is in the shadow of a building I can see two ends of from a rise a mile out past the loams, and the woman in it is not one of the four hundred and eleven and is not on any sheet, and she asked me for a second bucket in the second week of last month and I put a second bucket there on the third of that month. So she is not on the list of the people who are to be moved and she has never been asked a question in nineteen years and I have asked her about a bucket twice and I am not going to ask her anything else today.** A clerk entered that the ninth of the eleven houses is a house in the shadow of a building eleven miles off and that no number on the sheet at that gatepost moved on the sixth of this month. **THE PANEL ARRIVED WITH NOTHING ELSE AND NOBODY ASKED FOR IT: A FIGURE MADE BY COUNTING THE PEOPLE WHO CAME TO A PLACE IS A FIGURE ABOUT THE COMING AND NOT ABOUT THE PEOPLE. A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX DISAGREED WITH IT OUT LOUD IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS: that is the fifth sentence of mine that has been said back to me in twenty days and I have not said it once.** A clerk entered that a panel creates no consent, makes no person a bearer, makes no house into a party, turns no right to give something up into a refusal, does not decide who hears a bell, and is not a seventh of the six. **At half past four the man of about thirty-seven and the man of fifty-six stood at the top of the bank, and the man of about thirty-seven said in a hundred and fifty-nine words that he is not going to ask about that house again, and that a house in the ground does not answer a door, and that a clerk entered the whole of the ninth of the eleven houses and that no number on the sheet at that gatepost moved.**

## Chapter 0287 — The First Morning

**The seventh of the seventh month, bright and already hot at nine.
