The board at 195, the train at 511, the unentered days at 225, a hundred and eighty-six from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-five days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights seventy-eight days back, ninety-eight days since anybody wrote on a board, the rival record in force thirty-six days.** **COST: A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART said it in a hundred and eighty-seven words: I took his hand-cart and his table and his tent out to that wall on the fifth of this month and I brought it back yesterday afternoon, and that is two days of my cart, and I had a load of stone in on the sixth that I did not take, and the man who paid for it has it by another road and I do not know whether that is going to cost me the road after next. I have not been paid for the two days and he did not offer and I did not ask, because when I saw that wall I understood there is not going to be anything to be paid for, there is going to be a table on it.** A clerk entered that he was not in this district on the fifth and the sixth and that those are two days off this district's figure and not two days on it. **A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat said one uncounted thing at the foot of her own gate and it was not about an arm: a gate that is not on a hinge is a gate somebody can be counted coming through, and I am not going to say that twice.** **A man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said, in a hundred and eighty-four words: a road does not go where a sheet says it goes. There is a turning off the eleven mile road about a mile before that wall and it is not on the sheet and there is nothing on it saying a table is down it, and on the fifth there was one cart on it going out and nothing coming back, and on the sixth there was nothing going out at all.** **THE FIRST MORNING, SAID IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS: ONE PERSON, AT EIGHT IN THE MORNING, ON THE FIRST MORNING, AND I WOULD LIKE THIS YARD NOT TO MAKE A FINDING OUT OF ONE.** A clerk entered that a person who stood at a table on the morning it was first set up has not agreed to anything and cannot be counted either way. **At half past four the man of about twenty-three who sells nothing and copies for nothing read the four lines of the printed sheet out loud in the ordinary voice in about nine feet of lane outside the office to about four people, because he cannot copy them any more and a thing that is only ever read by one man in a yard stops when that man is ill. The woman who keeps the books in that room was four feet away with her hand on the door and did not stop him.** And the man of about thirty-seven said he will come to him every morning of a week and the people will hear it from him and not from me, and that I am not going to say thank you for it, and that you have not done it for me and I have not asked you.

## Chapter 0288 — A Room With A Shut Door

**The eighth of the seventh month, close and still. The board at 196, the train at 512, the unentered days at 226, a hundred and eighty-seven from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-four days off, the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given three months back and a hundred and six days.** **THE ROMANCE'S HARDEST MOVEMENT OF THE VOLUME, IN A ROOM WITH A SHUT DOOR IN IT FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THAT GATE, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, AND IT IS A REFUSAL OF HELP.** He said the room out loud before he said anything else, because he had learned that in this district last month, and then made the offer in two hundred and seventy-eight words: he wants to put both sheets into the first of the two books in that room with the day on them, and to have the reason printed on the face of it in her hand, because a wrong thing that is on the record of this district is a thing a body four hundred miles off has to read before it does anything about it, and right now the second of those four lines is a rule nobody can point at, because there is no record anywhere in this basin of an offer having been made. **SHE REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AND GAVE A REASON IN TWO HUNDRED AND NINETY-FOUR WORDS, AND THE REASON IS ABOUT THE BOOK AND NOT ABOUT HIM: I have kept that book in this room nine years and I know exactly what is in it, every line, in the order the lines are in, and the reason that room is safe is not the shut door. The shut door is nothing. If the fifth of those five lines goes on a page in that book with a day on it, then this basin has written down, in a place a stranger can walk up to, that eleven coppers a week was offered to people who are not in households on a morning in the seventh month, and the fifth line says a person who stood at that table once cannot be uncounted again. So the day goes in the book and the figure goes in the book and the third of those five lines, which is the only line on that sheet worth having, is undone by the second sheet we would put in beside it. You would be publishing the morning. I am not refusing you because it is your book. It is my book, it is my hand, and it is my room.** **AND HE DID NOT TAKE IT BACK**, in two hundred and two uncounted words, and said that in five days it will be on the end of that trestle table and on nine other walls in four other settlements if he has to carry it there himself, and that he is not going to say in that room that she is wrong, because she is not. **AND SHE SAID, UNCOUNTED, that it will be in a yard and not in a book and that that is not the same thing and it is not nothing, and that there is no room in this district that nobody can walk into and that he shut this one for an hour and a half that afternoon and about nine people were in it and a clerk four hundred yards off did not know until about seven, and that is the whole of what a shut door buys, and do not come and ask me again this month.** A clerk entered that two people who have said out loud that they are not going to call a thing anything have not refused it and have not consented to it, and that a man who has not withdrawn an offer has not bound anybody to it and has acquired nothing by having made it, and that the second book on that shelf has been open since the sixth of last month and was not read that afternoon and nothing was entered in it. At half past five the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing was in the lane outside that door, told one sentence, and said he was not going to say whether the man was right.

## Chapter 0289 — One Thing, Asked Once

**The ninth of the seventh month, dull and warm. The board at 197, the train at 513, the unentered days at 227, a hundred and eighty-eight from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-three days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty days back, a hundred days since anybody wrote on a board, the pool of refusals a hundred and seven days, the rival record in force thirty-eight days. A man of about nineteen counted the heads at the gate and got nine and gave the figure out loud without being asked, and a clerk entered that a head counted at a gate in daylight is a figure about a gate and not about the nine people.** **At about eleven in the morning about nine people at the gate asked the man of about thirty-seven how many people had stood at his table on the two mornings it had been up, and he said no in about four seconds in a hundred and ninety-eight words: a figure of two mornings is a figure about two mornings, and on the third of this month a woman who keeps a scale in this district said the word rate out loud in that yard before anybody asked her for it, and I have been in this basin nine days and I had not thought of it, and she weighs about nine sacks a day and has never weighed a day.** **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY CAME DOWN THE LANE WITH HIS BOOK UNDER HIS ARM ON HIS OWN TWO FEET AND WENT PAST THAT YARD WITHOUT STOPPING AND SAID GOOD MORNING AND NOTHING ELSE, and a clerk entered the figure of his six out of a ladder, seventy-nine days, and that she did not ask him for it and will not ask him for it this month.** **SHE CAME DOWN FOUR MILES AT HALF PAST FOUR AND WENT BACK UP THEM AT HALF PAST FIVE, AND HE ASKED HER FOR ONE THING IN ABOUT NINE FEET OF LANE OUTSIDE THAT DOOR, IN FRONT OF THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN: whether anybody in this district who is not in a household is standing at that table.** **AND SHE REFUSED IT IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND THE COUNT CAME TO TWO HUNDRED AND SIXTY-NINE, AND SHE GAVE HIM A REASON SHE HAD NEVER GIVEN ANYBODY, WHICH IS NOT THE SAME AS THE ELEVEN YEARS: THE ELEVEN YEARS ARE HOW LONG SHE HAS WALKED HER LINES, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS SUMMARY AND OF THE CHAPTER'S OWN FOOTER MADE THE REASON ELEVEN YEARS OLD, WHICH THE PROSE DOES NOT SAY. WHAT SHE SAYS IS: You have asked me for this three times since the first of last month and I have said no three times and I have never given you a reason, and I am going to give you one now because you have finally asked me for one thing instead of a general favour. I keep lines. I walk them at night in fours and fives and there is a wire on the left of one of them that has taken the skin off a shin twice. If I tell you who is standing at a table, then in eleven years somebody can work out who walks a line. I am not going to stand in a lane and hand you the second one dressed up as the first.** A clerk entered that she is not a keeper of a route, that a line is not a document, and that **nobody in that lane and nobody in that room was given a sentence that settles anything.** **And he said, uncounted, I enter that I got nothing, and I am not going to ask her a fourth time and I am not going to find another way round her.**

## Chapter 0290 — Two Sides, One Gate

**The tenth of the seventh month, hot and clear. The board at 198, the train at 514, the unentered days at 228, a hundred and eighty-nine from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-two days off being twenty-one days to the end of the seventh plus the first of the eighth, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-one days back, the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given three months back and a hundred and eight days, a hundred and one days since anybody wrote on a board, the chair seventy-eight days, the rival record in force thirty-nine days. A clerk entered the whole ten-term removal ladder out loud with its own day named: thirty-one, thirty, twenty-nine, twenty-eight, twenty-seven, twenty-six, twenty-five, twenty-four, twenty-three and twenty-two, and that the first of those ten was counted off the first of the seventh month and not off the thirtieth of the sixth.** **COST: THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN COULD NOT LIFT THE SECOND OF TWO TRESTLE BOARDS OFF THE BACK OF HIS OWN HAND-CART WITH EITHER HAND AND STOOD AT THE BACK OF IT WITH BOTH HANDS UNDER THE BOARD FOR ABOUT NINE SECONDS AND DID NOT GET IT UP, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM LIFTED IT OFF HIMSELF, PUT IT AGAINST THE OUTSIDE WALL AND SAID NOTHING AT ALL.** And he said it in a hundred and thirty-seven words: I have carried a hand-cart eleven miles twice this month and I have lost the use of both hands doing it and I am not going to be able to set that table up on my own tomorrow or the day after. I am not asking this yard for anything. **THE COUNT PARAGRAPH, ENTERED IN ONE HAND AND NOT AS A PARAGRAPH: five things and the fifth named out loud on the third and still not made; six instruments, one seventh proposed and refused on the fifth and seven further things entered as not a seventh; three documents nobody owns and two that arrived; five protected things and no sixth, and an address is not a sixth and a lean-to is not a sixth and a table is not a sixth and a panel is not a sixth; four conditions; thirteen and twelve and one join refused; nine refusals to read and the fourth line of the offer not read out; NINE REFUSALS OF ELEVEN COPPERS A WEEK WHICH MOVED FROM EIGHT ON THE THIRD OF THIS MONTH AND EIGHT REFUSALS WITH NO REASON A CLERK OF A HOUSE HAS GIVEN WHICH DID NOT MOVE, AND THE TWO ARE NOT THE SAME FIGURE, AND THE SEVEN REFUSALS ABOUT THE NINTH HOLDING ARE A THIRD FIGURE; a hundred and one days since anybody wrote on a board; the chair seventy-eight days; the six households eighty days with the figure entered out of a ladder and him asked for it on none of the ten days; the rival record thirty-nine days in force, seven readings, the eighth refused out loud with a reason, unanswered; five ninths with a sixth available and not taken; the man of about thirty at a counter, the man of about forty-one, the man with the portfolio, and a man of about forty-four with a case who went out of that gate on the nineteenth of last month with a list nobody has seen and nobody has counted; and a man of about thirty-seven who came down the eleven mile road on the last day of last month with a hand-cart and gave no name to anybody and was not asked for one.** **THE COST, IN TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-FOUR WORDS, IN A LANE BEHIND A BANK AT ABOUT HALF PAST SEVEN: Two sides came at that gate in ten days and neither of them came to take anything out of it. A market is a place where somebody asks somebody a question. That yard has not asked anybody in it a question about what they want in ten days and two people have come to it and one of them has lost the use of both hands and neither of them has been refused by it, because a yard cannot refuse anybody, and that is not a thing to be proud of at the end of ten days. I do not know what that gate is yet. I know what it is not and I have said so out loud in a lane and I am not going to be the man who writes it down as something else.** A clerk entered that a wrong count is not struck in this district and no count has been struck in this block, and that the forty-one names said out loud in that yard on the seventeenth of last month are forty people and one of them is a person twice and that figure is not struck either.

## Chapter 0291 — A Table a Man Cannot Set Up

**The eleventh of the seventh month, already hot at nine. The board at 199, the train at 515, the unentered days at 229, a hundred and ninety from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty-one days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-two days back, the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given three months back and a hundred and nine days, the whole ten-term removal ladder 21 to 12 printed out loud with its day named, and the table against that wall standing for the fifth morning.** **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAYS BEFORE ANYBODY ASKS THAT HE CANNOT SET HIS OWN TABLE UP AND HAS NOT BEEN ABLE TO SINCE THE TENTH, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM CARRIES IT ELEVEN MILES ON HIS OWN SHOULDER AND CANNOT GET IT DOWN AT THE FAR END, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN GETS UNDER THE OTHER END OF IT WITH BOTH FOREARMS BECAUSE HIS HANDS DO NOT CLOSE, AND IT COMES DOWN BETWEEN THEM. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD SAYS THAT A SIXTH MORNING IS A FIGURE NOBODY HAS ASKED FOR AND A SEVENTH IS A HABIT AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE THE MAN WHO ENTERS WHICH OF THE TWO IT WAS. THE FIGURE A CLERK ENTERS IS FIVE MORNINGS, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A FIGURE ABOUT A TABLE IS NOT A FIGURE ABOUT ANYBODY WHO STOOD AT IT. THE DIGGER PUTS HIS HAND UP AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR IN THE AFTERNOON AND THE QUESTION THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN ASKS HIM FOLLOWS IT AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST FIVE, WHICH IS THE ORDER THE REPAIR PUT THEM IN AFTER A REVIEW FOUND THE CLOCK RAN BACKWARDS. THE YARD IS THE HOTTEST PLACE ANY OF THEM STOOD IN THAT WEEK AND THE HAND-CART IS PUSHED OUT OF THE WALL INTO WHAT SHADE THERE IS.**

## Chapter 0292 — The Second Line

**The twelfth of the seventh month, close and hot. The board at 200, the train at 516, the unentered days at 230, a hundred and ninety-one from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twenty days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-three days back, and the sixth morning at that table, which is the figure nobody asked for.** **THE FOUR LINES OF PRINT COME BACK WITH FIVE ON IT. THE SECOND OF THE FIVE IS NEW: A PLACE WHERE A SHEET HAS BEEN READ OUT IS A PLACE WHERE AN OFFER IS MADE. IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN THIS VOLUME THAT A BODY HAS PRINTED A THING BECAUSE OF SOMETHING THAT HAPPENED IN THIS DISTRICT, AND NOBODY HERE ASKED FOR IT, AND THE SECOND OF THE ORIGINAL FOUR IS NOW THE THIRD OF THE SHEET AS IT STANDS AND NOBODY MOVED A LINE. THE WOMAN WHO KEEPS THE BOOKS IN THE ROOM FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THAT GATE CAME UP THE BANK, READ THE FIVE LINES FROM ABOUT NINE FEET AND SAID ONE THING NOBODY ASKED HER FOR: THAT IN NINE YEARS NOT ONE LINE HAS COME INTO THAT BECAUSE OF SOMETHING SHE DID, AND THAT THERE IS GOING TO BE ONE, AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO BE ASKED ABOUT IT. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAID HE IS NOT GOING TO TAKE THAT TABLE DOWN.**

## Chapter 0293 — The Seventh Morning

**The thirteenth of the seventh month, flat and warm. The board at 201, the train at 517, the unentered days at 231, a hundred and ninety-two from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month nineteen days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-four days back, and the seventh morning at that table.** **THE BLOCK'S SENTENCE HAPPENS AND IS HALF FINISHED. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM SAYS THAT THERE IS A FIGURE ON THAT SHEET AT THAT GATEPOST AND THAT THERE IS A FIGURE GOING TO BE AT THAT TABLE, THAT BOTH WERE ARRIVED AT BY COUNTING PEOPLE COMING TO A PLACE, THAT HE DID THE FIRST OF THOSE AT TWO IN THE MORNING ON THE NIGHT OF THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF LAST MONTH TO ABOUT NINE PEOPLE AND DID NOT KNOW ONE NAME, AND THAT HE IS GOING TO STOP THERE. HE STOPS. A CLERK ENTERED THAT HE DID NOT SAY THE REST AND THAT THE RECORD ABOUT THE HALF HE DID NOT SAY SAYS NOT ASKED. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAID HE WAS NOT GOING TO EXPLAIN A MAN'S STOPPING. A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE SAID ONE SENTENCE NOBODY ASKED HER FOR. THE PIECE OF PAPER WITH TWO LINES ON IT THAT A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE COULD NOT FINISH HAS BEEN LYING ON THE END OF THAT TABLE SINCE THE FIFTH OF THIS MONTH AND NOBODY HAS PICKED IT UP.**

## Chapter 0294 — Nobody Is Sent

**The fourteenth of the seventh month, bright, with an hour of wind in the lane. The board at 202, the train at 518, the unentered days at 232, a hundred and ninety-three from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month eighteen days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-five days back, and the eighth morning at that table.** **THE MOVEMENT OF THE BLOCK, AND IT IS NOT THE ROMANCE. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST STANDS AT THE END OF A LANE FOUR HUNDRED YARDS SHORT OF A DOOR HE DOES NOT GO THROUGH FOR ABOUT FOUR MINUTES, AND SENDS NOBODY TO THAT DOOR, AND SAYS OUT LOUD THAT SHE TOLD HIM ON THE EIGHTH OF THIS MONTH NOT TO COME AND ASK HER AGAIN THIS MONTH AND THAT IT WAS A MONTH AND NOT A THING. A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE SAYS BEFORE ANYBODY ASKS HIM THAT NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM FOR A COPY AND THAT HE CANNOT COPY IT. ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WALK UP TO THE SHEET AND READ IT TO THE END AND A MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN COUNTS THEM. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT WHO KEEPS A TALLY STOPS AT THAT GATE FOR NINE SECONDS, LOOKS AT THE SHEET FROM NINE FEET AND GOES ON UP THE LANE, AND IS ASKED NOTHING. HIS COUNTED SPEECH IS AT TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-EIGHT WORDS, RE-MEASURED AFTER A REPAIR TOOK A *NINETEEN DAYS* OUT OF IT THAT DID NOT RE-DERIVE OFF A THIRTY-ONE DAY MONTH ON THE FOURTEENTH.**

## Chapter 0295 — Two Miles The Other Way

**The fifteenth of the seventh month, hot, with no shade in the lane. The board at 203, the train at 519, the unentered days at 233, a hundred and ninety-four from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month seventeen days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-six days back, the ninth morning at that table, and the fifth of the five things this district does not have still not paid on any of the nine mornings that table has stood up on.** **HE WALKS THE TWO MILES TO A ROOM WITH NOTHING OVER THE DOOR, WHERE ABOUT NINE PEOPLE ARE AND A BENCH HAS ELEVEN NOTCHES CUT IN THE UNDERSIDE OF ITS SEAT, AND STANDS IN THE LANE OUTSIDE IT ABOUT NINE MINUTES AND DOES NOT GO IN, AND WALKS BACK, AND AT ABOUT HALF PAST TWO HE IS SICK BEHIND THE BANK ABOUT NINE FEET FROM A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING, WHO SAYS NOTHING THEN AND SAYS NOTHING AFTERWARD. HE SAYS OUT LOUD THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO BE THE MAN WHO WALKS IN AND TELLS NINE PEOPLE THEY HAVE BECOME A PLACE IN A BOOK FOUR HUNDRED MILES OFF, BECAUSE A WOMAN CARRIED A COPY UP THERE ON THE SECOND OF THIS MONTH AND NOBODY ASKED HER TO. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAID THAT ROOM IS A PLACE BECAUSE OF A THING HE WROTE.**

## Chapter 296 — Ten Mornings

**The sixteenth of the seventh month, the hottest day of that week. The board at 204, the train at 520, the unentered days at 234, a hundred and ninety-five from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month sixteen days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-seven days back, the tenth morning at that table, and the chair at eighty-four days.** **TEN IS A ROUND FIGURE AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A ROUND FIGURE IS A FIGURE ABOUT A CALCULATION AND IS NOT A FIGURE ABOUT ANYBODY. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAYS HE IS NOT GOING TO TAKE THAT TABLE DOWN, THAT A TENT IS NOT A LEAN-TO, AND THAT HE WOULD RATHER THE FIRST OF HIS FIVE LINES STAYED WRONG ON ITS FACE THAN HAVE HIM MAKE IT TRUE SIDEWAYS. A WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT IS AT THE BANK WITH TWO BUCKETS AND TWO BUCKETS WERE NOT COUNTED. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING SITS DOWN ON THE END OF THE TRESTLE TABLE IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE FOR ABOUT NINE MINUTES AND NOT IN THE CHAIR, AND SAYS SO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND IT IS NOT COUNTED. A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE SAYS SHE IS NOT GOING TO PUT A WOMAN WHO ASKS HER THAT QUESTION IN ONE OF HER FOUR EMPTY HOUSES. THE MAN WHO DIGS LOAM STANDS AT THE TOP OF THE DITCH BEHIND THE BUILDING WITH TWO DOORS FOR ABOUT A MINUTE AND DOES NOT GO DOWN INTO IT, AND THE FAR END OF THAT FIELD LOOKS THE WAY IT HAS LOOKED FOR ELEVEN DAYS, AND HE WALKS BACK UP THE LANE AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST SIX WITH HIS ARM HANGING. HE WAS NOT THE ONLY ONE WHO SAT DOWN THAT AFTERNOON.**

## Chapter 0297 — Nobody Has Been Asked

**The seventeenth of the seventh month, close, and no cooling until six. The board at 205, the train at 521, the unentered days at 235, a hundred and ninety-six from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month fifteen days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-eight days back, the eleventh morning at that table, the chair at eighty-five days.** **A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN HOLDS HIS TWO DOCUMENTS APART AND SAYS HE WILL NOT PUT THEM ON ONE PAGE. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST SAYS THE WHOLE OF THE POSITION OUT LOUD IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE: THAT THE ONLY RECORD OF THIS PLACE IS A BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY, THAT THERE IS ONE PERSON WHO CAN PUT ANYTHING IN IT, THAT SHE TOLD HIM ON THE EIGHTH OF THIS MONTH NOT TO ASK HER AGAIN THIS MONTH, THAT SHE HAS NOT BEEN ASKED, AND THAT BEFORE THE FIRST OF THE EIGHTH MONTH SOMEBODY IS GOING TO WANT A THING IN THAT BOOK AND THERE IS NOBODY BUT HER. A MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SIX SAYS HE IS NOT GOING TO TAKE IT OFF HIM. A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART SAYS AT ABOUT HALF PAST TWELVE THAT HE IS GOING TO GET SOMETHING WRONG ONE DAY AND IS NOT GOING TO BE THE MAN WHO STARTS AT THE WORD — AND THEN AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR HE GETS THE FOURTH OF THE FIVE LINES WRONG IN THAT YARD AND READS ALL FIVE AGAIN FROM THE TOP.**

## Chapter 298 — Fourteen Days

**The eighteenth of the seventh month, low and grey. The board at 206, the train at 522, the unentered days at 236, a hundred and ninety-seven from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month fourteen days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights eighty-nine days back, the twelfth morning at that table, the chair at eighty-six days.** **THIS IS THE FIRST MORNING ON WHICH THE DAY ON THAT SHEET AT THAT GATEPOST IS A FORTNIGHT OFF OR LESS, AND A CLERK COULD HAVE WRITTEN A FORTNIGHT ON THE BOARD AND WROTE FOURTEEN. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD COMES BACK INTO THAT YARD ON THE DAY HE NAMED ON THE ELEVENTH AND SAYS THE SEVENTH MORNING WAS A HABIT, THAT HE WALKED OUT AND SAT IN THE DITCH AT THE FAR END OF THAT FIELD AT ABOUT HALF PAST EIGHT AND COULD NOT GET UP THE FIRST TIME, AND THAT A HABIT AND A FIGURE ARE THE SAME THING AFTER A MONTH AND THAT HE LOOKED FOR A TEST FOR SIX DAYS AND DID NOT FIND ONE. A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A GOAT SAYS SHE WOULD CARRY A COPY UP TWO MILES AGAIN TOMORROW IF SOMEBODY WANTED IT CARRIED. A WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT AT THE BANK SAYS THE ONE THING SHE HAS SAID TWICE IN THIS DISTRICT. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING, ON THE TOP OF THE BANK AT ABOUT HALF PAST TWO, SAYS HE IS A MAN WHO READS THINGS OUT LOUD AND HAS GOT NOTHING TO READ, AND THE TWO OF THEM CAME DOWN OFF THE BANK TOGETHER AT ABOUT FIVE AND SAID NOTHING ELSE ON THE WAY DOWN.**

## Chapter 299 — A Figure About Coming

**The nineteenth of the seventh month, grey and close. The board at 207, the train at 523, the unentered days at 237, a hundred and ninety-eight from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month thirteen days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety days back, the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given three months back and a hundred and seventeen days, the thirteenth morning at that table.** **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD SAYS THAT A FORTNIGHT IS A NUMBER AND HE KNEW IT AND NINETEEN PEOPLE IN THAT YARD DID NOT, AND THAT HE DOES NOT KNOW WHICH OF THE TWO HE HAS BEEN TALKING ABOUT FOR A FORTNIGHT. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING SAYS A FIGURE ABOUT A WEEK IS ARITHMETIC WITH A MEMORY AND STOPS IN THE MIDDLE OF THE NEXT SENTENCE ON PURPOSE — THE SAME SHAPE HE STOPPED IN SIX DAYS AGO, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A SHAPE NOTICED A SECOND TIME IS NOT A FINDING. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAYS HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHAT HE IS GOING TO DO ABOUT IT. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAYS HE IS GOING TO PUT THE TENT UP AGAINST THAT WALL. A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-THREE READS THE FIVE LINES OUT ALOUD IN ABOUT NINE FEET OF LANE OUTSIDE THE OFFICE, LOSES HIS VOICE ON THE FOURTH OF THEM, FINISHES THE FIFTH, AND HAS NOTHING ABOVE A WHISPER UNTIL THE MORNING OF THE TWENTY-FIRST.**

## Chapter 0300 — The Fourteenth Morning

**The twentieth of the seventh month, white and still and warm at eight. The board at 208, the train at 524, the unentered days at 238, a hundred and ninety-nine from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month twelve days off being eleven days to the end of the seventh plus the first of the eighth, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-one days back, the pool of refusals a hundred and eighteen days, the rival record in force forty-nine days, the chair at eighty-eight days, the sixth households at ninety days, the hundred and eleventh day since anybody wrote on a board, the fourteenth morning at that table, and the whole ten-term ladder printed one last time.** **THE LEDGER IS ENTERED IN ONE HAND AND NOT AS A PARAGRAPH, AND IT NAMES EIGHT THINGS PUT IN FRONT OF THE CLERK THIS MONTH AND ENTERS ALL EIGHT AS NOT A SEVENTH OF THE INSTRUMENTS. A MAN AT THE END OF THAT TABLE ASKS THE YARD WHETHER ANYBODY HAS BEEN PAID ANYTHING FOR STANDING AT THAT TABLE ELEVEN MILES OFF, AND A WOMAN BY THE GATE SAYS OUT LOUD THAT NOBODY HAS BEEN PAID, AND A CLERK ENTERS THE TWELVE FIGURES THAT DID NOT MOVE IN TEN DAYS IN NINE LINES RATHER THAN IN THE WHOLE OF IT, BECAUSE A CLERK ENTERS A THING WHERE THE THING HAPPENED AND NINE OF THEM HAD GONE IN ON THE DAY THEY HAPPENED. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM PUTS HIS HAND UP TO THE HEIGHT OF HIS SHOULDER AT ABOUT ELEVEN AND SAYS HE CARRIED THAT TABLE OUT FOUR TIMES THIS MONTH AND CANNOT DO IT ON THE TWENTY-FIRST OR THE TWENTY-SECOND, AND HAS NOT TOLD THE MAN IT BELONGS TO. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAYS THE LAST THING OF THE TEN DAYS: HE ASKED THAT YARD FOR ITS ADDRESS ON THE FIRST AND THE YARD DID NOT SAY THE WORD, THE FIFTH OF HIS OWN FIVE LINES IS NOT TRUE AND HE WROTE IT ON PURPOSE, HE HAS NOT DESCRIBED THE ONE PERSON WHO STOOD AT THAT TABLE, THE ONLY THING THAT HAS HAPPENED IN TEN DAYS THAT NOBODY HERE ASKED FOR IS A PRINTED LINE, AND HE HAS GOT NO THIRD THING AND HE HAS STOPPED SAYING THAT AS THOUGH SAYING IT WERE A WAY OF DOING SOMETHING ABOUT IT. THE BLOCK ENDS ON A TABLE STANDING AGAINST A WALL WITH NOBODY GOING TO PUT IT UP, A BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY WITH NOBODY ASKED TO WRITE IN IT, AND TWELVE DAYS.**

## Chapter 0301 — A Man Arriving With Nothing

**The twenty-first of the seventh month, hot before seven and no wind. The board at 209, the train at 525 days, the unentered days at 239, two hundred from the second of January, the first day of the eighth month eleven days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-two days back, the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given three months back and a hundred and nineteen days, the table against that wall standing for the fifteenth morning, the chair eighty-nine days, the rival record fifty days in force, the hundred and twelfth day since anybody wrote on a board, the six households ninety-one days unmoved. A clerk entered that the length of the eighth month is not stated and was not used, and that the figure on the sheet at that gatepost is four hundred and eleven and has not moved on any of the fifty days since the first of the sixth, and that fifty is a figure about the figure and not about the sheet. A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WHO CARRIES A MESSAGE FROM ONE PLACE TO ANOTHER ON HIS OWN TWO FEET CAME THROUGH THE GATE, WHICH TOOK TWO GOES, AT ABOUT A QUARTER PAST TEN WITH NOTHING IN HIS HANDS: no case, no portfolio, no list, no seal, no paper, both hands down, about five foot nine, grey at the temples, thin across the top of the shoulders, the outside edge of the sole of his right shoe gone, the heel of his left one off in a piece about the size of a coin, and a pale print about nine inches long under every step. He said he had come nine days on his own two feet, gave no name, was not asked for one, and a clerk entered that nine is a figure about his own legs and not about a road or a distance and that nobody asked him where he started from. A woman asked him what he had come for and he said he had heard a bell and did not say what kind or how far, and a clerk entered that nobody in that yard asked him whether he had heard anything and that nobody was going to. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING DECLINED TO DRAW ANYTHING OUT OF THE TWO MEN WHO HAVE COME THROUGH THAT GATE IN A MONTH, ONE WITH A CASE AND ONE WITHOUT, AND SAID THAT A THING HE HAS NOT SEEN IS NOT A THING A MAN HAS NOT GOT. The man of about thirty-seven asked him where he was going to sleep and he said he had not worked that out. He read the sheet at the gatepost and the five lines on the end of the table from about four feet off and came no nearer to either.**

## Chapter 0302 — Three Dates

**The twenty-second of the seventh month, hotter than the day before. The board at 210, the train at 526, the unentered days at 240, two hundred and one from the second of January, the removal day ten days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-three days back, the table for the sixteenth morning, the chair ninety days, the pool a hundred and twenty days, the hundred and thirteenth day with no line on a board, the rival record fifty-one days. A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX WORKED THREE DATES OUT LOUD TO ABOUT NINE PEOPLE AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAD ASKED HIM FOR ANY OF THEM: the first day of the eighth month; the night of the twenty-fourth of last month at about two in the morning for about nine minutes, and the piece of paper with about nine names in about nine hands is a record of the people who came into a building and is not the record; and a month after the twenty-fourth of last month, which is the twenty-fourth of this month and the earliest day that bell may be pulled again. THE WORD COURIER ARRIVED ORDINARY AND WAS CHECKED: a man of about forty with a cart of his own off the lane said it about a man who takes a parcel for a shop and is paid at the end of the week, and the man of about sixty-four said that was not what he was, and said what he was, and a clerk entered that a word and a thing are two things and that a word somebody has been given is not a description of anybody. A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAID THE THIRD THING HE HAS NOT GOT IS A MAN ON A WALL AND THAT IT IS NOT A THIRD THING, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A MAN ON A WALL IS NOT A SEVENTH OF THE SIX INSTRUMENTS. A stay is counted without the day of arrival, so a man who came in on the twenty-first has been here one day on the twenty-second, and the clerk entered that.**

## Chapter 0303 — Nobody Ever Asked Him

**The twenty-third of the seventh month, grey over the loams. The board at 211, the train at 527, the unentered days at 241, two hundred and two from the second of January, the removal day nine days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-four days back, the pool a hundred and twenty-one days, the table for the seventeenth morning, the chair ninety-one days, the six households ninety-three days, the rival record fifty-two days, the hundred and fourteenth day with no line on a board. A MAN WHO CONVENES NINE SETTLEMENTS ASKED A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD, OUT LOUD IN THE ORDINARY VOICE IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, WHETHER HE WOULD GO AND DO A THING: to go out from the bell in the oak frame four miles out past the loams on his own two feet in daylight and walk outward until he could not be sure any more, and bring back the number and nothing else, and not mark anything and not count anybody and not say what he thought it meant. HE SAID THE REASON NOBODY HAD EVER ASKED HIM IS THAT NOBODY ASKED HIM, THAT HE HAS KEPT FOUR MILES OF THAT ROAD FOR ELEVEN YEARS AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED TO DO A THING ON IT, THAT IT WOULD TAKE TWO DAYS, AND THAT IF HE COULD NOT GET UP OUT OF SOMETHING OUT THERE HE WOULD SIT THERE UNTIL HE COULD. A clerk entered that a man who convenes nine settlements is not a representative of a settlement that has not asked to be represented, and that a man who keeps a road is not a bearer of that road and has never been asked to be one. HE WENT UP THE LANE AT ABOUT FOUR TO GET HIS COAT AND CAME BACK WITH IT OVER HIS ARM AND NOTHING ELSE IN HIS HANDS.**

## Chapter 304 — The Earliest Day

**The twenty-fourth of the seventh month, white and very hot. The board at 212, the train at 528, the unentered days at 242, two hundred and three from the second of January, the removal day eight days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-five days back, the pool a hundred and twenty-two days, the table for the eighteenth morning, the chair ninety-two days, the six households ninety-four days, the rival record fifty-three days, the hundred and fifteenth day with no line on a board. A clerk entered that this is the earliest day the bell may be pulled and that being the earliest day is not the same as the day and that nobody has said it is the day. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD WENT OUT OF THAT GATE AT ABOUT TEN TO FIVE AND A MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN SAW HIM GO. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING READ THE THREE THINGS A BELL HAS TO HAVE BEFORE THIS DISTRICT WILL CALL A SOUNDING SAFE, IN CHAPTER 273'S WORDS: A BELL THAT IS RUNG RECORDS EVERYBODY WHO HEARS IT FOR ONE NIGHT; A BELL RUNG WHEN THERE IS NOTHING TO ANSWER IT CANNOT BE RUNG AGAIN FOR A MONTH; AND A COMMUNITY HAS TO PROVIDE A SHELTER AND A WAY TO TAKE THE RECORD OFF. THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX ANSWERED THE QUESTION THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR ASKED HIM, WHICH WAS WHAT A MONTH WAS, AND SAID A MONTH IS WHAT THE SECOND OF THE THREE CALLS A MONTH AND A MONTH IS NOT A THING YOU CAN HAVE HALF OF, AND THAT HE HAS BEEN SAYING THAT SECOND THING OUT LOUD SINCE THE TWENTY-SECOND AND HAS NOT ONCE ASKED HIMSELF WHETHER A MONTH IS THE SAME LENGTH EVERY TIME, AND HE DOES NOT KNOW. ABOUT NINE PEOPLE WALKED OUT TO THE FRAME AT ABOUT TWO IN THE AFTERNOON AND STOOD UNDER IT FOR ABOUT FOUR MINUTES AND NOBODY PULLED IT AND NOBODY WAS ASKED WHETHER IT SHOULD BE, AND A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY PUT BOTH HANDS ON AN UPRIGHT AND PUSHED ON IT ONCE AND IT DID NOT MOVE. The third of the three is not true and a building is not a shelter and a table with four legs outside a door is not a counter until somebody asks somebody a question at it, and nobody in this district has been asked whether they would come into that building at two in the morning.**

## Chapter 0305 — The Edge of a Sound

**The twenty-fifth of the seventh month, the third hot day running. The board at 213, the train at 529, the unentered days at 243, two hundred and four from the second of January, the removal day seven days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-six days back, the pool a hundred and twenty-three days, the table for the nineteenth morning, the chair ninety-three days, the six households ninety-five days, the rival record fifty-four days, the hundred and sixteenth day with no line on a board. THE WALK, NARRATED IN TWO DAYS. He went out of the gate at about ten to five on the morning of the twenty-fourth, got out to the frame by about two in the afternoon, went outwards in daylight until the light went, and started again at about ten to six on the twenty-fifth. On the first of the two days he crossed a turning that is not on any sheet in this district and could not see the frame of oak at all when he looked back. At about eleven on the twenty-fifth he came to the far end of a field about two hundred yards across and the ditch was at the far end of it, about three feet deep, with about a foot of standing water in the bottom, AND HE WENT DOWN INTO IT because he wanted to see what was past it, and nobody watched him do it. HE COULD NOT GET UP THE FIRST TIME. He tried it twice with his left leg and once with his right, his left arm went into the water to the elbow, and he came out of the ditch on the second attempt about nine seconds after the first one, and stood on the lip of it for about a minute with his hands on his knees, and picked his coat up with his right hand and not with his left. A CLERK ENTERED THAT A MAN WHO CANNOT GET OUT OF A DITCH THE FIRST TIME HAS NOT REFUSED THE DITCH AND CANNOT BE COUNTED EITHER WAY, AND THAT THE GETTING UP THE SECOND TIME IS NOT ENTERED AS A FIRST TIME AND IS NOT ENTERED AS A SECOND TIME EITHER, AND THAT A LEG AND A BOOT AND ABOUT NINE SECONDS ARE THREE THINGS ABOUT A MAN AND ARE NOT THREE FIGURES ABOUT ANYBODY AND ARE NOT A PRICE OF ANYTHING. He walked back in from about half past eleven and came through that gate at about half past six in the morning of the twenty-sixth and said nothing to anybody. THE YARD HAD NO MAN IN IT THAT DAY AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT NOBODY ASKED HIM ANYTHING AT ALL ON THE TWENTY-FIFTH BECAUSE HE WAS NOT IN IT, AND THAT NOBODY WENT OUT AFTER HIM, AND THAT WANTING IS NOT ASKING.**

## Chapter 0306 — Eleven Miles

**The twenty-sixth of the seventh month, the fourth hot day running. The board at 214, the train at 530, the unentered days at 244, two hundred and five from the second of January, the removal day six days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-seven days back, the pool a hundred and twenty-four days, the table for the twentieth morning, the chair ninety-four days, the six households ninety-six days, the rival record fifty-five days, the hundred and seventeenth day with no line on a board. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD CAME IN THROUGH THAT GATE AT ABOUT HALF PAST SIX THAT MORNING, SAID NOTHING, AND SAT DOWN AT THE FOOT OF THE WALL, AND HE STOOD UP AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN AND SAID THE FIGURE. HE COUNTED IT FROM THE FRAME AND NOT FROM THAT GATE BECAUSE THAT IS WHERE HE STARTED, AND HE HAS NO WHEEL AND NO CORD AND NO CHAIN, AND HE COUNTED IT THE ONLY WAY A MAN ON HIS OWN TWO FEET CAN COUNT A DISTANCE, AND THE GROUND STOPPED BEING GROUND HE KNOWS SOMEWHERE AROUND THE POINT WHERE HE COULD NOT BE SURE ANY MORE, AND THE LAST PLACE HE COULD BE SURE IS ELEVEN MILES FROM THAT FRAME. HE WENT PAST THE POINT AND CARRIED ON FOR ABOUT A MILE AND A HALF TO SEE WHETHER THE NOT-BEING-SURE CAME BACK AND IT DID NOT, AND HE IS NOT GOING TO GIVE THE MILE AND A HALF A NAME. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING READ THE ELEVEN MILES OFF THE SHEET AT THE GATEPOST AND SAID, WITHOUT STOPPING IN THE MIDDLE THIS TIME, THAT ELEVEN MILES AND ELEVEN MILES IS TWO FIGURES, THAT PUTTING THE SAME LENGTH TWICE ON ONE PAGE IS NOT A SUM AND NOT A MEANING AND NOT A PATTERN, THAT HE HAS NO WAY OF PRICING THE DIFFERENCE AND NEVER HAS, AND THAT NOBODY PUT ONE OF THEM THERE. A CLERK ENTERED THAT TWO FIGURES OF THE SAME LENGTH ARE TWO FIGURES AND ARE NOT ADDED TOGETHER, AND THAT A CLERK WHO COULD HAVE MADE A FINDING OUT OF THEM AND DID NOT IS NOT A CLERK WHO HAS FOUND NOTHING, AND THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID THE WORD COINCIDENCE AND THAT THE WORD IS NOT IN THAT CHAPTER AND IS NOT IN THIS DISTRICT. A MAN of fifty-six said six words: nobody put one of them there. A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE SAID THE WORD EVEN OUT LOUD BEFORE ANYBODY ASKED HER FOR IT, and said that even is a word about a beam and that she has not had either of those two figures on her scale and is not going to say they are even because they are the same length, and is not saying they are not even either. The man who convened nine settlements said he was not going to carry eleven miles up the bank this month and the month runs out on the thirty-first.**

## Chapter 0307 — Nine Days of Nothing

**The twenty-seventh of the seventh month, grey over the loams and no cooler after eleven. The board at 215, the train at 531, the unentered days at 245, two hundred and six from the second of January, the removal day five days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-eight days back, the pool a hundred and twenty-five days, the table for the twenty-first morning, the chair ninety-five days, the six households ninety-seven days, the rival record fifty-six days, the hundred and eighteenth day with no line on a board. The man of about sixty-four had been in this district six days, a stay counted without the day he arrived on, and that six is not the nine he gave about his own walking and the two do not make fifteen. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAID THE SHAPE OF THE THIRD THING HE HAS NOT GOT, AND THE SHAPE IS A QUESTION, AND HE HAS NOT ASKED ANYBODY ONE IN THAT YARD IN THREE WEEKS AND NOBODY HAS ASKED HIM ONE, AND HE HAS NOT DESCRIBED THE ONE PERSON WHO STOOD AT THAT TABLE ELEVEN MILES OFF AND IS NOT GOING TO. A MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO DRIVES A CART ASKED THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WHETHER HE WOULD CARRY A PARCEL UP THE ELEVEN MILE ROAD TO A TURNING ABOUT A MILE SHORT OF A WALL AND TAKE IT BACK DOWN AGAIN, AND HE REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS WITH A REASON: A MAN WHO CARRIES THINGS GETS ASKED TO CARRY MORE THINGS AND HE HAS NOT GOT ROOM FOR THE ONE HE HAS GOT. A clerk entered that a refusal with a reason is not one of the refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given, and that a man who drives a cart is not a clerk of a house, and that a man who carries a message is not a keeper of it. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN SAID THE SHAPE A SECOND TIME AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A SHAPE NOTICED A SECOND TIME IS NOT A FINDING AND A THIRD TIME IS A HABIT, AND THAT A MAN WHO SAYS A THING A SECOND TIME BECAUSE IT HAS NOT BEEN SAID SINCE THE TWENTIETH OF LAST MONTH IS NOT MAKING A HABIT OF IT AND HAS NOT BEEN ASKED TO STOP.**

## Chapter 0308 — Four Days

**The twenty-eighth of the seventh month, white and hot early. The board at 216, the train at 532, the unentered days at 246, two hundred and seven from the second of January, the removal day four days off, the ninth of the nine printed nights ninety-nine days back, the pool a hundred and twenty-six days, the table for the twenty-second morning, the chair ninety-six days, the six households ninety-eight days, the rival record fifty-seven days, the hundred and nineteenth day with no line on a board. THE WHOLE OF THE LADDER OFF THE FIRST DAY OF THE EIGHTH MONTH WAS READ OUT LOUD ONCE IN THAT YARD, WITH THE DAY NAMED AGAINST EACH TERM: ELEVEN, TEN, NINE, EIGHT, SEVEN, SIX, FIVE AND FOUR, and the day after the last of them does not have a figure in front of it at all. A CLERK ENTERED THAT A LADDER IS A FIGURE ABOUT A LADDER AND IS NOT ABOUT ANYBODY, AND THAT A DAY IS NOT A PERSON. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD COULD NOT GET BACK UP THE TWO GOES OF THE BANK INTO THE LANE ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT AND GOT UP ON THE SECOND, and said out loud that he has got eleven miles in his legs from two days on foot and a ditch about three feet deep in it, that he did not fall in that ditch and got out of it, and that HIS LEG IS NOT A FIGURE, and that if a man four hundred miles off could count legs the way he counts doors he would have a number about this district by now and he has not got one, and that he is not going to walk out there again and nobody asked him to. THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT WAS AT THE BANK WITH TWO BUCKETS AND TWO BUCKETS WERE NOT COUNTED AND NOBODY COUNTED THEM, and she said she is not going to stand at a bank and count anything down to anything, and that a thing she said on the sixth of this month she said again on the eighteenth, and that was twice, and she is not going to make it three. The table against that wall stood for the twenty-second morning and nobody is going to put it up, and the man who digs loam has still not told the man it belongs to him.**

## Chapter 0309 — What He Came For

**The twenty-ninth of the seventh month, hot from before six. The board at 217, the train at 533, the unentered days at 247, two hundred and eight from the second of January, the removal day three days off, **the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred days back**, the pool a hundred and twenty-seven days, the table for the twenty-third morning, the chair ninety-seven days, the six households ninety-nine days, the rival record fifty-eight days, the hundred and twentieth day with no line on a board. A clerk entered that a hundred days is a round figure and that a round figure is a figure about a calculation and is not a figure about anybody. THE BLOCK'S TURN. A MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR STOOD UP OFF THE WALL AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN AND CAME AND STOOD AT THE END OF THAT TABLE ON HIS OWN TWO FEET, WITHOUT BEING ASKED BY ANYBODY, AND SAID FOUR HUNDRED AND THIRTY-FIVE WORDS IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE: THAT HE CAME BECAUSE HE HEARD A BELL; THAT HE WAS IN THE SOUND OF IT ON THE NIGHT OF THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF LAST MONTH AT ABOUT TWO IN THE MORNING, THAT THE BELL WAS ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED MILES FROM WHERE HE WAS STANDING, THAT IT HELD FOR ABOUT NINE MINUTES AND HE WAS IN THE SOUND OF IT FOR THE WHOLE OF THOSE NINE MINUTES, AND THAT HE DID NOT GO TO A DOOR AND DID NOT GO TO A BUILDING AND STOOD WHERE HE WAS; THAT THE PIECE OF PAPER IN THE ROOM FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY WITH ABOUT NINE NAMES ON IT IN ABOUT NINE HANDS IS A LIST OF THE PEOPLE WHO CAME IN AND HE IS NOT ON IT; THAT HE IS IN THE RECORD OF THAT NIGHT, THAT THE RECORD IS NOT A PAPER AND IS NOT IN A BOOK IN THIS DISTRICT AND CANNOT BE TAKEN OFF, AND THAT THERE IS NO OTHER ONE OF IT HE CAN GET TO; **THAT IN THE RECORD OF THAT NIGHT THERE IS A NAME, THAT THE NAME WAS TAKEN OFF A LIST BEFORE THE LONG FRACTURE, AND THAT HE WILL NOT SAY IT IN A YARD**; AND THAT HE IS A COURIER, THAT A MAN AT THE BACK OF THAT YARD USED THAT WORD ABOUT HIM ON THE TWENTY-SECOND AND MEANT A MAN WHO TAKES A PARCEL FOR A SHOP, THAT HE WAS RIGHT ABOUT THAT MAN AND WRONG ABOUT HIM, AND THAT IT IS THE SAME WORD AND NOT THE SAME THING; AND THAT HE HAS CARRIED THIS ONE THING FOR EIGHTEEN YEARS AND NEVER WRITTEN IT DOWN AND WILL REFUSE TO PUT IT DOWN WITH A REASON, AND THE REASON IS THAT A NAME IN A BOOK IS A NAME ANYBODY WITH THE BOOK CAN FIND. A CLERK ENTERED THAT A NAME IS NOT A FIGURE, THAT A FIGURE A PERSON SAYS OUT LOUD IN A YARD IS A FIGURE AND A FIGURE NOBODY SAYS OUT LOUD IS A FIGURE IN A BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY, AND THAT A MAN WHO HAS SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE HAS BEEN CARRYING SOMETHING FOR EIGHTEEN YEARS HAS NOT BEEN ASKED TO PUT IT DOWN AND CANNOT BE COUNTED EITHER WAY, AND THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD ASKED HIM WHETHER HE HAD HEARD ANYTHING AND NOBODY WAS GOING TO. Nobody said one word for about nine seconds. A man of fifty-six said he is not going to take this off him and that what was said out loud was the shape of a thing and not the thing, and that a shape is worth exactly what he has always said a shape is worth, which is nothing, and that he has been wrong every time he has thought it.**

## Chapter 0310 — The Day Before the Last Day of the Month

**The thirtieth of the seventh month, the hottest day of that month. The board at 218, the train at 534 days, the unentered days at 248, two hundred and nine from the second of January, the removal day two days off with the derivation of two printed out loud, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and one days back, the pool three months back and a hundred and twenty-eight days, the table for the twenty-fourth morning, the chair ninety-eight days, the six households a hundred days, the rival record fifty-nine days, the hundred and twenty-first day since anybody wrote on a board. A clerk entered that a stay is counted without the day a man arrived on and that the man of about sixty-four has been in this district nine days this morning, and that the nine days is the same nine he gave about his own walking and that the two are two figures about the same man and do not make eighteen. THE THREE THINGS, SAID OUT LOUD AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN AND NONE OF THEM MOVED: **THE FIGURE ON THE SHEET AT THAT GATEPOST IS FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN AND WAS FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN ON THE TWENTY-FIRST OF THIS MONTH; THE TABLE AGAINST A WALL ABOUT FOUR FEET HIGH ELEVEN MILES OFF IS STANDING FOR ITS TWENTY-FOURTH MORNING AND NOBODY IS GOING TO PUT IT UP, AND A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM PUT IT DOWN THERE FOUR TIMES THIS MONTH AND CANNOT PUT IT UP AND HAS NOT TOLD THE MAN IT BELONGS TO; AND THE BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY HAS A SHUT DOOR ON THE ROOM IT IS IN AND ONE PERSON WHO MAY WRITE IN IT AND NOBODY HAS BEEN ASKED TO WRITE IN IT AND THE RECORD ABOUT THE NOT ASKING SAYS NOT ASKED.** A clerk entered that a figure somebody says out loud in a yard is a figure, and that a table, a figure and a book are three things and are not added together. The man of fifty-six said he is not going to stand in that yard on the last morning but one of the month and tell anybody what he thinks about the third of the three things, and that two days is all he has got. The man of about twenty-three who copies for nothing read the five lines out loud in about nine feet of lane outside the office to about four people, and a woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat listened from her own gate, and nobody had asked either of them to do anything. The man of about sixty-four came back up the lane at about five and stood about four feet off the end of that table and nobody asked him anything. The man of about thirty-four who digs loam came up the lane at about six and said his arm has not gone above the shoulder on any of the thirty days of this month and that he has not told the man the table belongs to him and is not going to today either. **AND A CLERK ENTERED THE NUMBER OF DAYS WITHOUT ENTERING WHAT IS GOING TO HAPPEN IN THEM, AND NO LINE HAS BEEN PUT ON A BOARD AND NO COUNT HAS BEEN STRUCK AND NO NAME HAS BEEN WRITTEN ON ANYTHING IN THIS DISTRICT IN THE TEN DAYS OF THIS BLOCK.**

## Chapter 0311 — One

**The thirty-first of the seventh month, the last day of that month. The board at 219, the train at 535, the unentered days at 249, two hundred and ten from the second of January, the removal day one day off, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and two days back, the pool four months back and a hundred and twenty-nine days, the table for the twenty-fifth morning, the chair ninety-nine days, the six households a hundred days, the rival record sixty days, the hundred and twenty-second day with no line on a board. THE LADDER STANDS AT ONE AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT ONE IS NO DAYS TO THE END OF THE SEVENTH MONTH PLUS THE FIRST OF THE EIGHTH, THAT THE LENGTH OF THE EIGHTH MONTH IS NOT STATED IN THIS DISTRICT AND WAS NOT USED, AND THAT THE DAY AFTER THE ONE IS THE FIRST OF THE EIGHTH MONTH AND DOES NOT HAVE A FIGURE IN FRONT OF IT AT ALL. A MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SAID THE WHOLE OF THE TURNED RELATIVE MONTH OUT LOUD IN A YARD IN A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO WORDS: THAT LAST MONTH IN THAT YARD MEANT THE SIXTH AT TEN IN THE MORNING AND THE SEVENTH FROM TONIGHT, THAT THE MONTH BEFORE LAST GOES FROM THE FIFTH TO THE SIXTH, THAT THE THIRD MONTH GOES FROM THREE MONTHS BACK TO FOUR, THAT FOUR IS THE RIGHT NUMBER AND THREE IS WHAT THIS DISTRICT HAS BEEN SAYING SINCE THE FIRST OF THE SIXTH MONTH, AND THAT A CLERK IS GOING TO WRITE FOUR ON THIS PAGE AND IS NOT GOING BACK TO THE OLD PAGES TO CHANGE THEM. A CLERK WROTE THE WHOLE RULE DOWN IN ONE HAND, ON ONE LINE, WITH *LAST MONTH* AND *THE MONTH BEFORE LAST* SET OUT IN A COLUMN OF THEIR OWN. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said the whole of the ladder is one and that a ladder that runs out is not a ladder that has been broken. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR HAD BEEN IN THAT YARD TEN DAYS AND HAD SLEPT ON THAT WALL ON TEN OF THE ELEVEN NIGHTS OF THAT RUN AND HAS NOT SLEPT ON IT ON ONE OF THEM, AND NOBODY ASKED HIM ABOUT THAT ONE. A man of about forty-five who digs and fills in holes for anybody who asks him came up the lane at about six, asked one question, which was whether anybody had lost a stake, and went up the lane again.**

## Chapter 0312 — The Day

**The first of the eighth month, the day the sheet at that gatepost names. The board at 220, the train at 536, the unentered days at 250, two hundred and eleven from the second of January, THE REMOVAL DAY IS THIS MORNING AND DOES NOT HAVE A FIGURE IN FRONT OF IT AT ALL, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and three days back, the pool a hundred and thirty days, the table for the twenty-sixth morning, the chair a hundred days, the six households a hundred and second days, the rival record sixty-one days, the hundred and twenty-third day with no line on a board. THE DAY. NOTHING CAME.** The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing stood under the sheet at the gatepost at about half past ten and read the line on it about the day, and said the words *on or before* out loud twice, in two hundred and six words, and said that a sheet which says on or before a day is not complied with by that day arriving and is not un-complied with by it either, and that a word that says on or before is a word that has left a place for a person to be late in, and that there is no place in that sheet for anybody to be late in. A clerk entered that nobody was refused because nothing was asked of anybody, and that a day on which nothing arrives is not a day on which anybody has said no, and that a day passing is not an answer, and **that this district does not know whether it has been stopped or is late and that not knowing is a finding.** The man the figure of twenty-one years is against stood at that gate for about forty minutes and said in a yard that a day on which nobody asks anybody anything is not a day on which anybody has said no, and that there is a difference between those two things and it is the whole difference between a day that passes and an answer. The woman of fifty-eight came down to the bank with two buckets and said she had not asked any of the eleven houses whether they were expecting anything and was not going to this evening, and that a person can be got out of a house in half an hour if they want to be got out, and that is a thing a sheet cannot do twice. The figure on that sheet was four hundred and eleven at ten in the morning and four hundred and eleven at half past eight in the evening.**

## Chapter 0313 — What a Day Like That Does to a Yard

**The second of the eighth month. The board at 221, the train at 537, the unentered days at 251, two hundred and twelve from the second of January, the removal day one day past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and four days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-one days, the table for the twenty-seventh morning, the chair a hundred and one days, the six households a hundred and third days, the rival record sixty-two days, the hundred and twenty-fourth day with no line on a board. A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing put the five lines of print down flat on the end of that trestle table at about half past ten and READ NOTHING OUT LOUD, and about nine people stood in that yard and waited for him to start, and he said he had got nothing to read that he had not read, and a clerk entered that a man not reading a sheet aloud in a yard on one morning is not a refusal to read and did not become a tenth of anything. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM PUT HIS RIGHT HAND OUT FLAT UNDER A PIECE OF PAPER ON THE END OF THAT TABLE AND COULD NOT GET IT UP OFF THE WOOD AND TOOK IT OFF WITH THE OTHER HAND AT THE BOTTOM CORNER. The man of about twenty-three who copies for nothing got four of the five lines on clean and the fifth stopped about two thirds of the way down, and put the chalk down, and said he was not going to be the man who reads it out wrong. The man of about thirty-seven said in a hundred and sixty-six words that twenty-seven mornings is a figure about a table and not about a man who cannot set it up on his own. The man of fifty-six said in a hundred and fifty-eight words that the only thing that has changed in this district since the first of the sixth month is that a sheet has stopped having a before in front of it, and that the finding is about us and not about them, because there is nobody on that sheet to be a finding about. The chair at the end of that table was moved about four inches and put back about four inches and that is not a sitting.**

## Chapter 0314 — Sixty-Three Days

**The third of the eighth month. The board at 222, the train at 538, the unentered days at 252, two hundred and thirteen from the second of January, the removal day two days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and five days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-two days, the table for the twenty-eighth morning, the chair a hundred and two days, the six households a hundred and fourth days, the rival record sixty-three days, the hundred and twenty-fifth day with no line on a board. THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX READ OUT LOUD THAT THE RIVAL RECORD TOOK EFFECT ON THE FIRST OF THE SIXTH MONTH AND HAS BEEN IN FORCE SIXTY-THREE DAYS, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A FIGURE OF DAYS ABOUT A RECORD IS NOT A READING OF THAT RECORD AND IS NOT AN ANSWER TO IT AND DID NOT BECOME AN EIGHTH, AND THAT THE RECORD IS UNANSWERED AND THIS BLOCK DOES NOT ANSWER IT.** The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said in a hundred words that a thing that has been refused once is a thing somebody is going to try again eventually, and that the way it gets tried is by somebody saying the words *read it out* in a yard in a voice that has decided in advance, and that nobody has said those two words in that yard since the twenty-fourth of the sixth month and that he has been listening for them. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WENT DOWN THAT LANE AT EIGHT IN THE MORNING AND CAME BACK UP IT AT FOUR MINUTES PAST THREE IN THE AFTERNOON AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD ASKED HIM ONE QUESTION, AND A CLERK ENTERED WHERE HE HAD BEEN OUT OF WHAT THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A GOAT SAID AT HER OWN GATE: HE STOOD AT THE TABLE WITH FOUR LEGS AGAINST THE OUTSIDE WALL OF THE BUILDING WITH TWO DOORS FOR ABOUT TWENTY MINUTES AND DID NOT LIFT THE CURTAIN AND DID NOT TOUCH THE HASP, AND HE STOOD AT THE TOP OF THE DITCH BEHIND IT FOR ABOUT NINE MINUTES AND DID NOT GO DOWN INTO IT.**

## Chapter 0315 — How Many People Are in the Yard

**The fourth of the eighth month. The board at 223, the train at 539, the unentered days at 253, two hundred and fourteen from the second of January, the removal day three days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and six days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-three days, the table for the twenty-ninth morning, the chair a hundred and three days, the six households a hundred and fifth days, the rival record sixty-four days, the hundred and twenty-sixth day with no line on a board. A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SIX WHO KEEPS A SCALE CAME UP THE LANE AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN WITH THE BLANKET OF THE SCALE STILL FOLDED OVER HER ARM, WHICH SHE DOES NOT DO, AND ASKED HOW MANY PEOPLE WERE IN THAT YARD.** The man of about nineteen who counts took the whole of that yard in without a piece of paper or a chalk and said it out loud, in a hundred and twenty-nine words, and did it again about four minutes later, and got nineteen both times. A clerk entered that a head counted in a yard in daylight is a figure about a yard and is not a figure about any of the nineteen people in it, and that the same is true of a head counted at a gate, and that a head counted at a gate is a figure about a gate. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said in a hundred and ninety-one words that a head that is moving is not a head and that a count of a yard in daylight is a count of a yard in daylight and is not the people in it. The scale keeper then said two more things: that nineteen and about nine are two figures and do not make twenty-eight and nobody in that yard is a twenty-eighth anything; and that her four empty houses are not going to be offered again before the first day of the season after this one on her own statement, and that the word for what is in those four houses is stock, and that a stock is a word about what a person has and not a word about what a person is. A clerk entered that this is a third word out of her and is neither *rate* nor *even*, and that a house that stands empty is not a sixth of the five protected things.**

## Chapter 0316 — No

**The fifth of the eighth month. The board at 224, the train at 540, the unentered days at 254, two hundred and fifteen from the second of January, the removal day four days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and seven days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-four days, THE TABLE FOR ITS THIRTIETH MORNING, WHICH A CLERK ENTERED AS A FIGURE NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAS ASKED FOR, the chair a hundred and four days, the six households a hundred and sixth days, the rival record sixty-five days, the hundred and twenty-seventh day with no line on a board. THE FIRST APPEARANCE OF THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO KEEPS A ROUTE FOR PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT IN ANY BOOK SINCE THE NINTH OF THE SEVENTH MONTH OF VOLUME 06, WHICH IS TWENTY-SEVEN DAYS BACK THIS MORNING.** She came up that lane on her own two feet and nobody went with her and nobody met her at the bottom of it, and came in by a gap in the wall about four feet wide because she did not go up the two goes of the bank in front of that yard, and a clerk entered that nobody in that yard knew she was on the road and nobody sent anybody to see. The man the figure of twenty-one years is against asked her one question in front of about nineteen people, which was whether she would join that yard to a charter, and a clerk entered that a word in a man's mouth is not a document and that no charter of any kind was in that yard and none was read out of it and none was described to anybody. SHE SAID NO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS WITH A REASON OF HER OWN, IN A HUNDRED AND FORTY-SIX WORDS: I KEEP A ROAD FOR PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT IN ANY BOOK AND NOBODY IN THIS YARD HAS ASKED ME ONE QUESTION ABOUT IT IN ELEVEN YEARS. A clerk entered that a refusal with a reason is not one of the refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given, and that this refusal is not a tenth refusal of eleven coppers a week and is not a ninth refusal with no reason and is not an eighth about the ninth holding. The man of about thirty-seven tried to slide the five lines of print nine inches along the wood to clear the end of the table and could not do it with either hand and did it with his right forearm.**

## Chapter 0317 — Four Seconds Again

**The sixth of the eighth month. The board at 225, the train at 541, the unentered days at 255, two hundred and sixteen from the second of January, the removal day five days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and eight days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-five days, THE TABLE FOR ITS THIRTY-FIRST MORNING AND NOBODY RESOLVES IT, the chair a hundred and five days, the six households a hundred and seventh days, the rival record sixty-six days, the hundred and twenty-eighth day with no line on a board. THE SAME WOMAN REFUSED A SECOND THING IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS WITH A REASON OF HER OWN**, this time asked by the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, and the reason was that a thing said out loud in a yard is said out loud in a yard and a room is a different thing, and that she has not kept anything in a room in eleven years and is not going to start, and that the first thing anybody could say about her after that is that she had started keeping a list, and they would be right. A clerk entered that the two refusals are of two different things from two different people with two different reasons, and that neither was asked for twice and neither was taken back. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST SAID OUT LOUD, IN A HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINE WORDS, THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK HER A THIRD QUESTION, THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK ANYBODY ELSE TO ASK HER ONE ON HIS BEHALF, THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO GO ROUND HER, AND THAT HE SENT NOBODY, and a clerk entered that the man of about thirty-four who keeps a road, the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart and the man of about twenty-three who copies for nothing were not sent to anybody's door by anybody in that yard. THE AFTERNOON WAS ENTERED AS WHAT A YARD DOES WITH A REFUSAL: A YARD IS A ROOM WITH NO ROOF, A ROOM WITH NO ROOF IS A PLACE WHERE A THING GETS SAID OUT LOUD AND NOTHING ELSE, IT WENT ON HOLDING ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE FOR THE REST OF THE DAY, NOBODY LEFT BECAUSE OF THE NO AND NOBODY STAYED BECAUSE OF IT, AND NOBODY WAS GIVEN A SENTENCE THAT SETTLED ANYTHING.**

## Chapter 0318 — A Word Over a Line

**The seventh of the eighth month. The board at 226, the train at 542, the unentered days at 256, two hundred and seventeen from the second of January, the removal day six days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and nine days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-six days, the table for the thirty-second morning, the chair a hundred and six days, the six households a hundred and eighth days, the rival record sixty-seven days, the hundred and twenty-ninth day with no line on a board. THE SECOND LINE OF PRINT, AND IT CAME OFF A STAKE.** A man of about forty-five who digs and fills in holes for anybody who asks him took the top of a stake out of the soft ground by that gatepost at about six because it was standing at an angle, and a folded sheet of print came away with it, and he carried it up that lane in his left hand and had not read it. A clerk entered that whoever folded that sheet over a stake in that gatepost is not in this district, has never been asked about, is not on the sheet and has never been on any sheet in this district, and that a nail in a gatepost is not a keeper and whoever drove one in is not on the sheet. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO DIGS LOAM ASKED FOR IT TO BE READ OUT ALOUD IN DAYLIGHT IN THAT YARD, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING READ IT OUT ONCE AND IT IS FIVE LINES OF PRINT WITH A RULED LINE UNDER THE THIRD OF THEM: THAT THE FIGURE OF THE PEOPLE IN THE GROUND IN THE SHADOW OF A BUILDING ELEVEN MILES OFF IS TO BE COMPLETED, THAT IT IS TO BE COMPLETED IN THE SECOND BOOK OF THE ROOM FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THE GATE AND IS NOT TO BE ENTERED TWICE, THE WORD *COMPLETED BY* AND NOTHING UNDER IT, AND THAT A FIGURE THAT IS TO BE COMPLETED IS NOT A RATE AND THE FIGURE ON A SHEET AT A GATEPOST IS NOT TO BE ENTERED AGAIN. The man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart read all five of those lines back from the top and got nothing wrong. A clerk entered that the sheet names no person, no body, no place, no signature and no seal, that a word over a line with nothing on the line is a column of a thing and is not a column of people, that a document which names a job that does not exist has found nobody, and that this sheet is not a fourth of the three documents this district does not own. The man of about thirty-four who digs loam said in a hundred and fifty-one words that a line like that is a line any one of them could put a name on with the piece of chalk tied to the leg of that board and that he is not going to be the one.**

## Chapter 0319 — The Fifth of the Same Shape

**The eighth of the eighth month. The board at 227, the train at 543, the unentered days at 257, two hundred and eighteen from the second of January, the removal day seven days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and ten days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-seven days, the table for the thirty-third morning, the chair a hundred and seven days, the six households a hundred and ninth days, the rival record sixty-eight days, the hundred and thirtieth day with no line on a board. A CLERK ENTERED THE FIVE SHAPES OF A THING IN THIS DISTRICT, AND IT IS THE FIRST TIME IN VOLUME 07 THAT THE THIRD COLUMN OF THE COVENANT, THE NINTH CHARTER'S LINE AND THE NINTH PLACE ON THE PAGE OUT OF THE CAVE HAVE BEEN NAMED ON THE PAGE: ONE, THE PRINTED SHEET NAILED TO A GATEPOST, WHICH CARRIES A WORD OVER A COLUMN AND HAS NOTHING UNDER IT; TWO, THE THIRD COLUMN OF A COVENANT IN A BOOK A STRANGER MAY WALK UP TO, RULED AND EMPTY; THREE, A LINE UNDER A HEADING ON THE NINTH OF THE NINE CHARTERS IN NINE ROOMS, WITH NOBODY PUT UNDER IT; FOUR, THE NINTH PLACE ON A PAGE THAT CAME OUT OF A CAVE, RULED AND EMPTY; FIVE, THE SHEET THAT CAME OFF A STAKE YESTERDAY MORNING. A clerk entered that the five are the same shape of thing, that none of the five is a person, that the five are not added together, and that two of them were ruled and left empty in this district and are still empty and neither has been filled on any day since the tenth of the sixth month. THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX SAID THAT HE HAS NEVER ONCE COUNTED THE SHAPES AND THAT HE HAS GOT FIVE SHAPES AND NOT GOT AN ARGUMENT. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING SAID THAT THE SECOND LINE OF THAT SHEET NAMES A BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS FROM THAT GATE, THAT THERE IS ONE PERSON IN THIS BASIN WHO MAY WRITE IN IT, THAT HE HAS NOT ASKED HER ANYTHING ABOUT IT AND IS NOT GOING TO, AND THAT ON THE TWELFTH OF THE SEVENTH MONTH SHE SAID OUT LOUD IN A LANE THAT SHE WAS NOT GOING TO BE ASKED ABOUT IT, AND HE WAS IN THAT LANE. At about half past twelve, in about four words of speech and a hundred and twenty in all, he said he has walked up that bank about nine times in a month and down it about nine times and has not knocked on that door in any of them, and that there is a sheet of print on the end of that table which names that book and he has read it out loud and has not handed it to her and is not going to, and that he would like that entered as a thing a man did not do and not as a thing a man did.**

## Chapter 0320 — Nothing

**The ninth of the eighth month. The board at 228, the train at 544, the unentered days at 258, two hundred and nineteen from the second of January, the removal day eight days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and eleven days back, the pool a hundred and thirty-eight days, the table for the thirty-fourth morning, the chair a hundred and eight days, the six households a hundred and tenth days, the rival record sixty-nine days, the hundred and thirty-first day since anybody wrote on a board. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR TOOK THE SHEET OF FIVE LINES OF PRINT OFF THE END OF THAT TRESTLE TABLE AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, WITHOUT ASKING ANYBODY FOR IT, AND CARRIED IT FOUR HUNDRED YARDS UP A BANK AND LAID IT FLAT ON THE TOP STEP OF TWO STONES OUTSIDE A DOOR WITH A SHUT DOOR ON IT AND DID NOT KNOCK.** It took him about nine minutes to get up the two goes of that bank, where a man of about thirty-four walks it in about four, and he stopped at the top of the first go for about a minute and a half with his hand on his knee and at the top of the second for about a minute, and stood on that step with the sheet under his arm for about four minutes without putting a hand on the handle, and crouched and squared the sheet with the edge of a boot, and it took him about eleven minutes to get back down. A clerk entered that a sheet of print laid on the top step of two stones outside a door with a shut door on it is not in a book, that a stranger may walk up to that step and read it, that nobody in this district has been asked whether anybody will, and that he did not knock and the record about the not knocking says not asked. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said in a hundred and sixty-two words that nobody in that yard has asked that man one question about where he has been and that he is not going to be the first, and that a leg is not a figure. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING SAID THAT A SHEET OF PRINT THAT HAS BEEN ON THE END OF THAT TABLE SINCE THE TWELFTH OF LAST MONTH WENT UP A BANK ON A MAN'S OWN TWO FEET AND IS LYING ON A STEP FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY, AND THAT A STRANGER CAN WALK UP TO THAT STEP AND READ ALL FIVE OF THE LINES OFF IT, AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO FOLLOW HIM UP THAT BANK. At about half past four the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart read the five lines out loud in about nine feet of lane outside that room to about four people from the step, and a woman of about thirty-four who keeps a goat walked down, listened to the whole of it, and asked him one question about the third line. **AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A QUESTION ASKED IN A LANE ABOUT A LINE OF PRINT IS NOT A QUESTION ASKED IN A YARD ABOUT WHAT A PERSON WANTS, AND THAT NOBODY ASKED THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WHETHER HE HAD HEARD EITHER OF THEM READ. THE FIGURE ON THE SHEET AT THAT GATEPOST WAS STILL FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN, THE TABLE ELEVEN MILES OFF WAS STILL STANDING AGAINST A WALL ABOUT FOUR FEET HIGH, AND THE BOOK FOUR HUNDRED YARDS AWAY STILL HAD A SHUT DOOR ON THE ROOM IT IS IN AND NOBODY HAD BEEN ASKED TO WRITE IN IT, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A DISTRICT WHICH HAS LOOKED AT THE SAME THREE THINGS FOR TEN DAYS AND CHANGED NONE OF THEM HAS FOUND OUT WHAT A DAY IS WORTH, AND THAT IS NOT NOTHING.**

## Chapter 0321 — The Charter Is Drawn

**The tenth of the eighth month, nine days past the removal day. The board at 229, the train at 545, the unentered days at 259, two hundred and twenty from the second of January, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and twelve days back, the pool four months back and a hundred and thirty-nine days, the table for the thirty-fifth morning, THE CHAIR A HUNDRED AND NINE DAYS AND NOBODY HAD SAT IN IT ON ANY OF THEM, the six households a hundred and eleventh days, the rival record seventy days, the hundred and thirty-second day since anybody wrote on a board. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR HAD BEEN IN THIS DISTRICT TWENTY DAYS AND SLEPT ON THAT WALL ON TWENTY OF THE TWENTY-ONE NIGHTS OF THAT RUN AND HAS NOT SLEPT ON IT ON ONE OF THEM, AND NOBODY ASKED HIM ABOUT THE ONE. A clerk of nineteen years entered that the ladder off the first day of the eighth month has no terms in front of it this morning and is not going to get any, and that the length of the eighth month is not stated in this district and was not used this morning. THE MAN THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-ONE YEARS IS AGAINST SAID IN A YARD THAT HE WAS GOING TO WRITE A CHARTER FOR THE BUILDING WITH TWO DOORS, AND A CLERK ENTERED THAT A WORD IN A MAN'S MOUTH IS NOT A DOCUMENT.** THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING ASKED HIM ONE QUESTION ABOUT IT IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE, IN A HUNDRED AND FIVE WORDS, AND HE STOOD ABOUT FOUR FEET OFF THE END OF THAT TABLE AND DID NOT ANSWER FOR ABOUT ELEVEN SECONDS AND THEN SAID THAT HE DID NOT KNOW, AND THAT WAS THE ANSWER. He carried the chair from the end of that table up the lane into the building, sat in it at about a quarter past eleven, ruled a line with a straight edge and a stub of chalk, and wrote eight lines in one hand between about half past eleven and about two, and said out loud at about two, in a hundred and thirty-three words, that he had been wrong about the document since the moment he started it, and that stopping a removal is not the same as working, and that he has nobody to ask whether it works and is not going to go and find one. **THE ROLL IS EIGHT LINES AND A COLUMN HEADED NOT ASKED WITH NOTHING UNDER IT, AND IT SAYS NOTHING ABOUT HOW A PERSON LEAVES IT, BECAUSE A PERSON LEAVES IT WHEN THEY GO.** A clerk entered that the old charter four hundred yards away is not corrected, not replaced, not superseded and not taken out, and that the fifth line of it is still wrong on its face, and that a wrong count is not struck by writing a correct one beside it. **HE COULD NOT HOLD THE PEN UPRIGHT IN HIS RIGHT HAND AFTER ABOUT HALF PAST TWO AND RULED THE SECOND LINE WITH THE STRAIGHT EDGE BRACED AGAINST HIS FOREARM AND GOT IT A SIXTEENTH OF AN INCH OUT OF TRUE AND LEFT IT, AND CARRIED THE CHAIR OUT OF THE BUILDING AND DOWN THE BANK IN ABOUT ELEVEN MINUTES, AND THE SHEET OF PRINT IS ON THE BENCH IN THAT BUILDING.**

## Chapter 0322 — A Table With Four Legs

**The eleventh of the eighth month, ten days past. The board at 230, the train at 546, the unentered days at 260, two hundred and twenty-one from the second of January, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and thirteen days back, the pool four months back and a hundred and forty days, the table for the thirty-sixth morning, THE CHAIR WAS SAT IN ONCE YESTERDAY AND HAD STOOD A HUNDRED AND NINE DAYS BEFORE THAT, the six households a hundred and twelfth days, the rival record seventy-one days, the hundred and thirty-third day since anybody wrote on a board. THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR HAD BEEN IN THIS DISTRICT TWENTY-ONE DAYS AND SLEPT ON THAT WALL ON TWENTY-ONE OF THE TWENTY-TWO NIGHTS OF THAT RUN. THE TABLE WITH FOUR LEGS AGAINST THE OUTSIDE WALL OF THAT BUILDING BECAME THE PLACE A QUESTION WAS ASKED AT, AND IT IS A COUNTER FROM THE MOMENT SOMEBODY ASKS SOMEBODY A QUESTION AT IT AND WAS NOT A COUNTER BEFORE THAT.** The man the figure of twenty-one years is against asked a man about thirty-eight who cuts reeds out of the ditch behind that building whether he would come into that building at two in the morning if it went off. **HE SAID NO IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS WITH A REASON OF HIS OWN, IN A HUNDRED AND FORTY-SEVEN WORDS: HE HAS NO BOOTS THAT WILL GO OVER THAT DITCH IN THE DARK, IT IS ABOUT THREE FEET DEEP WITH ABOUT A FOOT OF STANDING WATER IN IT, YOU HAVE NOT ASKED ME TO COME, AND THAT IS A SHED WITH A DOOR ON IT AND IT IS A GOOD SHED.** The man who asked wanted the yes and caught himself wanting it about two seconds after it had not come, and a clerk entered that a man who has wanted an answer and got one is not a man who was represented by the answer.
