## Chapters 0341 to 0350 — the craft-repair note on the summaries above, and the two words of measure that moved

**THE TEN SUMMARIES ABOVE WERE WRITTEN AGAINST THE TEXT AS IT FIRST STOOD. THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW-FIX PASS THEN REWROTE THE PROSE OF ALL TEN CHAPTERS AROUND THE SPEECHES, TURNING LEDGER TEXT BACK INTO SCENE AND BREAKING REPEATED CONSTRUCTIONS. EVERY PLOT BEAT, DOCUMENT, SPEECH, DIALOGUE LINE, DATE AND LADDER FIGURE IN ALL TEN CHAPTERS AND ALL TEN SUMMARIES ABOVE IS UNCHANGED, AND EVERY FIGURE IN EVERY SUMMARY ABOVE STILL REPRODUCES AGAINST THE REPAIRED TEXT. THE FULL BEFORE-AND-AFTER ACCOUNT IS AT `state/volume-08-batch-0001-summary.md` SECTION 12.**

**WHAT THE REPAIR CHANGED, AND NO WRITER HAS TO CHASE ANY OF IT:**

- **NO CHAPTER'S DATE MOVED AND NO FIGURE ON THE LADDER MOVED.** The block still runs the thirtieth of the eighth month to the ninth of the ninth month, one chapter a day, the board at 249 rising to 258, the train at 565 rising to 574, the figure on the sheet at that gatepost four hundred and eleven on all ten days, the rival record at 90 rising to 99, and the man of about sixty-four forty days in the district rising to forty-nine.
- **NOT ONE SPEECH WAS TOUCHED.** All twenty-two class-one counting claims re-verify against the repaired files, measured by splitting each bold-wrapped speech on whitespace: 341 195/195 and 139/139; 342 127/127, 203/203 and 152/152; 343 174/174 and 150/150; 344 176/176 and 161/161; 345 217/217, 176/176 and 188/188; 346 179/179 and 140/140; 347 154/154 and 170/170; 348 220/220 and 146/146; 349 173/173 and 126/126; 350 217/217 and 156/156.
- **THE LEDGER SHARE FELL FROM 22.4 PER CENT OF PARAGRAPHS TO 8.4, AND FROM 31.1 PER CENT OF WORDS TO 8.1.** Repeated twelve-word runs inside a single chapter went from 304 to zero. Identical paragraphs of twelve words or more are still zero. The blocks are 24,917 words by `wc -w` on a repository denominator of 25,162, against 24,365 and 24,236 before.

**ONE MEASURE IN ONE CHAPTER IS NOT WHAT THE FIRST TEXT OF IT SAID, AND IT IS NOW CANON: `chapter-0348.md` PRINTED *LOOKED DOWN NINE FEET OF STANDING WATER* OF THE DITCH BEHIND THAT BUILDING, AND NOW PRINTS *LOOKED DOWN AT THE FOOT OF STANDING WATER IN IT*. THE DITCH IS ABOUT THREE FEET DEEP AND HAS CARRIED ABOUT A FOOT OF STANDING WATER IN EVERY OTHER CHAPTER THAT NAMES IT, AND NINE FEET IS THE HEIGHT OF THE BRICK.**

**ONE SEQUENCE INSIDE ONE CHAPTER WAS CORRECTED AND IT IS A CLOCK, NOT A PLOT: in `chapter-0347.md` the reading of the pair of terms at about ten past twelve now stands before the question asked at about two, which is the order the day runs in. The question, the nineteen people who do not answer it, the hour of a carting day that goes out of the lane late, and the rival record at ninety-six days are all exactly where the canon contract puts them.**

**AND TWO NEW BEATS EXIST THAT THE SUMMARIES ABOVE DO NOT MENTION BECAUSE THEY ARE NOT PLOT: the woman of fifty-eight has about six and a half thousand bucketfuls on the page and carries two buckets up two goes of a bank herself on the last day with nobody offering to take one; and a clerk of nineteen years wipes a hand-print out of the dust on a shelf with her sleeve, and the wipe is on no page and was entered by nobody.**

## Chapters 0351 to 0360 — Volume 08 Batch 0002, the tenth to the nineteenth of the ninth month

**351. The tenth of the ninth, thirty-nine days past the removal day.** The board at 259, the train at 575, the days nobody has entered at 289, two hundred and fifty days from the second of January, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and forty-two days back, the table eleven miles off for its sixty-fifth morning, the rival record in force a hundred days. A BID HAD BEEN OPEN IN THAT YARD FOR NINE DAYS AND WAS NOT RUN ON ANY OF THEM. THE FIGURE: the man who convened the bid described the same lot a second time in that yard and then, instead of stopping, which is the thing a man cannot teach himself to do, said a figure aloud about it — about six and a half thousand bucketfuls of water, arrived at from nine years of a woman carrying two buckets a day into that building — and said out loud that it is a figure about the lot and not about anybody in that yard and that he did not ask her for it. THE COST: she was in that yard with a full bucket in each hand, she put them down one after the other, and she said nothing. The term's figure came off the reading and it is a month, and a clerk entered the day at the end of that term out of a ladder and did not say it out loud. Nobody read it back and a thing read back by nobody binds nobody. The fifth of the five is on the page and still not paid.

**352. The eleventh of the ninth, forty days past.** The board at 260, the train at 576, the unentered at 290, the second of January at 251, a hundred and sixty-third day with no line on a board. THE TOLL: the man of fifty-six said eleven coppers a week out loud as what his own fence has come to, and a clerk entered that a price a man named out loud is not a rate and that no offer was made on that figure and none refused. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said a toll is a thing a holder pays under a term, and there is no holder in that yard, so there is no toll, and a figure left with a space for it is not a rate. THE COST: the man who convened the bid could not hold a page down in the wind, his thumb would not go down past the first joint, and he said out loud in front of about nineteen people that he could not hold it and that he was sorry about it, and the mender put his own hand over the corner of it and neither of them said one word. THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT asked a third question in nineteen years, about whether a toll would be for keeping that building or for being in it, and said it was about a bucket, and nobody answered her, and she carried the buckets up the bank herself. The man of about nineteen asked the clerk one question out loud. The fourth line of the offer is still not read out loud.

**353. The twelfth of the ninth, forty-one days past.** The board at 261, the train at 577, the pool a hundred and seventy-one days and five months back. THE PRICE OF A NAME: the man of fifty-six said out loud, in the ordinary voice, that he was going to put a figure on what the name of a person in a list of hearers is worth to somebody four hundred miles off holding a count of the people who answered a door, and he said it was worth exactly as much as the paper. A woman of fifty-eight said no in about four seconds with a reason of her own, which was that you cannot get it back off afterwards. THE COST: she picked the buckets up before she had finished the sentence, and the man of fifty-six then asked for the figure to be struck out of what the boy had and the boy said he could not promise it, and the man of fifty-six said the other half of it to nobody in particular, and it is the only thing he said in ten days that nobody counted. The eight refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given did not move and are still eight, and neither this refusal nor his figure is a ninth of anything.

**354. The thirteenth of the ninth, forty-two days past. THE PANEL.** The board at 262, the train at 578, the unentered at 292, the rival record a hundred and three days. He went up the bank at about two and did not knock, and the clerk of nineteen years was in the room, and the woman who keeps those two books was not in it and he did not ask. His right hand would not go under the edge of a book and she took it off the shelf for him. Then a rule came into the room and said, unasked, that a use ends when its term ends and that a person who held it may not hold it again without a new reading. He read it twice and it did not change, and the clerk had her eyes on a page the whole time and had not seen it come. He argued with it out loud in that yard at about a quarter to four in front of about nineteen people, that a rule which says a thing only holds while somebody reads it again has handed the whole of it to a man who cannot keep it, and he lost, and the rule did not answer him. A clerk entered that a rule is not a document and is not a seventh of the six instruments, and that a man who has argued with one has not refused it and cannot be counted either way. Nobody wrote it down.

**355. The fourteenth of the ninth, forty-three days past. THE HAND.** The board at 263, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and forty-six days back, the table eleven miles off for its sixty-ninth morning, the man of about sixty-four asleep on that low wall on fifty-three of the fifty-four nights of that run. THE COST OF THE VOLUME, IN A BODY: the reading went back to the lot with about nine people, and the man who convened the bid put his right hand on the edge of the plank door at the height of the nail and it did not close, his fingers went about halfway round the wood and stopped, and he stood there in daylight for about four seconds while nobody in that lane said a word, and he tried it again and the door did not move and his hand came off and left the print of four fingers in the grit. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing took the door off its hasp with one hand and held it against the wind for about four minutes and said the inside of that lot out loud, and he had not been asked to read anything out and nobody asked him anything. The column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud is ruled and still empty and nothing went in it.

**356. The fifteenth of the ninth, forty-four days past. THE MONTH.** The board at 264, the train at 580, the rival record a hundred and five days, the six households of the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally a hundred and forty-six days, entered out of a ladder and not asked for. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing asked in front of about nineteen people how long the ninth month is, and said he was not going to count that board. The clerk of nineteen years said thirty-one and named the day she counted from and the day she counted to and said out loud that she was entering it and not asserting it, and refused to say it again, and the man of fifty-six asked her to write it on the end of that trestle table in a line he could read off in a yard. The month after this one was entered at thirty, taken off the month before it, and she said out loud that she is not going to check a month that has not started. The mender counted thirty-one himself on the edge of the table with one hand, in about nine minutes, and got the same figure, and a clerk entered that two men counting the same figure is not a finding. NOBODY PUT A LINE ON A BOARD.

**357. The sixteenth of the ninth, forty-five days past. THE FIVE THINGS.** The board at 265, the unentered at 295, the pool a hundred and seventy-five days, the second of January at two hundred and fifty-six. There were four figures on the end of that trestle table and the fourth was in a hand about half the size of the other three. The man who convened the bid said out loud that a document that sets a thing out has five things in it, said the lot and the term, said that the third of the five is a toll and a toll cannot be said because there is no holder, and then stopped, and said out loud that he was not going to say the fourth, which is a person, or the fifth, which is a remedy, and that he would rather be a man who stopped than a man who read a thing out that is not there. THE COST: he asked the clerk afterwards what a clerk writes under a reading that stopped, said he did not want the word written down, and she refused him out loud in front of about nineteen people and wrote it on the page anyway, because a page is not a yard. The pair of terms was carried four feet and put back down and neither of them was read out.

**358. The seventeenth of the ninth, forty-six days past.** The board at 266, the train at 582, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and forty-nine days back. The man of fifty-six was not in that yard at ten for the first time in that run and about nine people worked that out inside four minutes without saying anything. The man of about thirty-four who digs loam read the four figures off the end of that trestle table out loud on his own, took about four minutes over them, lost his place on the fourth and got it the same both times, and said out loud that he was not going to point at them because his right arm does not go above the level of that shoulder and has not since the first of the seventh month. THE COST: the man of fifty-six came in at eleven and stood about nine feet off the end of that table for about a quarter of an hour and said nothing, and stood with his back to the boards for about nine minutes in the afternoon, and read the four again at about one, and about four people said in that yard that the man who digs loam could do it every morning, and he was not in that yard, having gone up the lane to the loam before one, and the man of fifty-six said nothing at all about any of the four of them saying it, and a clerk entered that he was not there to hear it. **THE REVIEW-FIX PASS AT `state/volume-08-batch-0002-summary.md` SECTION 5.2 ITEM 1 MADE THAT CHANGE, AND THE TEXT IT REPLACED HAD HIM ANSWERING FOUR PEOPLE IN A YARD HE HAD LEFT BEFORE ONE O'CLOCK.** Getting a figure right is not a finding and not a habit and not a figure about the man who got it right, and getting one figure right once was entered on the twenty-ninth of the eighth as not a finding either. Nobody was appointed to anything and no column was filled.

**359. The eighteenth of the ninth, forty-seven days past.** The board at 267, the unentered at 297, the second of January at two hundred and fifty-eight, the hundred and seventieth day with no line on a board. The bid was opened on the first of the ninth and has been open seventeen days and nobody proposed anything about closing it. The fifth of the five things this district does not have was said out loud for the third time in a fortnight and is still not paid. Four men said four different things out loud about what four figures on a table are for and nobody agreed with anybody. THE ROAD: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a road, with his boots black to the ankle, said one thing out loud in about two hundred and twenty-seven words, that a road is not for the people who know it, that a road is for somebody who has not been on it, that knowing where a road goes is not holding it, and that if a document came along with his name on it for that road he would tear it up. Four people said he could read the figures every morning and he said no four times in about four seconds each. THE COST: the man of about thirty-four who digs loam, at the top of that ditch and not at the table, said out loud that a rule nobody can be made to keep is a thing one man is carrying, and named the nine feet of wire the mender bent for nobody, and was not asked whether it was true.

**360. The nineteenth of the ninth, forty-eight days past. THE LAST DAY OF THE BLOCK.** The board at 268, the train at 584, the rival record a hundred and nine days and unanswered, the table eleven miles off for its seventy-fourth morning, the man of about sixty-four on the fifty-eight of the fifty-nine nights. THE THING HE IS AFRAID OF: he said sixty-one days out loud in that yard for the second time and did not count it, and gave the reason out loud, that he counted it on the fourth of this month and got the same figure and a figure that reproduces is a figure about a count and is not a finding and he has learned nothing from the second one, and that a figure about how often a body prints is a figure about a habit and not about the body. THE COST, A QUESTION: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a road waited until about ten past twelve and asked, out loud, in front of about nineteen people, whether anybody was going to be able to sleep in that building this winter, and the man who convened the bid answered that he did not know and gave three reasons and said out loud that a person who has given a speech instead of an answer has not answered it, and at about half past three he went up the lane and stood in the doorway of that building for about nine minutes and did not go in. The man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart was not in that yard at all and nobody said what for. THE BID IS STILL OPEN AND THE READING OF THAT LOT IS STILL BEGUN AND NOT FINISHED, AND THE FIGURE ON THE SHEET AT THAT GATEPOST WAS STILL FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN.

---

## Chapters 0361 to 0370 — Volume 08 Batch 0003, the twentieth to the twenty-ninth of the ninth month

**361. The twentieth of the ninth, forty-nine days past the removal day, and the day the block opens.** The board at 269, the train at 585, the rival record in force a hundred and ten days and unanswered, the table eleven miles off for its seventy-fifth morning, the man of about sixty-four on his sixtieth night of that run and sixty days in this district. THE REVERSAL: a cart came up that lane at about six and stood at the head of it, and at about half past ten the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said, in two hundred and fifty words and in front of about nineteen people, that a reading is a thing a man says and a second person reads back from the top, that there is one person in this district who has ever read a thing back from the top, that he is gone four days in a week, that on those four days nothing in this district binds, and that a thing which does not bind on four days in seven is a thing that has been agreed to and not kept and an agreement nobody can be made to keep is not a term. THE COST: a loaded cart stood at the head of that lane for the best part of three hours, because a man will not drive a cart through about nineteen people standing in the mud, and nobody in that yard paid for those three hours and he did not say he had. Then the man the figure of twenty-one years is against said he has been making a reading out of his own two hands since the tenth of this month and is not going to ask that man to do it. The man of fifty-six said out loud that he is not a part of this and is a man reading numbers. Nobody was asked to do anything.

**362. The twenty-first of the ninth, fifty days past.** The board at 270, the train at 586, the days nobody has entered anything at 300, two hundred and sixty-one days from the second of January, the man of about sixty-four on his sixty-first night. A day that was nothing but the day before, and nobody in that yard said one word about it. THE COST: the clerk of nineteen years carried the day's page up that bank three times and did not go the fourth time, stood at the foot of it for about nine minutes, and put the page on the end of that trestle table under a stone, and then the woman of fifty-eight, coming down that bank with two empty buckets of her own, said one thing out loud, in one hundred and forty words and in front of about nine people, that anybody at all can walk up to a page and read it and that a page in a room behind a shut door cannot, and that she has been coming up and down that bank for nineteen years and has never been let into that room and has never asked to be. Nine people turned round and the clerk said nothing. The mender said out loud that he bends fencing and is not a man who fixes things for a district. The page was still on that table in the morning.

**363. The twenty-second of the ninth, fifty-one days past.** The board at 271, the train at 587, the hundred and seventy-fourth day with no line on a board, the table eleven miles off for its seventy-seventh morning, the man of about sixty-four asleep on that low wall on sixty-one of the sixty-two nights. THE MACHINERY USED: the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing put four sentences about a fence into that yard and said he was not going to say them twice, and a man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart, at the head of that lane, READ THEM BACK FROM THE TOP, which is the first time a thing has been read back from the top in this district. He left out a clause and turned the last sentence into a question. THE COST: the man who had said it said the whole of it again from the top and not from the word, after having said in advance that he would not say it twice, and then the man with the cart said out loud that he does not know whether he had it right, because a man who knew would be the same man again. And in the afternoon nine feet of wire with a knot in it went into the gate at the head of the loam, unpaid, not asked for and not offered, because the man who digs loam said out loud on the eighteenth of this month that he had not asked for an inch of it. The man who keeps a road said eleven out loud and said it is a figure about a road and not about a man.

**364. The twenty-third of the ninth, fifty-two days past.** The board at 272, the train at 588, the pool of refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given a hundred and eighty-two days, the rival record in force a hundred and thirteen days. THE FOURTH OF THE FIVE: the man who convened the bid said out loud, in two hundred and forty-six words, that the fourth of the five things a document that sets a thing out has to say is the holder, that a holder is a person, that there is no person in that yard because he has put that lot in front of about nineteen people for twenty-two days and not one has come forward, and that the fifth is a remedy and a document that names a remedy nobody can use has found nobody, and that he would rather stop twice than say the fourth thing. THE COST: the man of about nineteen, who has counted everything said in that yard since the first of this month, did not count it, and wrote one line on his own page saying that he did not count it and that he does not know why. Four people in that yard said the fourth of the five ought to be a man who looks after that building. The man of fifty-six said the word keeper out loud once and a clerk entered that there has never been one of that lot on any page in this district. Nobody asked the mender to let anybody read anything back and he said so out loud.

**365. The twenty-fourth of the ninth, fifty-three days past.** The board at 273, the train at 589, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and fifty-six days back, the first day of the eighth month fifty-three days past. A man of about thirty-seven who puts tables up for anybody who will use them stood at the end of that trestle table for the first time in three weeks, read the four figures out loud, and got the length of this month wrong by one. THE COST: the man of fifty-six told him out loud, in front of about nine people, that the fourth of them is thirty-one and not thirty, and said it so that the other people who had heard it wrong would not go off that morning with it, and the man said thank you and nothing else and stayed at that table for about nine minutes afterwards with his hands where he keeps them and did not go and get his hand-cart and leave. A clerk entered that getting a figure right is not a finding and not a habit and not a figure about the man who read it, and that getting one wrong is not a finding either, and that nobody was found. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said out loud that he had got one of those four wrong twice this month going past that table and had said nothing for a fortnight. The man who convened the bid said out loud that a yard is the only instrument this district has that can correct a man in front of about nine people and that a book four hundred yards up that bank could not have done it.

**366. The twenty-fifth of the ninth, fifty-four days past.** The board at 274, the train at 590, two hundred and sixty-five days from the second of January, the table eleven miles off for its eightieth morning, the man of about sixty-four on sixty-four of the sixty-five nights. THE SECOND REFUSAL: the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing said out loud, in one hundred and seventy-six words, that he was going to put a figure on what a name in a list of hearers is worth to somebody four hundred miles off, and that his figure is a day's work. A man of about thirty-four who keeps a road said no in about four seconds, in thirty-nine words, with a reason of his own: a road a stranger can walk up to has not got my name on it, and neither has a man, and the day something wants a name put on it is the day it stops being a road. THE COST: the man who named the figure said that a reason has to be given twice to be a reason, and the same reason was given a second time in the same words at the same speed, and then about four people asked the man who named it what his figure was and it came out a different number in his own mouth the third time and he heard it change and said nothing about it and would not say it a fifth time. A clerk entered both refusals as refusals with a reason, entered that neither is a ninth of anything, that the eight refusals with no reason a clerk of a house has given did not move and are eight, that the pool last moved on the twenty-fourth of the third month and is five months back, and that a third would be a thing this district is doing on purpose and a thing a district is doing on purpose is a habit.

**367. The twenty-sixth of the ninth, fifty-five days past.** The board at 275, the train at 591, the days nobody has entered anything at 305, the rival record in force a hundred and sixteen days, the six households of the man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally a hundred and fifty-seven days, entered out of a ladder. THE ROAD ON THE PAGE: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a road took about nine people up that lane and put eleven miles of it and four gates into daylight, in two hundred and eighty-two words, and said that eleven is a figure about a road and not about him and that he said in this yard on the eighteenth that knowing where a road goes is not holding it and has not changed his mind. The mender put his right hand on the wire in the first gate and said out loud that nine feet of wire is the whole of the reason and that it is the first thing on that road a stranger would put his hand on. THE COST, TWO HALVES: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against said out loud, in one hundred and ninety-five words, in front of about nineteen people, that he had a sheet of paper under his arm with four things on it and was going to give it to the man in the lane at the first gate and did not, because that man said in this yard on the eighteenth that he would tear up a document with his name on it for that road. And the man who keeps a road was then kept in that yard until the light went answering nine questions about where a stranger would find the water, the loam, the ditch and the gate, refused the tenth in about four seconds, and got back to that low wall at about half past nine for the first time this month. Nobody handed him anything and nobody asked him to sign anything.

**368. The twenty-seventh of the ninth, fifty-six days past.** The board at 276, the train at 592, the hundred and seventy-ninth day with no line on a board, the rival record in force a hundred and seventeen days. THE QUESTION AND THE ANSWER: the man of about nineteen asked, in fifteen words and in front of about nineteen people, what a person has to be able to do to be a second reader, and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing answered, in a hundred and thirty-seven words, that you have to not be the man who said it, in a yard at a different hour, with the sentence in your head and no way of getting at the page, and that if you get it wrong you have to say so and be got right, and that everything else is a man reading a sentence twice and calling it a check. Nine people looked at him and nobody said anything. THE COST: the boy wrote a sentence down instead of a figure for the first time in twenty days, asked the clerk out loud where a sentence goes, was told it goes on the page with the rest of the day's line, and said he was going to leave it where it was because he was nobody's clerk; four people said he ought to be a clerk and he said no to all four. Then he asked what happens on a day this district cannot name, in nine words, and was told in a hundred and ten words that it goes past, and the mender went back up that lane to the gate with the broken latch. Everybody in that yard said the reading of that lot stands at the fourth of the five and it is begun and is not finished.

**369. The twenty-eighth of the ninth, fifty-seven days past.** The board at 277, the train at 593, the pool a hundred and eighty-seven days, the table eleven miles off for its eighty-third morning, the man of about sixty-four on sixty-seven of the sixty-eight nights. THE DAY WITH AN END ON IT: the man of fifty-six said one thing out loud, in a hundred and thirty-seven words, that there is a term on that lot, that it has an end on it, that he reads four figures off a table most mornings and has never been asked when it runs out and never offered to say, and that he is going to say in front of about nineteen people that he does not know and is not going to be told. A clerk entered that end out of a ladder and has not said it out loud. **NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID ONE WORD ABOUT IT AT ANY POINT IN THAT DAY.** The mender was not in that yard at all and nobody asked where. The man who digs loam said twenty-seven days out loud and said a figure about a thing is not a reason for nine people to be unhappy in a yard. THE COST: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against went up that lane to the foot of that bank at about half past five, stood there for about four minutes and turned round and came back down, and he had not gone up that bank since the thirteenth of this month, and nobody in that yard said one word to him about it and nobody asked where the woman who keeps those two books was. The page with the twenty-first of the ninth month on it was still on the end of that trestle table under a stone, seven days on.

**370. The twenty-ninth of the ninth, fifty-eight days past, and the last day of the block.** The board at 278, the train at 594, two hundred and sixty-nine days from the second of January, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and sixty-one days back, the man of about sixty-four on sixty-eight of the sixty-nine nights. THE BLOCK'S LAST ACT: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against said one thing out loud at about half past ten, in three hundred and twenty-one words, in front of about nineteen people, that he has worked out two things about the ten days he has stood in that yard saying figures out loud, that a reading is a thing a man says and a second person reads back from the top, that there is one man in this district who can and he is gone four days in a week, that he has known that since the sixteenth and said it out loud on the twentieth and has done one single thing about it since and neither has anybody else, and that he is not going to write a rule about it and not going to ask that man to do it and not going to name anybody to anything, and that a rule nobody can be made to keep is a thing one man is carrying, which a man who digs loam said at the top of that ditch on the eighteenth of this month and which is true. THE COST: his right hand did not close on a wet board and went about four inches across that trestle table with the page going with it and the corner off the stone, about nine people saw it, and the clerk of nineteen years did not stop writing and did not say anything and did not have to be asked and held the page down with the flat of her own hand for about four minutes. The mender said out loud that a district that has built one thing and cannot get it to do anything has still built it. At about six the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart came up that lane with his shafts down and about nine people watched him not come in. **THE READING OF THAT LOT IS STILL BEGUN AND NOT FINISHED, THE BID IS STILL OPEN AT TWENTY-EIGHT DAYS, THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD IS RULED AND EMPTY, THE PAGE IS STILL ON THE TABLE UNDER A STONE, AND THE NUMBER ON THAT SHEET IS STILL FOUR HUNDRED AND ELEVEN.**

**371. The thirtieth of the ninth, fifty-nine days past.** The board at 279, the train at 595, three hundred and nine days since anybody entered anything, and the term on that reading a month that runs out at about six this evening. THE DAY THE TERM RAN OUT. A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing asked the clerk of nineteen years, in that yard, in front of whoever was standing there, for the one figure on that lot nobody has ever heard said out loud, in a hundred and twenty-five words, and she gave it in sixty-two, and the day was said out loud in a yard for the first time in this district, and the man of fifty-six was asked what a term is four times and told all four of them the same thing, which is that he does not know when a term runs out and is not going to be told, and was not told. A lot book was ruled on the end of that trestle table at about two in the afternoon with nine inches of wire the mender took out of his pocket and put on the boards and did not stay to watch, and its first line was written, and it is the whole of what is in it: One lot. A building with two doors. About six and a half thousand bucketfuls of water. No person in it. At about ten to six the man who convened the bid said the term was up and tried three times to say the figure of it and could not get it out, and afterwards nobody in that yard could, and the clerk, who had said a month out loud at half past ten without any trouble at all, could not either, and wrote it down out of a ladder at about ten past seven with him standing about nine feet off. THE COST IS A VOICE. The bid stood open twenty-nine days and was not run and nobody proposed anything about closing it. The column is ruled and was empty. The man of about sixty-four is on his seventieth night.

**372. The thirty-first of the ninth, sixty days past, and the day with nothing in it.** The board at 280, the train at 596, the pool a hundred and ninety days, the table eleven miles off for its eighty-sixth morning. NO COUNTED SPEECH IN THIS CHAPTER AND NO FIGURE ON THE PAGE ABOUT ANYBODY IN IT. A man of about thirty-four who mends fencing stood at the end of that trestle table from about ten until about twelve with nothing to read and did not make one up. A man of about nineteen stood about nine feet off that table from about eleven until about half past twelve and counted nothing at all, for the first time in this volume, and nobody asked him why. A man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally, who is in no scene in any other block of this volume, came down that lane, stood at the end of that table with a flat book under his arm and his sleeves turned back, looked at the one line in that lot book standing up, said out loud that a line is a line and that he keeps six households and that is all he has come to say, and went back up the lane, and nobody asked him for his figure. The mender gave nine inches of his own wire to rule that book and went to the gate at the head of the loam. The term on that reading is one day past and has not renewed. The bid is open thirty days and was not run. The fifth of the five things this district does not have is still not paid and the count is still five. The page with the twenty-first of the ninth month on it had been on that table ten days. THE COST IS THE DAY.

**373. The first of the tenth month, the first morning of a month nobody in this district has checked, and sixty-one days past.** The board at 281, the train at 597, two hundred and seventy-two days from the second of January, the pool a hundred and ninety-one days. A clerk of nineteen years said out loud, in eighty-five words, that nobody in this district has ever checked how long this month is and that the figure for it came off the month before it, which is a figure about a list and not about a day, and that she is not going to check a month by standing in it. THE FIGURE A MAN IS AFRAID OF WAS NAMED OUT LOUD, IN ONE HUNDRED AND NINETY WORDS, WITH THE DAY ON IT. Nothing came on the first of the tenth month. Nobody in that yard went up that lane toward that gatepost and nobody said out loud that they were going to look. The man the figure of twenty-one years is against stood at the end of that trestle table from about eleven until about four and said nothing, and at about half past three got as far as the fourth of the five things a document that sets a thing out has to say and stopped there, and the reading that was standing ready was not used. Three people in that yard each said one short sentence and the man of about nineteen counted all three and got the same figure three times, and a clerk entered that getting a figure right three times is not a finding and is not a habit and is not a figure about the man who counted it, and that getting it right once was not a finding either and that was entered on the twenty-ninth of the eighth. Six people in that yard argued about where a second figure would go if one came and named six places and agreed on none. THE COST: at ten minutes before the light went the boy put his own page on the end of that trestle table under the stone, where anybody can walk up and read it.

**374. The second of the tenth, sixty-two days past, and the reversal dealt with in a room.** The rival record in force a hundred and twenty-three days and the age of the figure about that figure a hundred and twenty-three days, and the six households a hundred and sixty-four days, and the first day of the eighth month sixty-two days past. At about eleven the man the figure of twenty-one years is against went into that building with two doors and left the plank door open, and about four minutes later the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing went in after him, and the door stayed open, and they were in there until about half past two, and the man who mends fencing said in two hundred and eight words that he said the reversal and has done nothing about it and cannot do anything about it either, and the man who convened it said in two hundred and three words that he is not going to write a rule about it and not going to ask the man with a cart and not going to name anybody. Nothing was said in that room that had not already been said in that yard. AT ABOUT ONE, WITH BOTH OF THEM IN THAT BUILDING, THE SECOND OF THE FOUR FIGURES ON THAT TRESTLE TABLE WAS SAID OUT LOUD IN THAT YARD AND WAS WRONG BY FOUR, AND WENT ROUND THAT YARD FOR ABOUT HALF AN HOUR, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN HEARD IT FOUR TIMES AND DID NOT STOP IT BECAUSE NOBODY HAD ASKED HIM TO, AND THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX WAS UP THAT LANE AT THE FENCE END. When the two of them came out at half past two the wrong figure was said again and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing listened to it and did not go to the boards. THE COST. A clerk entered that a man who catches a mistake and has not been asked to catch one is not a reader and is nobody's clerk.

**375. The third of the tenth, sixty-three days past, and a question asked about a person.** Eighty-nine mornings that table eleven miles off has stood, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and sixty-six days back, and a hundred and eighty-six days with no line on a board. About four people in that yard had worked out since the twenty-third of the ninth which man the fourth of the five things a document that sets a thing out has to say is, and the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing was at that table with his arms folded and had not read the four figures out loud. AT ABOUT ELEVEN THE MAN OF ABOUT NINETEEN ASKED HIM, IN A YARD, IN THE ORDINARY WAY, WHETHER HE IS THE FOURTH OF THOSE FIVE THINGS, IN A HUNDRED AND FOURTEEN WORDS, AND HE ANSWERED IN A HUNDRED AND FIFTY-ONE WORDS THAT IT IS A PERSON AND HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHETHER IT IS HIM, AND THAT THE FOURTH OF THOSE FIVE IS WHOEVER IS STANDING AT THAT TABLE WHEN THE THING GETS SAID, AND THAT A DIFFERENT PERSON MOST MORNINGS. THE BOY WROTE BOTH SIDES DOWN BEFORE THE ANSWER WAS FINISHED. From half past eleven the mender stood at the fence end of that lane and was not at the end of that trestle table for the rest of that day, and about four people said the top one of the four to each other behind the men who read them instead and got it right both times. The man the figure of twenty-one years is against said out loud at about two that if he said one word about where that man was standing he would have made it a post in about four sentences and there is not a post in this district. The man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart came up the lane at about five and did not come in and nobody sent anybody out to him.

**376. The fourth of the tenth, sixty-four days past, and the rival record answered once.** The board at 284, the train at 600, the rival record a hundred and twenty-five days and the age of the figure about that figure a hundred and twenty-five days. THAT RECORD IS SET AGAINST FOUR PLACES FOUR HUNDRED MILES AWAY AND HAD BEEN READ OUT IN THAT YARD SEVEN TIMES IN SEVENTEEN DAYS, AND ITS EIGHTH READING WAS REFUSED OUT LOUD WITH A REASON ON THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF THE JUNE. At about half past eleven the man the figure of twenty-one years is against went down on his right knee in the mud at the foot of that trestle table and got it out from under the leg, and it unfolded about halfway and would not unfold further and he did not shake it and did not read it, and he could not get up in one movement and got up in about four seconds on his own with nobody offering an arm. THEN HE ANSWERED IT, OUT LOUD, IN DAYLIGHT, IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE, IN TWO HUNDRED AND EIGHTY-TWO WORDS, WITH A BOOK: THIS DISTRICT HAS ONE BOOK AND IT IS ON THE END OF THAT TABLE AND ANY PERSON CAN WALK UP AND READ IT AND THERE IS ONE LINE IN IT AND THE LINE GIVES A FIGURE OF THAT LOT AND NOT ONE PERSON IN IT, AND IT IS SMALLER THAN WHAT THAT RECORD HAS AND HE IS NOT GOING TO PRETEND THEY ARE THE SAME SIZE. The man of fifty-six read that one line out loud standing up about a minute later and nobody read it back from the top. Four people in that yard then asked what that book was worth and did not get a figure, in a hundred and nineteen words. The man of about thirty-four who mends fencing, who refused the eighth reading out loud in about four seconds on the twenty-fourth of the sixth, said in a hundred and forty words that what happened in that yard is not the eighth and that the two things are not the same size. A clerk entered that a record answered in a yard is answered for the people standing in that yard and not for a body four hundred miles off, and that what was said was not a figure and not a number and not a ninth reading, and that no column was filled and no name went into it. The man of about sixty-four got up off that low wall at about half past seven for the first time in a run and sat down again.

**377. The fifth of the tenth, sixty-five days past, and a page in the rain, and the flag spent in a mouth.** Nobody has entered anything for three hundred and fifteen days, two hundred and seventy-six days from the second of January, the pool a hundred and ninety-five days and seven months back, the table eleven miles off for its ninety-first morning. The rain came in at about half past two and went on for about four hours. THE PAGE WITH THE TWENTY-FIRST OF THE NINTH ON IT HAD BEEN ON THAT TABLE FIFTEEN DAYS AND THE CLERK OF NINETEEN YEARS, ABOUT NINE FEET OFF, SAW THE WHOLE OF WHAT HAPPENED AND SAID NOT ONE WORD: THE WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT PUT ONE OF HER TWO BUCKETS DOWN IN THE MUD, PICKED THE PAGE UP OFF THE STONE WITH BOTH HANDS, SHOOK IT ONCE AGAINST HER THIGH, PUT IT BACK UNDER THE STONE, PICKED THE BUCKET UP OUT OF THE MUD AND WENT ON UP THE BANK. THE COST, IN TWO FULL HANDS AND ONE BUCKET. Then the clerk of nineteen years said one thing out loud in that yard, in front of about nine people, in two hundred and forty-three words, and it is the thing the previous block owed: the old pages carry three months back and four months back and five months back, the third month is seven months back this morning, every one of those pages is now carrying the wrong figure, she is not going back up that bank to alter one line of one of them, and a correction that goes back and edits the thing it corrects cannot afterwards be checked. NO LEDGER PAGE WAS ALTERED AND THE HEDGE IS NOT BROKEN. Nobody carried a page up that bank that afternoon and nobody went up it, the second of the two books was not touched and was not named by anybody in that yard, and the two sheets of print were on that step of two stones in the morning and in the evening. At about five the boy asked her whether a page goes in that room, in ninety-three words, and she answered in a hundred and forty-five that a page goes in that room when somebody carries it up there, and that she has not gone up it since the twenty-first of the ninth, and that a page on a table is a page a person can read and that is not the same as a page in a room.

**378. The sixth of the tenth, sixty-six days past, and a day's work asked for in a yard.** The board at 286, the train at 602, the removal day sixty-six days past, the six households a hundred and sixty-eight days, and a hundred and eighty-nine days with no line on a board. AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A ROAD CAME INTO THAT YARD INSTEAD OF GOING PAST IT AND ASKED THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO MENDS FENCING FOR SOMETHING, OUT LOUD, IN A HUNDRED AND SIXTY WORDS, AND SAID BEFORE HE WAS ANSWERED THAT HE HAD NOTHING TO GIVE ANYBODY AND THAT IT IS A QUESTION AND NOT AN OFFER: there is a fourth gate on that road about a mile off and the bottom rail is out of it and he has been opening it with his boot since the second day of this month. THE MENDER ANSWERED IN A HUNDRED AND FORTY-ONE WORDS THAT HE WILL DO IT AND IS NOT GOING TO NAME A FIGURE FOR IT, BECAUSE A FIGURE IS A THING SOMEBODY IS TOLD AND NOBODY IN THAT YARD HAS BEEN TOLD WHAT A DAY'S WORK IS WORTH TO ANYBODY. HE WENT UP THAT ROAD AT ABOUT ELEVEN AND WAS GONE UNTIL ABOUT FIVE AND PUT WIRE IN THE LATCH AND BOUND THE RAIL AND THE GATE DOES NOT HANG STRAIGHT, AND HE CAME BACK WITH HIS HANDS BLACK TO THE WRIST AND SAID IN A HUNDRED AND THIRTY-ONE WORDS THAT THAT GATE IS WORSE THAN IT WAS AND IT IS HELD AND THOSE ARE TWO THINGS AND HE HAS ONLY GOT ONE PAIR OF HANDS. The road-keeper stood at the top of that rise and watched for about nine minutes and went down the road, and on his way back at about half past four he put his boot against that gate and it did not give. NOBODY PAID HIM. THE FIFTH OF THE FIVE THINGS THIS DISTRICT DOES NOT HAVE IS STILL NOT PAID AND THE COUNT IS STILL FIVE, AND A DAY'S WORK IS NOT A TOLL BECAUSE A TOLL IS A THING A HOLDER PAYS UNDER A TERM AND THERE IS NO HOLDER IN THAT YARD.

**379. The seventh of the tenth, sixty-seven days past, and an ordinary day.** The rival record a hundred and twenty-eight days and the age of the figure about that figure a hundred and twenty-eight days, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and seventy days back, and a hundred and ninety days with no line on a board. THE DAY WAS ORDINARY AND THAT IS ITS COST. The man of about sixty-four sat at the foot of that low wall on his seventy-eighth night and seventy-eighth day with nothing in his hands, and nobody in that yard said one word about his hands, or about the seventy-eight days, or about the night of the twenty-first of the eighth, and about four people said good morning to him and about four of them said good morning to each other instead. AT ABOUT ONE TWO PEOPLE IN THAT YARD SAID A THING OUT LOUD ABOUT HIM THAT WAS NOT ABOUT HIS HANDS AND WAS NOT SAID TO HIM: one said it was not right that a man had sat at the foot of that wall for seventy-eight days and nobody had asked him one thing, and the other said there was nothing to give him, and he heard both of those. A clerk entered that a thing a person is not given is not a thing a person has refused. The four figures were the same four as yesterday and the day before. The man of about thirty-seven who puts tables up said that there is nothing this week to put a table under, and untied his hand-cart from that wall at about six and wheeled it four feet and left it there, and that was the only thing anybody moved in that yard all day that was not moved on purpose. The boy counted four figures and wrote four figures down and put nothing else on his page. Nothing was proposed about the bid, the term, the reading, or a second line in that lot book, and the column was ruled and empty. The removal day is sixty-seven days past and nobody in that yard said one word about it in the morning or in the evening.

**380. The eighth of the tenth, sixty-eight days past, and the last day of the block.** The board at 288, the train at 604, two hundred and seventy-nine days from the second of January, the pool a hundred and ninety-eight days. A clerk entered that this is the last day of the run of figures this district began at the end of the month before last, and that whether anybody is going to read them out loud in that yard tomorrow is not a thing she has been asked and is not a thing she has offered. AT ABOUT HALF PAST ELEVEN A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN WHO PUTS TABLES UP CAME DOWN THAT LANE WITH A CHAIR HE COULD NOT CARRY, BECAUSE HE HAS HAD NO USE IN EITHER OF HIS HANDS SINCE BEFORE THIS DISTRICT BEGAN KEEPING FIGURES, AND HE WALKED IT ABOUT FORTY YARDS ON THE SIDE OF HIS RIGHT BOOT AND IT WENT ALONG IN THE MUD AND TWICE STOPPED AND HE GOT IT GOING AGAIN WITH HIS HIP, AND HE UNTIED IT OFF HIS HAND-CART WITH HIS FOREARM AND ABOUT NINE FEET OF STRING CAME DOWN INTO THE MUD AND HE LEFT THE STRING THERE AND NOBODY PICKED IT UP. About nine people in that yard watched a chair come down that lane and not one of them said anything. HE SET IT DOWN AT THE END OF THAT TABLE AT THE CORNER THE OTHER CHAIR IS NOT AT, PUSHED IT SQUARE WITH HIS BOOT, STOOD THERE ABOUT A MINUTE, SAID NOTHING AT ALL, AND WENT BACK UP THE LANE. A clerk entered that a chair is a chair, and that a second chair is not a sixth of the five things this district protects and is not a seventh of the six instruments and carries no job, and that a man who puts tables up is not a keeper of tables and is not a holder of anything, and that nobody in that yard asked him to put it down. NOBODY SAT IN IT TODAY. There is no figure on any page in this district of how long the first of those two chairs has stood there and nobody in that yard will say one out loud, and a clerk entered that the absence of a figure is not a zero and is not a way of saying that nobody has ever sat in one. At about half past four he came back down the lane and said one thing out loud to nobody in particular, in seventy-seven words: I did not put that chair there for anybody, and if somebody sits in it one day that is a thing they did and not a thing I got. At about half past six the clerk of nineteen years folded the day's page and carried it up that bank for the first time since the twenty-first of the ninth and did not go into that room and did not knock and nobody in that yard asked her where she was going. The term on that reading is nine days past. The rival record is in force a hundred and twenty-nine days and was answered once out loud on the fourth of this month and is still in force. The bid is open. The column is ruled and was empty. The chair was still at the end of that trestle table with nobody in it.

## Chapters 0381 to 0390 — Volume 08 Batch 0005, the ninth to the eighteenth of the tenth month

**381. The ninth of the tenth, sixty-nine days past the removal day, and the first of the nine sittings.** The board at 289, the train at 605, two hundred and eighty days from the second of January, the pool a hundred and ninety-nine days, the rival record in force a hundred and thirty days and the age of the figure about that figure a hundred and thirty days, the nine printed nights a hundred and seventy-two days back, the table eleven miles off for its ninety-fifth morning, the man of about sixty-four on his eightieth night of that run and eighty days in this district. THE COST, IN A BODY AND IN FOUR MOUTHS: the man of about thirty-four who digs loam came up out of that ditch wet to the middle of his thigh and sat down in the second chair for about a minute, and four people in that yard had already said out loud among themselves that it was the chair, and he said in sixty-five words, in front of them, that his legs went in that ditch at about half past nine and that he was telling the yard before about four of them told it the other way. The man of about nineteen did not count the minute and wrote on his own page that he does not know why. The bid is open thirty-nine days and the term on that reading is ten days past and a second line was not asked for in that lot book and was not written.

**382. The tenth of the tenth, seventy days past, and the day before the third of the three things.** The rival record in force a hundred and thirty-one days, nobody has entered anything for three hundred and twenty days, and the mender, who has been in this yard five months, said the rule out loud in the ordinary voice in front of about nineteen people in TWO HUNDRED AND TWO WORDS: a thing binds when a second person reads it back from the top and gets it right; it does not bind on a name, a signature, a mark or a person; a second reader is nobody in particular and is not the man who counts because the man who counts is the one who catches the mistake; it is not a post, a column, a steward or a keeper, and there has never been a keeper on a page in this district; it is found by whoever is standing there and is not found the same way twice. THEN HE SAID THE PRICE OUT LOUD IN THE SAME VOICE AND WAS NOT ASKED TO: nobody can be made to keep it, and the day one of us decides not to do it there is nobody above him to be told and nobody behind him to be asked. THE COST, A BLANK PAGE: at about ten past twelve the man of about nineteen asked one question in forty-two words and was told in about four seconds, with a reason, that he is not the second reader, and the boy put his own page face down and did not turn it over again until about four, and when he turned it over there was nothing on the back of it, and he looked at the blank side of his own page for about two minutes. Nobody sat in either chair that day. The term on that reading is eleven days past and did not renew.

**383. The eleventh of the tenth, seventy-one days past, and the third of the three things becomes true, and the cost is paid.** The ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and seventy-four days back, the table for its ninety-seventh morning, the six households a hundred and seventy-three days out of a ladder, the pool two hundred and one days. The man the figure of twenty-one years is against said out loud that the building with two doors is held for a term of a month on the two terms lying on the table against its outside wall, and that anybody may go into it, and that nobody may be entered in a record of this district by having heard a sound, and a clerk entered that a building is not a person and cannot hold a lot and cannot pay a toll. The man of fifty-six read the one line of the lot book out loud and put the book back under the stone and nobody read it back. THE COST, AND IT IS BEING ASKED: at about twelve the woman of fifty-eight came down that bank with two full buckets and stopped, and he asked her, out loud, in that yard, in daylight, in front of about nineteen people, whether she would read that one line back from the top, and she said one thing about nineteen years and about not being asked for two things, and wiped her hands on her apron for about a second and a half, and read the whole line from the beginning without stopping in the middle of it and got it right. Then she sat in the second chair for about a minute and carried her buckets up the bank and said out loud at the top of the first go, in thirty-three words, that she is not going to do it again and that nobody has asked her to do it again. NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID ONE WORD ABOUT HER NINETEEN YEARS AND A CLERK DID NOT ENTER THAT SHE WAS HONOURED AND A CLERK MAY NOT ENTER IT.

**384. The twelfth of the tenth, seventy-two days past, and a right hand opens in the middle of a reading.** The board at 292, the train at 608, a hundred and ninety-five days with no line on a board. He read the first of the five things a document that sets a lot out has to say in a hundred and sixty words and the second in seventy-eight, and got four inches into the third, which is the toll, and his right hand opened and the sheet went off the top of that table and landed on the wet boards. THE COST, IN A HAND AND IN A WET SHEET: the man of about thirty-four who mends fencing got there with his right forearm and lay it flat on the sheet for about eleven minutes without picking it up or reading it or turning it over, and said afterwards that he had not been asked and had not said yes, and the reader began again from the top holding the sheet in his left hand and got through three of the five and did not say the fourth, which is a person, and there is no person in that yard. The man of about nineteen sat in the second chair from about half past eleven until about one and counted nothing at all for an hour and a half and said out loud that there is a difference between a figure and a thing somebody said while they were holding a piece of paper with a hand that does not close. THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD IS RULED AND EMPTY AND NOTHING WENT INTO IT BY ANYBODY.

**385. The thirteenth of the tenth, seventy-three days past, and the man who convened the bid is not the man who read it, and the toll.** Two hundred and eighty-four days from the second of January, the table for its ninety-ninth morning, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and seventy-six days back. THE COST, IN A LEFT HAND AND ONE WORD: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against picked the lot book up with his left hand, put it down in front of the mender the wrong way up, and the clerk of nineteen years said one word out loud from about nine feet off, and his right hand did not come out of his coat again that day. The mender read the lot out loud and said before he started that he did not open it and is not the one who asked for it, and then read the toll in a hundred and seventy words: a toll is a thing a holder pays under a term, there is no holder of that lot, if a building were a holder the toll would fall on whoever was standing inside it, and a building cannot be asked who is in it. THE PRICE OF THE RULE COLLECTED: nobody read any of it back from the top, and a clerk entered that the rule costs nothing to keep and was not kept on this day and that nobody can be made to keep it. A second line went into the lot book at about two out of a figure the digger gave with his mouth about a ditch. The man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds sat in the second chair for about a minute, wet to the waist. The fifth of the five things this district does not have is still not paid and the count is still five.

**386. The fourteenth of the tenth, seventy-four days past, and the bank, the door, and one question.** The board at 294, the train at 610, the rival record in force a hundred and thirty-five days and the age of that figure a hundred and thirty-five days, the table for its hundredth morning. The man the figure of twenty-one years is against sat in the second chair for about a minute at about eleven and got up out of it on his own in one movement, and he has not been asked to sit in it and is not entered either way. THE COST, IN FOUR MINUTES ON A STEP: he went up about nine feet of bank at about half past twelve, stood on the top step of two stones for about four minutes before he moved, and DID NOT KNOCK, and the door opened about the width of a hand and then of a shoulder, and he asked her one question, in fifty-six words, about whether a thing binds in a book and in a room and in a yard, and she said no in about four seconds, in thirty-nine words, and gave a reason of her own: nineteen years she has written down what people said in that room and has never once written down what they ought to do. HE DID NOT ASK HER TWICE. The second of those two books was not opened, nothing in that room touched it, the one line in it in her own hand that nobody asked her to write was not read aloud by anybody and he was not told what it says. Then he said one thing out loud on the step, in fifty-five words, about two sheets of print he did not move, and went back down the bank more slowly than he came up it.

**387. The fifteenth of the tenth, seventy-five days past, and a day's work, and what it is worth, which is nothing.** Two hundred and eighty-six days from the second of January, three hundred and twenty-five days with nothing entered, the six households a hundred and seventy-seven days. THE ASK, BY A WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT, IN A YARD: she asked the man of about thirty-seven who cuts reeds for a day's work in a hundred and forty-eight words, and told him before he answered that the outlet at the low end of that ditch has been silted up since before she started carrying water out of it, that she is not able to get into it because she is fifty-eight and has two buckets and a bank, that she cannot pay him and cannot be paid, and that she is asking him because he is in that ditch every day. HE SAID YES IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS. THE COST, IN A BODY: he went down into about a foot and a half of standing water at the low end of a three foot ditch with his hands and no third thing to stand on, the digger stood at the top of that bank for about nine minutes and did not go down, and he came up wet to the chest and could not get the last two feet with his legs and got up it on his hands and his knees, and said out loud in ninety-eight words that it was a day he had spent and that he would have said no to a figure and that it was a ditch and not a favour. NOBODY WAS PAID. A man of about thirty-seven who puts tables up sat in the second chair for a minute in a chair he brought down that lane himself. The figure on the second line of the lot book stopped being true at about six and nobody altered the line and nobody wrote a second figure beside it.

**388. The sixteenth of the tenth, seventy-six days past, and five ninths on one page, none of them filled.** Two hundred and eighty-seven days from the second of January, the pool two hundred and six days, the term on that reading seventeen days past. The man of about fifty-six read the four figures before the mender got to them, got all four, and then read them again for the three people who had come in behind the mender, and that was the first time in five months he had read the same four numbers twice in one morning. THE COST, IN A WORD MISSING OUT OF A PAGE: the man of about nineteen copied the five headings onto his own page in four minutes and got the third one wrong by leaving a word out of it, and the clerk of nineteen years was about nine feet off and looked at what he had written at about two and did not tell him, because he is nobody's clerk, and he found it himself at about four and said out loud that he does not know whether that is a thing about him or about this yard, and then left the word out on purpose. A clerk entered that nine sittings is a figure about nine sittings and is not a figure about nine people, and that whether the nine are nine people or one person nine times is not a question she has been asked and is not going to answer.

**389. The seventeenth of the tenth, seventy-seven days past, and four accounts of one sentence.** The rival record in force a hundred and thirty-eight days and the age of that figure a hundred and thirty-eight days, two hundred and eighty days with no line on a board, the removal day seventy-seven days past. Two hundred and eighty-eight days separate the second of January and this morning. About four people in that yard were not there on the fourth of this month and asked what had been said, and he said it again in sixty-eight words, shorter than the two hundred and eighty-two he said it in the first time, and then he was told all four accounts of it, one after another, and said out loud, in a hundred and twelve words, that he was not going to correct one word of any of them, because a yard is a room with no roof and what gets said in one cannot be got back out of it. THE COST IS THAT: he let four wrong accounts of his own sentence stand in a yard on purpose. At about four the man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart came into that yard for the first time in this block, was asked nothing, read nothing, and sat in the second chair for about a minute while the yard watched the only man in this district who has ever read a thing back from the top read nothing at all. The interval a body four hundred miles off prints at is sixty-one days and it is named once in a narrator's register and not spent, and a clerk entered that a body which prints does not arrive, that it has not arrived on any day of this month, and that a thing which does not arrive has no face.

**390. The eighteenth of the tenth, seventy-eight days past the removal day, and the last day of Volume 08.** The board at 298, the train at 614, two hundred and eighty-nine days from the second of January, the pool two hundred and eight days, the table eleven miles off for its hundred and fourth morning, the man of about sixty-four on his eighty-ninth night of that run and eighty-nine days in this district, the rival record in force a hundred and thirty-nine days, the bid open forty-eight days, the term on that reading nineteen days past, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and eighty-one days back, and the figure on the sheet at that gatepost still four hundred and eleven. THE COST, IN A BOY'S HAND: at about half past five the man of about nineteen wrote nine at the top of his own page and took nine names off it, in his own hand, and said out loud, in seventy-nine words, that nine sittings is a figure about nine sittings and that he does not know that they are nine people and neither do you, and that there is one of him on that page nine times over and that is the most he is going to put down. A clerk entered that the fifth of the five things this district does not have is still not paid, and that this district has had two lots in a book for four days and has not paid anybody for either of them, and that the old charter in the book four hundred yards from that gate is still wrong on its face in a book a stranger may walk up to, and that a wrong count is not struck by writing a correct one beside it. THE COLUMN FOR THE NAME OF WHOEVER READ A THING OUT LOUD WAS RULED AND WAS EMPTY AT ABOUT SIX AND NO NAME WENT INTO IT BY ANYBODY. **AND AT ABOUT HALF PAST SIX THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX FINISHED AT THE END OF THAT TRESTLE TABLE, LOOKED AT THE SECOND CHAIR FOR ABOUT A SECOND AND A HALF, AND SAT DOWN IN IT, AND THE WHOLE OF THAT YARD SAW A MAN WHO HAS READ THE FIGURES OFF THOSE BOARDS EVERY MORNING FOR FIVE MONTHS SIT DOWN IN A CHAIR THAT A MAN WITH NO USE IN EITHER HAND PUT DOWN ON THE EIGHTH OF THIS MONTH, AND NOBODY COUNTED HOW LONG HE WAS IN IT, AND NOBODY SAID WHETHER HE WAS GOING TO BE THE SECOND READER OF ANYTHING, AND THE CHAIR WAS AT THE END OF THAT TRESTLE TABLE WITH SOMEBODY IN IT.**

## Chapters 0391 to 0400 — Volume 09 Batch 0001, the nineteenth to the twenty-eighth of the tenth month

**391. The nineteenth of the tenth, seventy-nine days past the removal day, and the day after the chair, and the month nobody has counted.** The board at 299, the train at 615 days, three hundred and twenty-nine days with nothing entered, two hundred and ninety days from the second of January, the pool at two hundred and nine days and seven months back, the bid open forty-nine days and not run, and the figure on the second line of the lot book four days out of date and not altered. NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID ONE WORD ABOUT A CHAIR AND THE MAN OF FIFTY-SIX DID NOT GO UP THAT LANE TO THE FENCE END FOR THE FIRST TIME IN MONTHS. **THE COST, IN A MAN SAYING HE DOES NOT KNOW: at about half past ten, unasked, he lifted his hand off those boards, turned round to the yard, and said out loud in front of about nineteen people that he has read the figures off those boards every morning for six months and has got them every morning and cannot tell you how many days the month after this one has and is not going to stand there and guess at it. The man of about nineteen counted what he said and got fifty and the count is fifty because that is what is printed there. A clerk entered that the length of the month after this one is not a figure this district has and that a month nobody has counted is a month nobody has checked and that a figure somebody has entered because they had to enter one is not a promise about a later day, which she wrote on the eighteenth of the sixth month of this year and is writing again, and that the second time a person writes a sentence is a habit and a habit is a finding.** Then about nine people in that yard spent about an hour trying to get the figure off four boards, four of them got it nine days out, one of them got it four days out the other way, and a board that carries days is not a board that carries months. **AND THE NAME DISCLOSURE, ONCE, IN A YARD, IN THE ORDINARY VOICE, AND IT IS NOT A NAME: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against said out loud that he is not going to be called anything, that he has not been called anything for six months, and that none of them had asked him for one this morning, and the boy counted what he said and got fifty-seven, and a clerk entered that a man who has said out loud that he is not going to be called anything has not refused and cannot be counted either way and that the record about the not asking says not asked and that no name went into any of the nine columns and none was going to.** NO NAME WAS PRINTED, NOBODY WAS ASKED FOR ONE, AND THE COLUMN WAS RULED AND WAS EMPTY AT ABOUT SIX.

**392. The twentieth of the tenth, eighty days past, and a line that is out of date, and a boy in the water.** The board at 300, the train at 616 days, three hundred and thirty days with nothing entered, one hundred and ninety-one days from the second of January, two hundred and three days with no line on a board, the rival record and the age of the figure about that figure both at a hundred and forty-one days off one anchor and one exclusion, and the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and eighty-three days back. THE DIGGER, WHOSE MOUTH THE SECOND LINE CAME OUT OF ON THE THIRTEENTH, READ THE TWO LINES STANDING UP WITHOUT BEING ASKED, STOPPED AT THE SECOND ONE, WENT ABOUT NINE FEET TO LOOK OVER THE LOW WALL AT THE WATER, CAME BACK AND SAID OUT LOUD THAT IT IS OUT OF DATE AND THAT IT STOPPED BEING TRUE ON THE FIFTEENTH AND THAT IT IS FIVE DAYS OUT OF DATE AND THAT NOBODY WROTE DOWN THE DAY AND HE DID NOT SAY THE DAY BECAUSE NOBODY ASKED HIM FOR IT. THAT IS SIXTY-ONE WORDS AND THE COUNT REPRODUCES. A clerk entered that a line which has stopped being true is not a wrong line and is not a mistake anybody made. THE MENDER STOPPED THE YARD IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS ON THE POINT THAT A CORRECT FIGURE ON A PIECE OF PAPER IN A YARD IN DAYLIGHT IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE IS A CORRECT FIGURE BESIDE IT IN EVERY WAY THAT MATTERS. **THE PROTAGONIST READ BOTH LINES OUT LOUD AND THEN SAID OUT LOUD, IN SIXTY-NINE WORDS, THAT NOBODY WROTE DOWN THE DAY AND THAT THE ONLY MAN WHO KNOWS IT IS THE MAN WHO CUTS REEDS AND THAT HE HAS NOT BEEN ASKED AND THAT HE IS NOT GOING TO ASK HIM IN THAT YARD. NOBODY WAS ASKED, NOTHING WAS CORRECTED, NOTHING WAS WRITTEN BESIDE THE LINE, AND NOBODY SAID THE CORRECT FIGURE OUT LOUD. THE COST, IN A BODY: at about half past two the boy went down the bank on his own, his right hand slipped on the wet wood, he sat on the bank with his feet in about four inches of water for about a minute and a half, he picked nothing up and put nothing in, and he came back up on his hands and his knees and said out loud that he is not going to be the one who brings a figure back out of it, because the moment he does there are two figures about that lot and a stranger cannot tell which is the one.**

**393. The twenty-first of the tenth, eighty-one days past, and sixty-one days in a mouth.** The board at 301, the train at 617 days, two hundred and ninety-two days from the second of January, the bid open fifty-one days and not run on one of them, the second line six days out of date, and a body four hundred miles off twenty days past a printing it did not make. **THE COST, IN A SENTENCE SAID IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINETEEN PEOPLE: the man the figure of twenty-one years is against said out loud that a month is the shortest interval this district has evidence for, that a figure written in a book a stranger may walk up to is out of date before anybody has finished looking at it, that he put the first one in there, and that the other figure he put in a book four hundred yards from that gate is sixty-one days and that that is the one he is afraid of, and that it is a figure about how often a body four hundred miles off prints and not a figure about the body, and a figure somebody has entered because they had to enter one is not a promise about a later day. THAT IS A HUNDRED AND FOURTEEN WORDS AND THE COUNT REPRODUCES. NOBODY SAID ANYTHING FOR ABOUT A MINUTE AND A HALF, AND THEN ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE SAID OUT LOUD THAT HE IS THE ONE WHO PUT IT THERE AND HE DID NOT SAY THAT THAT WAS NOT SO. A clerk entered that a figure about a body's habit is not a figure about a body and is not a promise about a later day, and that a clerk may not enter that anybody was waiting for it, and that a thing which does not arrive has no face.** Then the mender put the three things that can be done with a book in the open out loud in eighty-three words, and said that the third one is not going to be said out loud in that yard that afternoon by anybody and that he had not said it. The road man said out loud that a thing in the open gets read wrong and gets read wrong by people who did not come here on purpose. The tables-up man said out loud that a table with four legs out of a door is a table until somebody asks somebody a question at it, and that nobody has in six months. THE LAST IMAGE IS A RIGHT HAND THAT DID NOT CLOSE ON THE EDGE OF A TABLE.

**394. The twenty-second of the tenth, eighty-two days past, and the rule again, and its price again, and not on a sheet.** The board at 302, the train at 618 days, the pool at two hundred and twelve days, a table against a wall eleven miles off for its hundred and eighth morning, the rule twelve days old, the second line seven days out of date, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and eighty-five days back, the bid open fifty-two days. **THE COST, IN A RULE SAID TWICE: about four people asked the mender, in the ordinary way, to say the thing he said on the tenth of this month again, and he said it again in ninety-five words, standing at that table with his left hand in its cloth, and it was not the same words, and then he said the price out loud in seventy-two words and said that it was not kept on the fourth day after he said it, that the man reading out loud on that day was not the man who opened the book, that nobody read any of it back from the top, and that he has not asked anybody since whether they have kept it and is not going to. BOTH COUNTS REPRODUCE.** A person is named in the entry as having said it and that is a description and not a name, and no name of his is in any column. A RULE WHICH COSTS NOTHING TO KEEP IS A RULE NOBODY CAN BE MADE TO KEEP, and she entered that on the day it was said and is entering it again, and a sentence entered twice in a district is a habit and a habit is a finding and the finding is about her. The boy said out loud, unasked, that the column for the name of whoever read a thing out loud has been ruled since the thirtieth of the ninth, has been empty every day since including today, and that nobody has asked him to put a name in it and he is not going to, and that is eighty words and the count reproduces. Then about four people asked the mender to say the fourth thing ought to be finished and he said the fifth is a remedy and there is nobody in this district who can use one and he is not going to say what it is. THE COST AT THE END OF THE DAY IS A CLOTH COMING LOOSE AT THE WRIST AND BEING RE-TIED WITH THE TEETH AND THE RIGHT HAND IN ABOUT TWO MINUTES.

**395. The twenty-third of the tenth, eighty-three days past, and two buckets, and a shut door four hundred yards up a bank.** The board at 303, the train at 619 days, two hundred and ninety-four days from the second of January, the bid open fifty-three days, the second line eight days out of date, the removal day eighty-three days past. A yard stood about ten minutes looking at a shut door and at the two books on a shelf inside a room about nine feet by eleven and nobody went up that bank all day. The boy counted a sentence nobody had asked him to count, in seventy-two words, and said out loud that he had counted it and that he was not going to read the second line of the lot book back from the top for anybody, and then read the two lines out loud to no one. **THE COST, IN A MAN WALKING NINE FEET AND TURNING ROUND: at about half past four the mender took a sheet out of his coat and a pencil out of his pocket and got nine feet down that lane, and stopped, and put the pencil back, and put the sheet back, and stood about twenty seconds, and came back and said nothing at all about it, and about nine people in that yard saw it and about four of them said out loud to each other and not to him that they would have asked her, and he heard both and answered neither.** THE MOMENT: at about half past five the woman of fifty-eight came down that bank with two empty buckets, filled them at the trough, came back down the first go to the table, read the two lines standing up with her hand on the near edge of the boards and the buckets on the ground beside her, and said NOTHING to anybody. NOBODY IN THAT YARD SAID ONE WORD TO HER. A clerk entered that she was not asked and did not read anything back for anybody and that a clerk did not enter that she was read to and is not going to enter it, and then entered the rest of the day, and the rest of the day was nothing.

**396. The twenty-fourth of the tenth, eighty-four days past, and a man with a cart, and four days in a week.** The board at 304, the train at 620 days, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and eighty-seven days back, the bid open fifty-four days, the second line nine days out of date.
