The man of about twenty-nine who drives a cart came up that lane at about half past eleven with his shafts down, did not come into that yard, stopped at the low wall, was asked nothing, read nothing, and said nothing. **THE COST, IN A WHEEL: he got down, lifted a wheel about nine inches off the ground with his right forearm in front of about nineteen people, set it down, and did it again four seconds later, and nobody in that yard helped him and nobody asked him to and about nine people watched and none of them said one word.** THE ARGUMENT: about four people in that yard turned the figure about a man's week into a figure about how often a thing binds in this district, got about four a week and about twenty weeks and about a hundred a year, and the mender stopped them in about four seconds with a reason, and the boy said the count out loud and the mender said a fact about a man with a cart is a fact about a man with a cart and is not a rule and a rule is not a fact and neither of them is a finding, in a hundred words, and nobody wrote it down. The protagonist said out loud, in eighty-four words, that he has not read anything back from the top today and is not going to, and that a man with a cart out four days in a week is not a reason why anybody in that yard is bound by anything. The tables-up man said out loud that he put the second chair down on the eighth without being asked and that a chair is a chair and a forearm is a forearm and he has got neither of his. A market was denied four ways and the word is at eleven on three days and every one is a denial.

**397. The twenty-fifth of the tenth, eighty-five days past, and the thing on the end of that table, seen from four feet back.** The board at 305, the train at 621 days, the rival record and the age of the figure about that figure both at a hundred and forty-six days, the bid open fifty-five days, the second line ten days out of date. A man who puts tables up for anybody who will use them got the figure off the top board at about twenty past eight from the side away from the boards, where nobody usually stands, and got it. **THE COST AND THE POINT, IN ONE SPEECH: at about half past ten he put his forearm on that table, looked at the stone, the two lines, the clerk's page with nine columns and nothing in one of them, and the two chairs, stepped back about four feet to where a stranger would stand, and said out loud in eighty-six words that a man walking up to that table from that gate sees a stone and two lines in a book and a page with nine columns and nothing in them, and CANNOT TELL WHICH OF THE FIGURES IN THAT BOOK IS CURRENT, AND IT IS NOT BECAUSE THE BOOK IS UNCLEAR, IT IS BECAUSE THE BOOK IS CLEAR, and that is the whole trouble and it is on the end of that table in the open. THE COUNT REPRODUCES.** At about two he moved the stone about an inch to see whether it was loose, it was not loose, he put it back, and he said out loud in seventy-one words that he did that and not a clerk of nineteen years and not a boy of about nineteen and that he is not a keeper of that book, and the count reproduces. About four people in that yard asked the boy which of the two lines is current and he said he did not know and would not guess in front of about nineteen people. The protagonist read the two lines out loud at about three with his right hand flat on the boards beside the stone and it did not close, and nobody read them back from the top, and a clerk entered that a thing read back by nobody binds nobody.

**398. The twenty-sixth of the tenth, eighty-six days past, and the thing nobody is asking for, and a man whose hand did not do what he sent it to do.** The board at 306, the train at 622 days, three hundred and thirty-six days with nothing entered, the bid open fifty-six days, the second line eleven days out of date, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and eighty-nine days back, and the six households at a hundred and eighty-eight days, out of a ladder, and the pool at two hundred and sixteen days. A man of about forty-eight who keeps a tally, and has said of himself that he is fifty-one, stood at the dry far end of that yard from about ten until about half past three with a flat book under his left arm, and the woman of about thirty-six who keeps a scale stood about four feet off him with her arms folded, and about nine people watched two people stand four feet apart for about two hours and not one word passed between them. **THE COST, IN SEVENTY-THREE WORDS AND A HAND: he said out loud that six households have not moved for a hundred and eighty-eight days and that he has got the figure off a ladder and has never once been asked for it and is not going to be, and that he would like it written down that nobody asked him, because in a year somebody is going to say he gave a figure out in a yard and he did not. THE COUNT REPRODUCES. At about half past three he shifted the flat book from under his left arm to under his right AND HIS LEFT HAND DID NOT GO THE WAY HE SENT IT, which took him about four seconds longer than it would have taken a hand that worked, and about nine people in that yard saw that happen and not one of them said one word about it.** About four people took a step towards him to ask him for a figure and stopped, and a clerk entered that a ladder is not a board and that a figure somebody worked out with a pencil and did not write down where is a figure this district is carrying and not one it can check, and that the length of the ninth month was entered off a list by somebody else a while back and can never now be checked for the same reason. A clerk may not enter that he is a holder of anything and did not enter it. The protagonist stood nine feet off the two of them and said he was not asking either of them for anything. Nothing changed on purpose, and a clerk entered six things this yard did not ask anybody about.

**399. The twenty-seventh of the tenth, eighty-seven days past, and a man with nothing in his hands on his ninety-eighth night.** The board at 307, the train at 623 days, the pool at two hundred and seventeen days, the rival record a hundred and forty-eight days in force, the bid open fifty-seven days, the second line twelve days out of date, the ninth of the nine printed nights a hundred and ninety days back. A man of about sixty-four sat at the foot of a low wall about four feet high and about nine feet long, with his coat folded on the stones beside him and nothing in his hands, in his ninety-eighth day in this district, having slept on ninety-seven of the ninety-eight nights of his run, and the one night he did not sleep on is the night of the twenty-first of the eighth. About four people said good morning to him on one day and about four said good morning to each other instead, at the same time, so that nobody could tell which of them had said it to whom, and he said good morning back to all of them, whichever of them it was, and said that is how a yard works and that is not a complaint. THE MENDER CAME NINE FEET DOWN THE LANE, STOPPED, DID NOT ASK HIM ANYTHING, AND WENT AWAY AFTER ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AND PUT THE WIRE IN A FENCE, and a piece of wire in a pocket is not an offer and was not offered and was not taken. A CHILD OF ABOUT EIGHT PUT HIS HAND THROUGH A GAP IN THAT WALL AND LOOKED AT HIM AND WAS ASKED NOTHING AND ASKED NOTHING. **THE COST, IN SEVENTY-SIX WORDS AND THEN IN SIX SECONDS: he said out loud that he has had nothing in his hands since the morning of the twenty-first of the seventh month except this wall and a coat and about the length of that yard, and that nobody has asked him what he is going to do with them and he is not going to be, and the count reproduces. About four people in that yard said out loud he should be asked to sit in the second chair and about four other people said at the same time that nobody in that yard is going to ask him, and he heard both and said nothing about the chair and was not offered anything. And at about half past seven, when the light had gone, THE FOREARM THAT GETS HIM OFF THOSE STONES TOOK ABOUT SIX SECONDS WHERE ON THE NINETY-SEVENTH NIGHT IT TOOK ABOUT A SECOND AND A HALF, and about four people in that lane who had not gone home yet stood about nine feet off and watched that happen and not one of them said one word about it.** A clerk entered that a figure he folds his coat for is the night he is about to sleep and that night has not happened and is a hundred and ninety-ninth, and that nobody in that lane was sent on nine days today, and that a man whose trade is over is not a failure and is not a tragic figure and is not redeemed, and that a clerk is not going to say that about him and is not going to say the other thing either.

**400. The twenty-eighth of the tenth, eighty-eight days past, and four things in one yard, one of them out of date, and a clerk refusing to tell a month its own length.** The board at 308, the train at 624 days, three hundred and thirty-eight days with nothing entered since the twenty-fourth of November, the figure on the sheet at that gatepost four hundred and eleven with the same day on it, and its own age as a figure about the figure one hundred and forty-nine days, and the sheet's own tenure on that post not a figure this district has and not one a clerk is going to invent. THE MENDER SAID OUT LOUD, IN SEVENTY WORDS, THAT THREE OF THE FOUR THINGS IN THAT YARD WERE FIGURES THIS DISTRICT COULD CHECK AND ONE OF THEM WAS A FIGURE IN A BOOK IN THE OPEN THAT NOBODY COULD CHECK AND HAD NOT BEEN ABLE TO SINCE THE FIFTEENTH OF THIS MONTH, AND THAT HE WAS NOT SAYING THAT WAS A FINDING BECAUSE HE DID NOT THINK IT WAS. THE COUNT REPRODUCES. About four people said the thing to do about it is to say which figure in that book is current and about four others said in about four seconds and with a reason that saying which figure is current is putting a word beside a figure and there is no column for it and no fifth column of that shape is going to be ruled. **THE COST, IN A CLERK AND A HUNDRED AND SIXTY-NINE WORDS AND A HABIT: at about two, earlier than she has shut it on any day this month, she was asked why and she said that tomorrow is the twenty-ninth of this month and the day after that is the last day of it, and that she is not going to stand there and tell anybody how many days this month has, because nobody in this district has counted it and a month nobody has counted is a month nobody has checked, and she would be reading a number off a list and calling it a count, and the man of fifty-six has told her this week that he cannot get it off a board either, and she has believed him. The count reproduces. She entered that the length of this month is not a figure on her page either and that she is not going to enter one before she has counted it off a day she can name, and that she has written the sentence about figures somebody had to enter three times in four months and that the third time it is a habit and a habit is a finding and she is entering the finding about herself.** The man of fifty-six read the figures for the last time that day, in eighty-nine words, and said he is not going to be told the length of a month out of a list and will read it off that board if anybody counts it, and the count reproduces. The protagonist read the two lines out loud at about half past five, put the book back under the same stone at the same corner, and nobody read them back from the top, and a thing read back by nobody binds nobody. **THE BLOCK'S LAST IMAGE, AND IT IS NOT THE VOLUME'S: over the low wall at the south end of that yard, out of sight of everybody standing at that table, a ditch about three feet deep had standing water in the bottom of it, and no figure about how much was written anywhere in this district except on one line in one book in the open, and that line was thirteen days out of date, and nobody in that yard knew what was in the water and nobody had been asked and nobody was going to be.**
